# pg-ledger-mcp (Java 25 / Spring Boot rewrite): Test-Case Catalogue

Status: written BEFORE any implementation. Design authority: the approved plan (`luminous-baking-garden.md`) plus the later user decisions listed in section 1.4. No case in this file is a Node port: every old scenario is restated as the behaviour the user relies on, then expressed against the new design.

## 1. Purpose, conventions, levels

### 1.1 Purpose
1. Fix, before coding, what "done" means for each module (domain, application, schema, MCP server, admin web, CLI, hooks, e2e).
2. Carry over every behaviour that the old suite (`tests/e2e.mjs`, `tests/e2e.sh`, `tests/upgrade-atomicity.sh`, `tests/admin-ui.mjs`, `tests/claude-hook.mjs`, `tests/host-attestation.mjs`) protected, restated for the new design; nothing is silently omitted (section 18 reconciles every old ID).
3. Add the cases the plan and the later user decisions require (read tools, server-assigned ids, `list_changed`, receipts, `before_data`, UTC, driver registration, V-file schema runner, ArchUnit, ANSI check, secret store, legacy import).

### 1.2 IDs
`<AREA>-<NNN>`; service cases add a group: `SVC-<GROUP>-<NN>`. Areas: `DOM` domain, `REPO` repository, `ATT` author attestation (token format and keys), `SCH` schema runner, `DRV` JDBC driver registration, `SVC` application services, `MCW` MCP write-tool contract, `MCR` MCP read-tool contract, `MCC` MCP catalogue/transport/security, `ADM` admin web, `CLI` CLI, `SEC` secret store, `HK` hooks, `E2E` deployed, `ARC` ArchUnit, `ANSI` static check, `MIG` legacy import, `DOC` documentation (READMEs, release note format, release notes), `INS` installer (account separation, deployment files, tokens, `deploy/install.sh`), `FUP` clean-up before the release of 2.0.0 (usage text of one command, removal of the 1.x files, client READMEs), `BLD` Gradle build optimization (configuration cache, build cache, parallel, deprecations, the runtime class path check). IDs are stable; a retired case keeps its ID and is marked `dropped`.
Old IDs (Source column) are the labels used in section 18 (`e2e.mjs:C1`, `e2e.sh:S-U07`, `admin-ui:A4`, ...).

### 1.3 Levels
| Level | Meaning | Runner |
|---|---|---|
| UNIT | Domain records, enums, policies, codecs. No Spring, no DB. | JUnit 6 |
| REPO | Spring Data JDBC repositories and direct ANSI SQL against a real database. | JUnit 6, the local PostgreSQL (see 1.4a) |
| SERVICE | Application services in a Spring context (`@Transactional`, `WriteGate`, receipts, ids). | JUnit 6, the local PostgreSQL (see 1.4a) |
| MCP | MCP contract through the MCP Java SDK client against the Boot server (`/mcp`, Streamable HTTP). | JUnit 6 |
| ADMIN | Admin web (MockMvc/HTTP, Spring Security chain). | JUnit 6 |
| CLI | `ledger-cli` commands in-process with captured streams. | JUnit 6 |
| HOOK | The one executable jar `ledger-client-hook` run as `java -jar ledger-client-hook.jar <claude\|codex\|antigravity>` with synthetic host JSON. | JUnit 6 (spawns the JDK launcher only) |
| E2E | Deployed server(s) on the test host, MCP Java SDK client and HTTP. | `ledger-e2e` |
| ARCH | ArchUnit rules and source/dependency scans. | JUnit 6 |
| ANSI | Static check of repository SQL strings and every `V{n}__*.sql` file. | JUnit 6 |
| MIGRATION | Legacy `ledger` schema import and V-file schema runner. | JUnit 6 |
| DOCS | The READMEs, `docs/RELEASE_NOTES_FORMAT.md` and `docs/release-notes/*.md` read as text and checked against the code (tools, commands, modules, keys). | JUnit 6 (module `ledger-cli`, package `org.pgledger.cli.docs`) |
| INSTALL | The installers: the grant template on an account of the test PostgreSQL (module `ledger-database`), the rendering of the deployment files for the three targets, the token generator and the static checks of `deploy/install.sh` and `deploy/install.cmd` (module `ledger-cli`, packages `org.pgledger.cli.deploy`, `org.pgledger.cli.token` and, for the scripts, `org.pgledger.cli.docs`, which reads the files of the repository). The scripts themselves are not run (they target Linux as root and Windows as an administrator); they run on the test server and on a Windows host in a later stage (INS-031, INS-038). | JUnit 6 |
| SYSTEM | The packaged product against the real DBMS along the install path: the built jars (`ledger-cli`, `ledger-mcp-server`, the hook) started as separate Java processes and the MCP server reached by the MCP Java SDK client (section 21, module `ledger-system-test`). | JUnit 6 (test code only, needs the built jars and the local PostgreSQL) |
| BUILD | The Gradle build itself (`gradle.properties`, the build scripts, the cache behaviour of the tasks; section 30). No JUnit test can observe it, because it is the build that runs the tests: the case is a Gradle command and the lines it must print. | Gradle commands run by hand on the build machine; the observed lines are recorded in the report of the unit |

### 1.4 DBMS matrix column (changed by user decision)
The module tests run on one real DBMS: the **PostgreSQL** installed on the test PC (decision of 2026-11-12, section 1.4a). H2 is not a test database. The column `DBMS` of the case tables is historical: `yes` and `no` both mean that the case runs once, on that PostgreSQL; no case runs a second time on another DBMS. There is no list of "supported DBMS" anywhere: documentation states only that a JDBC driver can be attached, that SQL is ANSI only and that no DBMS-specific feature is used (case ARC-031 checks the docs). MariaDB/MySQL/Oracle/SQL Server are out of scope and every old or planned case that depended on them was removed.

Later user decisions that this catalogue applies (they supersede the plan where they differ):
1. **No Liquibase.** Schema = ANSI DDL files `V{n}__*.sql` applied by `ResourceDatabasePopulator` with our own history table `schema_history(version, attempt, script, digest, applied_at, outcome SUCCEEDED/FAILED)`, the form that the three products share (section 41, RUN). No IDENTITY, no sequences: every surrogate key (history, fix, case, grant) is allocated from counter rows by `IdAllocator`.
2. **No JDBC driver is bundled.** A driver jar path + driver class are registered through admin web or the CLI command `driver-register`, stored with the jar SHA-256 in `/etc/<name>/jdbc-driver.yaml` (mode 0640, readable by the service user, read through `spring.config.import`), applied after restart, loaded `URLClassLoader` -> `Driver` -> `SimpleDriverDataSource` -> Hikari. `DriverManager` is never used.
3. **`history.before_data`:** every operation that modifies an existing row stores the full previous row (JSON) in the same transaction; CREATE has none; failed requests leave no history; `ledger_history` returns it only with `include_before=true`; imported rows keep it empty.
4. **UTC everywhere.** All time columns hold UTC; MCP and admin web emit ISO-8601 with `Z`; the admin HTML carries `<time datetime="...Z">` and the browser converts for display; the server never converts zones.
5. **No boolean columns** (enums instead: `common_code.state` ACTIVE/INACTIVE, `defect_fix.origin` NATIVE/IMPORTED). JSON booleans that are part of the external tool contract (`remove`, `mark_source_fixed`, `removed`, `truncated`, `author_format_ok`, `can_verify_login`) stay; they are mapped at the boundary.
6. **Empty strings** are normalised to NULL or rejected in Java, per field (rule table in SVC-NORM).

7. **Second-round decisions of the main agent** (applied in this version; they resolve the former "unclassified" list): (a) deploy script keeps nginx reverse-proxy config and optional cloudflared ingress, admin native HTTPS through a Spring Boot SSL bundle; (b) `one-click-install.sh` stays a thin OS wrapper (OS prepare, `ledger-cli migrate`, `secret-set`), the argv secret option `--google-client-secret` is removed; (c) `install.sh` on PostgreSQL takes a DB dump before `migrate`; (d) admin overview MCP health is a Spring `RestClient` GET of the configured MCP `/healthz` URL; (e) CSRF is the Spring Security token plus a `SameSite=Strict` session cookie, the old `Sec-Fetch-Site`/`Origin` checks are dropped; (f) a FAILED schema file is retried by the next `migrate`, every attempt appends a `schema_history` row, the latest outcome of each version must be SUCCESS before the next version; (g) without a registered driver the MCP server and every CLI command except `driver-register` fail fast, the admin starts in setup mode (login + driver registration only); (h) `ledger_search` match is `ANY` (default) or `ALL`; (i) imported receipts are flagged LEGACY and a replay with such a request id is refused with `REQUEST_ID_LEGACY`; (j) legacy `''` in NOT NULL columns: the import reports the rows and stops (revised by REH3: the five long-text columns that 1.x allowed to be `''` are nullable in 2.0 and the import stores `''` as NULL, see section 27; a NOT NULL column that identifies a row still stops the import); (k) `category-add` dropped (`code-set` covers it); (l) three questionable behaviours stay as they are ("unchanged by design"); (m) plan fixes: grant check `revoked_at IS NULL AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > ?)`, case-versus-merge serialisation by `UPDATE defect SET case_seq = case_seq + 1 WHERE id = ? AND status <> 'MERGED'`, entity ids accepted only by an upper-case regex (case sensitivity then does not depend on the DBMS; revised by REH3: the ids of 1.x are kept, and the case does not depend on the DBMS because ids that differ only in case are refused and every lookup compares exactly, see section 27), hooks are one executable jar module `ledger-client-hook` (Jackson inside, first argument = client enum), not single-file sources.

Marker `(P)` in a Then cell = provisional contract detail (key name, token name or semantic choice not fixed by the plan). The semantic assertion is mandatory; the literal may be renamed once the contract is fixed, in one edit of the constants the tests reference.

### 1.4a Test database: the local PostgreSQL (user decision 2026-11-12; replaces the decision of 2026-11-11 "H2 only")
The user decided on 2026-11-12: 「dbms 설치가 전제라니까?」, 「h2는 초기 셋팅값이나 뭐 이것저것 담아두는 용도로만 쓰고 dbms에 마이그레이션하는 목적으로만 써.」 and 「h2는 그런목적으로만 쓰라고. 애초에 h2가 불안정한 dbms인건 찾아봤어?」. Consequences for the module tests:

1. **H2 is not a test database.** No repository, service, MCP, admin or CLI test runs on H2, and no test module has the H2 driver on its class path. H2 has two later roles only, both outside these tests: the store of the initial settings and the reference of the migration to a DBMS (a later unit of the task, in `ledger-cli`).
2. **Every module test that needs a database runs on the PostgreSQL 18 installed on the test PC** (`127.0.0.1:55432`, database `ledger_test`, login `ledger_test`, the owner of that database, with no superuser, `CREATEDB` or `CREATEROLE` right). The tests are configured with the Gradle properties `ledger.test.jdbc-url`, `ledger.test.jdbc-user`, `ledger.test.app-user`, `ledger.test.jdbc-password-file` and `ledger.test.app-password-file` (section 1.4b). A test that cannot reach the database fails with a connection error; it is never skipped and never falls back to another database.
3. **Every test class that uses the database works in a schema of its own** (`t<yyyyMMddHHmmss>_<8 hex>`, created by the test support, selected through the product setting `ledger.schema.name` or `Connection.setSchema`) and drops it at the end. At the start of a test JVM the test support drops the schemas of that name pattern that are older than one hour (the leftovers of an interrupted run). Nothing is left behind except in the test database.
4. **The application account** (`ledger.test.app-user`, the least-privilege login of the application) is used by the account cases (REPO-023, REPO-024, REPO-038, SCH-014, INS-010, INS-011 and the `GrantTemplateTest` and `DmlAccountTest` cases). The owner account cannot create roles, so those tests grant the account the rights of the product's grant template inside their own schema (and `USAGE` on that schema, a step of the DBMS) and revoke them with the drop of the schema. If the application account cannot log in, those tests fail with a message that names the property; they are never skipped and never run as the owner.
5. **The PostgreSQL part of the cases that had moved to the test server stage is a module test again**, because the PostgreSQL is available on the test PC. H2 is no longer part of any case.

| ID | Status now | Run by the module tests |
|---|---|---|
| SCH-004 | PostgreSQL part back in the module tests | transactional DDL: V3 whose 3rd statement is invalid leaves no partial V3 objects |
| DRV-006, DRV-007, DRV-011 | PostgreSQL part back in the module tests | the chain and the smoke run with the PostgreSQL driver jar (the jar is the test-only `org.postgresql:postgresql` artifact, its path resolved by Gradle and passed as the system property `ledger.test.postgres-jar`) |
| REPO-023, REPO-024, REPO-038, SCH-014 | PostgreSQL part back in the module tests | refusal for the least-privilege account with SQLState 42501 and the set-up of that account in the schema of the test |
| SVC-TZ-01, SVC-TZ-02, REPO-029, SCH-019 | PostgreSQL part back in the module tests | the session time zone of a PostgreSQL session (`SET TIME ZONE`, ANSI) |
| SCH-001 .. SCH-011, SCH-015, SCH-020, SCH-021, REPO-001 .. REPO-038 (the rest) | the only run is on PostgreSQL | the former H2 run is gone |
| MIG-016, MIG-027, MIG-008, MIG-003, MIG-004 (import; run in `ledger-migrate-1x`, MIG-004 replaced by M1X-003) | PostgreSQL part back in the module tests | `timestamptz` read as `OffsetDateTime` with the legacy session zone set to UTC, `Asia/Seoul` and `America/New_York`; the legacy schema `ledger` and the new schema in one database (the fixture builds the old schema in a schema of its own of the same database) |
| REPO-037 | stays a test server stage case | prepare every captured SQL string on a second DBMS; needs two databases (module test removed) |
| SCH-013 | stays a test server stage case | compare the logical schema of two DBMS (module test removed) |
| E2E-014, SCH-013, REPO-037 | dropped or test server stage | E2E-014 is dropped (H2 is not a product database, 2026-11-12); SCH-013 and REPO-037 need two DBMS and stay test server stage cases; not run by the module tests |
| TDB-001 | new, run | the tests really run on PostgreSQL: `DatabaseMetaData.getDatabaseProductName()` is `PostgreSQL`, the major version is at least 18, the database is `ledger_test` and the session time zone is UTC |
| TDB-002 | new, run | a schema of the test support exists while its test runs and is gone after the drop; the leftover sweep drops a schema of the name pattern older than one hour and keeps a younger one |
| TDB-003 | new, run | the credentials: with `ledger.test.jdbc-password-file` set to a temporary file the test helper uses the first line of that file as the password of the data source (a trailing line break removed); with the property unset no password is set, so the PostgreSQL driver reads its own pass file; a missing or unreadable file fails with a message that names the property and the path and never the content. No connection is opened by this case |
| TDB-004 | new, run | the application account: when `ledger.test.app-user` cannot log in, the test that needs it fails with a message that names the property and the account (it does not skip and does not use the owner account) |

The case `TestDatabaseTest` of the MCP server module (1.4a: "the test database is the embedded H2 in its default mode") is replaced by TDB-001; the check of the `MODE` setting of H2 is removed.

Test support of the application module (SERVICE level, §6 and §8): the service cases run in a Spring Boot test context (`@SpringBootTest` through the test annotation `LedgerServiceTest`) on the PostgreSQL of the test PC: a data source of the test support (one small connection pool per test JVM, shared by all cached contexts) whose connections use the schema that the context took from the test support (`ledger.schema.name`); the schema is built by the real `V{n}__*.sql` files and the real migrator before the first test, and Spring's test context cache shares the one context between the test classes. No transaction wraps a test method (the test classes are not `@Transactional`, and the parameter resolver refuses a test that would run in one), so the race cases (SVC-RACE, SVC-RCP-09..11, SVC-GATE-18, SVC-GRT-06, SVC-BEF-08/09) run real threads that commit. The variants that need another context (database session in another time zone: SVC-TZ-02; a fresh installation without categories: SVC-CC-10; a restart of the application on the same database: SVC-RCP-13) are combinations of configuration and properties of `ApplicationContextRunner`. The long text type of the schema files is the product default `TEXT`.

### 1.4b Test database settings
| Gradle property (`-P` or `gradle.properties`) | System property of the test JVM | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `ledger.test.jdbc-url` | the same name | `jdbc:postgresql://127.0.0.1:55432/ledger_test` |
| `ledger.test.jdbc-user` | the same name | `ledger_test` |
| `ledger.test.app-user` | the same name | `ledger_test_app` |
| `ledger.test.jdbc-password-file` | the same name; carries the path only | none in the repository (omitted falls back to `pgpass`) |
| `ledger.test.app-password-file` | the same name; carries the path only | none in the repository (omitted falls back to `pgpass`) |
| (resolved by Gradle) | `ledger.test.postgres-jar` | the path of the PostgreSQL JDBC driver jar of the test-only configuration |

A password is never a property value, never in code, logs, test names or messages. When a password file property is set, the test support reads the file (one line, UTF-8) and sets its content as the password of the data source; when it is unset it sets no password and the PostgreSQL driver falls back to the user's own pass file. At most 10 connections are open at the same time from one test JVM.

### 1.5 Error-token registry
Tokens are the external contract (MCP tool error `code`, admin `ProblemDetail.code`). MCP tool errors return `isError=true`, `structuredContent{code,message,details}`, and the text content starts with `<CODE>: <message>` so that text-matching clients keep working.
| Token | Origin | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED` | carried (sql `_begin`, `whoami`) | `_hostAttestation` absent/null |
| `AUTHOR_ID_FORMAT` | carried | bad `<vendor>:<session>` / `user:owner` format, also `owner_actor_id` |
| `SESSION_NOT_GRANTED` | carried | no live grant for author+project+scope |
| `VERIFY_NEEDS_REVIEWER_LOGIN` | carried | verify-scope operation through the AUTHOR channel |
| `CODE_REQUIRED` / `CODE_INVALID` | carried (trigger `_check_codes`) | common-code columns; details list valid codes |
| `SAME_SESSION` | carried (changed by verifyrules, section 45) | verifier session equals the session that registered the defect, recorded a case or a fix of it; for a task: the session that registered it, owns it or reported it done (at any time); for a condition: the session that registered it; the cases and the registrants of the defects merged into a defect count as its own |
| `MERGED_DEFECT` | carried | case on a merged defect; message names the merge target |
| `VERIFIED_DEFECT` (P) | new (verifyrules, D-0023) | `record_fix` or `save` of a defect whose status is `VERIFIED`; the message names the defect and says that a reviewer has to reopen it first |
| `ID_COUNTER_CHANGED` (P) | new (verifyrules review, D-0022) | the project counter changed between the statement that takes an explicit number and the read that follows it (another request took the number); the message names the project and says to send the request again |
| `ID_NUMBER_AHEAD` (P) | new (verifyrules, D-0022) | an explicit defect or task id whose number is above the next number of the project (the project counter plus one); the message names the id and the next id |
| `VERSION_CONFLICT` | plan | expected version differs; message has expected and current |
| `AGENT_TOKEN_FORBIDDEN`, `UNAUTHORIZED`, `DNS_REBINDING_BLOCKED`, `TOO_MANY_AUTH_FAILURES`, `COMMON_CODE_DELETE_FORBIDDEN`, `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` | carried (admin server) | admin web |
| `VALIDATION_FAILED` (P) | new (replaces zod text, DB CHECK text, `INVALID_*` admin tokens) | structure/format/enum/unknown argument; `details[]{path,reason,allowed?}` |
| `REASON_INVALID` (P) | new (sql `_check_reason`) | reason not 10..1000 chars after trim |
| `OPERATION_CONFLICT` (P) | new (sql "already bound") | same author with the same operation key, different operation/payload/channel (changed by opkey: was "request id"; the code `REQUEST_CONFLICT` is renamed `OPERATION_CONFLICT`, like `ARTIFACT_OPERATION_CONFLICT`; changed by opkey round 2: receipts are unique per (author, operation key), so another author with the same key is no conflict, it writes its own receipt, OPK-020; a batch key bound to other operations or to another tool is a conflict, OPK-019) |
| `NOT_FOUND` (P) | new ("not found") | unknown id, project, history id, merge source |
| `DUPLICATE_KEY` (P) | new (raw PK/UNIQUE violation) | explicit id exists, condition (project,release,code) exists |
| `ID_PREFIX_MISMATCH` (P) | new ("must start with") | defect id not `<CODE>-D-` |
| `PROJECT_MOVE_FORBIDDEN` (P) | new ("cannot move") | defect project change |
| `NOTHING_CHANGES` (P) | new ("nothing changes", "nothing changed") | no-op save, no-op fix update, duplicate link, remove of absent link |
| `TRANSITION_INVALID` (P) | new | same state, forbidden source state, task to UNKNOWN |
| `FIX_REQUIRED` (P) / `FIX_ALREADY_RECORDED` (P) | new | SOURCE_FIXED/VERIFIED without fix; fix re-recorded without CI data |
| `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED` (P) | new | service-level guard when evidence is missing |
| `MERGE_TARGET_INVALID` (P) | new | missing, self or already merged target |
| `CSRF_REJECTED` (P) | new | admin CSRF failure |
| `FORBIDDEN` (P) | new, HTTP layer of the admin web only (no `LedgerException`, no domain enum constant) | an OpenID Connect sign-in that fails: wrong audience, issuer, nonce, state, expired token, or an e-mail that is not the owner's (403) |
| `DRIVER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `DRIVER_INVALID` (P) | new | driver registration |
| `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `INTEGRITY_VIOLATION` (P) | new | unexpected failure, DB integrity error; never contains SQL or vendor text |
| `SCHEMA_FILE_NAME_INVALID`, `SCHEMA_VERSION_DUPLICATE`, `SCHEMA_VERSION_GAP`, `SCHEMA_FILES_UNREADABLE`, `SCHEMA_DATABASE_UNREACHABLE`, `SCHEMA_HISTORY_MISSING`, `SCHEMA_HISTORY_NOT_CREATED`, `SCHEMA_APPLIED_FILE_MISSING`, `SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, `SCHEMA_RECORDED_FAILED`, `SCHEMA_OUT_OF_ORDER`, `SCHEMA_PENDING`, `SCHEMA_MIGRATION_FAILED`, `SCHEMA_MIGRATION_INTERRUPTED`, `SCHEMA_GRANT_FAILED`, `JVM_ZONE_NOT_UTC`, `SESSION_ZONE_NOT_UTC`, `SESSION_CLOCK_UNREADABLE` | new (AGS-3446 principles unit W1: the codes that replace `SchemaMigrationException`, which is thrown by the schema migrator, verifier, grant applier, file reader and UTC session check of `ledger-database`) | schema work failures; the arguments are the version, the file name, the digests (no SQL, no vendor text); thrown by the database module from the unit W2 on |
| `DATABASE_PRODUCT_UNREADABLE`, `DATABASE_PRODUCT_UNSUPPORTED`, `DATABASE_PRODUCT_MISMATCH` | new (unit sqlbuilder, SQB-001) | the product of the database cannot be read from the connection; the product is none that the ledger has SQL for ({0} = the product); the configured JDBC URL names another product than the connection reports ({0} = configured, {1} = connected); thrown by `SqlBuilder` and `DatabaseProductName`, no SQL and no vendor text beyond the product name |
| `ATTESTATION_UNSIGNED` | new (D-0012) | `_hostAttestation` is a bare `<vendor>:<session-id>` (the format of the old hook): nothing proves it |
| `ATTESTATION_MALFORMED` | new (D-0012; prefix changed to `pgla2.` by AGS-3446 attest-fix) | the value has the token prefix `pgla2.` but is not a canonical token |
| `ATTESTATION_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` | **removed by PCS (PCS-041; no release had the version 1 token)**; was: new (AGS-3446 attest-fix, F1/F3) | the value has the prefix `pgla1.` of the retired token format (no arguments digest): the hook must be updated; the old token is refused, never accepted |
| `SESSION_KEY_NOT_REGISTERED` | dropped (AGS-3446 attest-fix, F5) | was: the author has no live registered key. Answering it before the signature is proven told a caller with a well-formed token which authors have a key (oracle); the case is now `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, and the precise reason is logged on the server only |
| `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID` | new (D-0012; widened by AGS-3446 attest-fix, F5) | the attestation cannot be verified: the signature does not verify under the live key of the author (forged, another author's key, replaced key) OR the author has no live key on this server (never registered, revoked). One code, no argument, no author in the message: until the signature is proven the caller learns nothing about which authors have a key; the message tells how to register a key for this server |
| `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH` | new (D-0012; `arguments` added by AGS-3446 attest-fix, F1/F3) | the token was signed for another tool, another `operationKey` or other arguments than the call; `details.field` is `tool`, `operationKey` or `arguments` (changed by opkey: was `request_id`) |
| `ATTESTATION_TIME_WINDOW` | new (D-0012) | the issue time is more than `ledger.attestation.window` (default 5 minutes) from the server clock, in either direction; `details.window_seconds` |
| `KEY_REPLACEMENT_NOT_CONFIRMED` | new (audit F4) | the author already has another live public key and the owner did not confirm the replacement naming that key's fingerprint; args are the author, the live key's fingerprint and its registration time |
| `LOGIN_NOT_ALLOWED` | dropped | role logins no longer exist |

### 1.6 Old-behaviour legend
Old checks without an own label in the source are labelled in file order: `E-01..` (`e2e.mjs`), `S-<group><nn>` (`e2e.sh`: U upgrade, F fresh, O orchestration, R race, D database, C constraint, I install, W web/domain, X data). `A1..A12` are the 12 `admin-ui.mjs` checks in file order, `CH-01..` the `claude-hook.mjs` tests, `S01..S14` the `host-attestation.mjs` tests. Section 14 lists them all.

## 2. DOM: domain (UNIT, no DB)

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 2a. ATT: author attestation (token format, keys; module `ledger-attestation`, UNIT, JDK only)

Decision D-0012 (user: "1번으로 가자. 이번에 같이 고쳐."): the server no longer trusts `_hostAttestation = <vendor>:<host id>`. The hook signs every write and whoami call with the Ed25519 key of its author (JDK `Signature` "Ed25519", RFC 8032); the server verifies with the public key the owner registered.

**Token format (version 2; changed from version 1 by AGS-3446 attest-fix, F1/F3: the signature now also covers the arguments of the call).** `_hostAttestation` = `pgla2.<author>.<tool>.<operation key>.<arguments digest>.<issued at>.<signature>` (changed by opkey: the third part was the request id; the byte layout is unchanged). A `pgla1.` text is not of the format: `MALFORMED` like any other (changed by PCS, PCS-040; it was refused by name with `ATTESTATION_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED`).
- `author`, `tool`, `operation key`: UTF-8 bytes of the value in unpadded base64url (alphabet `A-Za-z0-9_-`); an empty value is an empty part, so the text always has exactly seven parts split at `.`.
- `arguments digest`: SHA-256 of the canonical form (below) of the arguments of the call without `_hostAttestation`, 64 lower-case hex characters.
- `issued at`: UTC epoch seconds in decimal, no sign, no leading zero (except `0`), at most 15 digits.
- `signature`: the 64 signature bytes in unpadded base64url.
- A text is accepted only in this canonical form (padding, other alphabets and non-canonical trailing bits are refused), at most 2048 characters. One token has one text.

**Signed bytes (version 2).** ASCII line `pg-ledger-mcp/attestation/v2` and `\n`; then for author, tool, operation key and arguments digest, in that order, `<UTF-8 byte length in decimal>:<value bytes>` and `\n`; then the epoch seconds in decimal and `\n`. Example: author `claude:s1`, tool `ledger_save`, operation key `r1`, arguments digest of `{}` (`44136fa355b3678a1146ad16f7e8649e94fb4fc21fe77e8310c060f61caaff8a`), time `1700000000` signs `pg-ledger-mcp/attestation/v2\n9:claude:s1\n11:ledger_save\n2:r1\n64:44136fa355b3678a1146ad16f7e8649e94fb4fc21fe77e8310c060f61caaff8a\n1700000000\n`. The length prefix makes the encoding unambiguous whatever characters a value holds; the first line keeps a signature of this protocol (or of version 1) from being valid for another one.

**Arguments digest (F1/F3).** One canonical form for the hook and the server, in module `ledger-attestation` (so the two cannot drift apart): the JSON Canonicalization Scheme, RFC 8785 (https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8785): object members sorted by the UTF-16 code units of the name, no whitespace, strings with the minimal escapes of RFC 8785 section 3.2.2.2, numbers as the ECMAScript `Number` text of the IEEE 754 double (RFC 8785 section 3.2.2.3), UTF-8 output. Input: the arguments of the call, an object, without its top-level member `_hostAttestation` (changed by opkey: the hook fills nothing, it signs the arguments as they were sent); the server computes it from the arguments it received (the map the MCP SDK hands the tool binding), before any normalisation. The receipt normalisation rules (DOM-028, SVC-NORM: key order, `""` vs absent) are deliberately NOT reused: they live in the domain and application modules, which the hook must not depend on, and the signature has to cover exactly what was sent (changed by opkey: the example "a token for the string form of `fields` does not fit the object form" is gone, the tool no longer accepts the string form). Key order and whitespace never matter (JCS), everything else does. (Changed by opkey round 2, M4: an object member whose value is null is left out of the digest, at every depth; the elements of an array are kept, null or not. Reason: the server side of the MCP SDK drops a null member of a map before the tool binding, so the server never sees it and the hook and the server must agree on the arguments without it, OPK-021.) For `ledger_batch` the digest covers every operation, its content and its order (changed by opkey: the operations carry no key, the key is the one of the batch); for `ledger_whoami` it is the digest of `{}`. (Changed by audit2 N2, RFC 7493 section 2.2: an integer whose magnitude is beyond 2**53-1 is refused, the hook does not sign it and the server does not verify it, because the double of two such integers is the same while the server binds each as its own `long`, ATT-037; a number with a fraction or an exponent is a decimal and keeps the text of its double, ATT-038.)

**Audience (F2).** The hook keeps one key per (MCP server name of the host, author) and the owner registers each server's key separately, so a signature made for one server never verifies on another (test server, look-alike server). The server name is the `<server>` of `mcp__<server>__...` (Claude Code, Codex) or the `ServerName` / `mcp:<server>...` of Antigravity; only `ledger_<op>` or `<server>_ledger_<op>` (the portal prefix equals the server name) with a known operation is signed. Codex Apps (`mcp__codex_apps__app_<app>_ledger_<op>`) share the pseudo server `codex_apps`, so the audience there is the app: the server identity is `codex_apps.<app>`.

**Operation key.** The `operationKey` argument of the call (exact text, no trimming or case change); `ledger_whoami` and the artifact tools have none and sign the empty string; an absent key and an empty key are the same on the server only for the claim, the call itself is refused (`VALIDATION_FAILED`, path `operationKey`); the hook never makes a key up. For `ledger_batch` the key is the `operationKey` of the batch. (Changed by opkey: was "**Request id.** The `request_id` argument ...; the hook fills a missing `request_id` with a random UUID before it signs".)

**Keys.** One Ed25519 key pair per (server, author), made by the hook (`java -jar ledger-client-hook.jar session-key <server> <author>` prints `server`, `author`, `public-key` (X.509 SubjectPublicKeyInfo DER, standard base64) and `fingerprint` (SHA-256 of the DER, 64 lower-case hex)). The private key lives only in the hook's key folder (file `<sha256 of server and author>.key`, owner-only: folder `rwx------` and file `rw-------` where the POSIX attribute view exists, an ACL that grants the owner alone where the ACL attribute view exists (Windows); a key folder or file with any other grant, another owner, or that is a link is refused). `LEDGER_HOOK_KEY_DIR` must be an absolute path. The owner registers the public key for the author (CLI `key-register`, `SessionKeyService`), after comparing the fingerprint the session showed. A key is replaced or revoked by closing its `session_key` row; rows are never deleted. No MCP tool registers keys or grants.

**What it stops, what stays (reported to the user).** It stops a holder of the bearer token who calls `/mcp` without the hook, copies a session id from `ledger_history`, or forges from another machine. It does not stop another process of the same OS user, which can read the private key file or run the hook with a forged event: isolation inside one user needs separate OS accounts. A captured token cannot be used for another operation key, another tool, other arguments (any change of any argument, an added operation of a batch included) or another time; the same signed call sent again is the same request (receipt idempotency). A token is not a one-time token: until the window ends the identical call can be replayed and returns the stored response (or, if the first call failed, is executed once).

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ATT-001 | UNIT | claims signed with a key pair | `text()` and `parse()` | (changed by attest-fix: version 2) seven parts, prefix `pgla2`, the arguments digest part is 64 lower-case hex, parts decode to the claims, 64 signature bytes; parse gives the same claims and the same text; the signature verifies | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-002 | UNIT | a token and the key of the signer, and another key | `verify` | verifies with the signer's key only; the other key is `SIGNATURE_INVALID` | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-003 | UNIT | a token whose author, tool, operation key (also made empty), arguments digest or time was replaced after signing | `parse` then `verify` | `SIGNATURE_INVALID` for each | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-004 | UNIT | the example claims | `signedBytes` | exactly the bytes above (changed by attest-fix: the version 2 example with the arguments digest) | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-005 | UNIT | claims that differ by moving characters between fields (the arguments digest included), newline and `<n>:` inside values, time `10` vs `1` | `signedBytes` | pairwise different | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-006 | UNIT | empty author, empty operation key, Korean values | round trip | same claims, verifies | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-007 | UNIT | (changed by attest-fix: version 2) null, empty, `claude:abc`, wrong prefix or case, 6 or 8 parts, an arguments digest that is not 64 lower-case hex (upper case, 63 or 65 characters, non-hex), bad base64url, padding, non-UTF-8 author, negative / leading-zero / exponent / empty / 16-digit time, signature of 63 or 66 bytes or empty, text over 2048 characters | `parse` | `MALFORMED` | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-008 | UNIT | a token | `toString` | claims only, never the signature or the text | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-010 | UNIT | an Ed25519 public key | `encode`, `decode` | 44 DER bytes in base64; reads back equal; surrounding whitespace ignored; `canonical` is stable | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-011 | UNIT | a public key | `fingerprint` | SHA-256 of the DER, 64 lower-case hex; another key differs | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-012 | UNIT | empty, not base64, DER with a trailing byte or truncated, a private key, an X25519 key, an RSA key, null | `decode` | `KEY_INVALID` | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-020 | ARCH | module `ledger-attestation` | scan | JDK only (no network, file, process, reflection, JDBC, Spring, Jackson, other product module, `System`); no mutable static state; ARC-033, ARC-036, ARC-037 hold | D-0012 | no |
| ATT-030 | UNIT | the sample of RFC 8785 section 3.2.2 (`numbers`, `string` with `€$\u000F\u000aA'B"\\\\"/`, `literals`) as a Java map | canonicalise | exactly the RFC output `{"literals":[null,true,false],"numbers":[333333333.3333333,1e+30,4.5,0.002,1e-27],"string":"€$\u000f\nA'B\"\\\\\"/"}` (F1/F3) | RFC 8785 | no |
| ATT-031 | UNIT | numbers 0, -0, 1, 1.0, 4.50, 2e-3, -1, 123456789012345680000, 1e21, 1e-6, 1e-7, 5e-324, 1.7976931348623157e308, 9007199254740992, 9007199254740993 as `Integer`, `Long`, `Double`, `BigInteger`, `BigDecimal` | canonicalise | the ECMAScript text of the double (`0`, `0`, `1`, `1`, `4.5`, `0.002`, `-1`, `123456789012345680000`, `1e+21`, `0.000001`, `1e-7`, `5e-324`, `1.7976931348623157e+308`, `9007199254740992`, `9007199254740992`), the same for every Java number type | (changed by audit2 N2: the integers `9007199254740992` and `9007199254740993` as `Integer`/`Long`/`BigInteger` are no longer canonicalised by their double, they are refused, ATT-037; the row now has the `Double` `9007199254740992` (a decimal, which keeps the text of its double, ATT-038) and the largest exact integers `9007199254740991` and `-9007199254740991`, which keep their own text) RFC 8785 3.2.2.3 | no |
| ATT-032 | UNIT | strings with `"`, `\`, `/`, the control characters U+0000..U+001F (`\b \t \n \f \r` short, others `\u00xx` lower-case), U+007F, Korean, an emoji (surrogate pair); member names `😀`, `דּ`, `a`, `B`, `` | canonicalise | the minimal escapes of RFC 8785; everything else literal; members sorted by UTF-16 code units (the emoji sorts before U+FB33, unlike UTF-8 order); the empty name first | RFC 8785 3.2.3 | no |
| ATT-033 | UNIT | arguments maps | `ArgumentsDigest.of` | 64 lower-case hex; equal for another key order and another spelling of the same number; `_hostAttestation` at the top level is ignored (present, absent, any value), the same name nested is part of the digest; different for a changed value, a changed or moved array element, an added or removed array element, `""` vs absent (changed by opkey round 2: an object member that is JSON null is left out, so it has the digest of the absent member, at every depth, while a null array element is kept and changes the digest; OPK-021), `"1"` vs `1`, an object vs its JSON text; the digest of `{}` is `44136fa355b3678a1146ad16f7e8649e94fb4fc21fe77e8310c060f61caaff8a` | F1/F3 | no |
| ATT-034 | UNIT | NaN, infinity, a double that overflows (`1e999` as `BigDecimal`), a lone surrogate in a string or a name, a non-`String` key, a value of another type (`Object`, `Date`), a null map | `ArgumentsDigest.of` | `IllegalArgumentException` (a programming error of the caller, never a digest of something else) | F1/F3 | no |
| ATT-035 | UNIT | claims with an arguments digest | sign, `text()`, `parse()`, `verify` | round trip equal; replacing the digest part of the text by the digest of other arguments is `SIGNATURE_INVALID` | F1/F3 | no |
| ATT-036 | UNIT | **removed by PCS (PCS-040): no release had the version 1 token, so it is not refused by name.** a text with the prefix `pgla1.` (a well-formed version 1 token, the old six parts) | `parse` | `VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` (not `MALFORMED`, never accepted) | F1/F3 | no |
| ATT-037 | UNIT | (audit2 N2, RFC 7493 section 2.2 https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7493#section-2.2, RFC 8785 sections 3.1 and 3.2.2.3) integers `9007199254740992`, `9007199254740993`, `-9007199254740992`, `Long.MAX_VALUE`, `Long.MIN_VALUE`, `BigInteger` `9007199254740992`, `-9007199254740993`, `12345678901234567890`, 10**40; and the largest exact integers `9007199254740991`, `-9007199254740991` | canonicalise alone and nested in a list / map, `ArgumentsDigest.of` | every integer beyond 2**53-1 in magnitude is an `IllegalArgumentException` (it has no exact canonical form: the double of `9007199254740993` is `9007199254740992`, so two integers would share one digest while the server binds each as its own `long`); the hook does not sign it and the server (same shared class) does not verify it; `9007199254740991` and `-9007199254740991` keep their text; the digests of `9007199254740990` and `9007199254740991` differ | audit2 N2 | no |
| ATT-038 | UNIT | (audit2 N2) decimals: `BigDecimal` `0.10`, `1e300`, `9007199254740993` (a `BigDecimal` is a decimal, not an integer type), `Float` `1.5f`; another `Number` type (`AtomicLong`) | canonicalise, `ArgumentsDigest.of` | decimals are canonicalised by their double as RFC 8785 says (`0.10` is `0.1` and has the digest of `0.1`; `1e300` is `1e+300`; `9007199254740993` as a decimal is `9007199254740992`): that is not ambiguous because the server also reads a number with a fraction or an exponent as a double; a `Number` type that is no JSON number type is an `IllegalArgumentException` | audit2 N2 | no |
| ATT-040 | UNIT | (audit2 S1) the four lines of the hook command `session-key` (`server: <server>`, `author: <author>`, `public-key: <key>`, `fingerprint: <fingerprint>`); the lines in another order, with blank lines, CRLF, white space around a line and an upper-case fingerprint; the bare key | `KeyBlock.isBlock`, `KeyBlock.parse` | the block is read into server, author, canonical key and lower-case fingerprint; `isBlock` is true when a line starts with `public-key:` and false for the bare key and null | audit2 S1 | no |
| ATT-041 | UNIT | (audit2 S1) null, empty, the bare key, a block without a line, with a line twice, with an unknown line, a line without a colon, an empty value, an empty server, a server with a space, an escape character or 101 characters, a line name in upper case; a block whose fingerprint line is another key (not its own); a block whose key is `AAAA` | `KeyBlock.parse` | `KEY_BLOCK_INVALID` for the shape problems, `KEY_BLOCK_FINGERPRINT` for the fingerprint line that does not belong to the key, `KEY_INVALID` for a key that is no Ed25519 key | audit2 S1 | no |
| ATT-050 | UNIT | claims for `ledger_save` / `r1` / digest D; a call that differs in the tool, in the operation key, in the arguments, in all three, with a null tool, a null operation key, a null digest; claims with the empty operation key (whoami, artifact tools) | `AttestationClaims.firstMismatch` | none when the call is what was signed; otherwise the first of `TOOL`, `OPERATION_KEY`, `ARGUMENTS` in that order (changed by opkey: was `REQUEST_ID`); a null operation key is the empty one; a null tool or digest never matches | plan ags-3446 W1 (the verifier of the application used to compare the three fields itself) | no |
| ATT-051 | UNIT | the three `AttestationClaims.Field` constants | `token()` | `tool`, `operationKey`, `arguments`: the values of `details.field` of `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH` (changed by opkey: the second constant is `OPERATION_KEY`, was `REQUEST_ID` / `request_id`; OPK-015) | plan ags-3446 W1 | no |
| ATT-052 | UNIT | SHA-256 of `abc` and of nothing (FIPS 180-4 vectors), of `{}`, of the DER bytes of a key | `Sha256Hex.of`, `ArgumentsDigest.of`, `PublicKeys.fingerprint` | known vectors; the arguments digest and the fingerprint are the one `Sha256Hex` (the module had two copies of SHA-256 + hex) | plan ags-3446 W1 | no |

## 3. REPO: repositories and direct ANSI SQL (PostgreSQL)

All REPO cases run against a schema built by the real `V{n}__*.sql` files (never by hand-made DDL) and the least-privilege DML account unless a case says "owner account".

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REPO-001 | REPO | schema from V files | list tables, columns, PK, UNIQUE, FK through JDBC metadata | tables exactly (`defect` carries the `case_seq` counter column, **changed by M1X (section 43)**: `request_receipt` has no `origin` column and no `NATIVE`/`LEGACY` value, and the check of the history column is `source_ref`): project, task, defect, defect_fix, defect_case, defect_task, defect_relation, condition (name may be adjusted by AGS rule), condition_link, session_grant, session_key (public key of an author; decision of D-0012), history, request_receipt, common_code, code_revision, id_counter, schema_history; none of: common_use table (replaced by `@CommonCode`), `_migration`, views, triggers | 10_tables, 15, 16 | yes |
| REPO-002 | REPO | same | scan column types | no BOOLEAN/BIT column anywhere; `common_code.state` and `defect_fix.origin` are enum-valued text with an IN-list CHECK | user decision 5; S-C01 | yes |
| REPO-003 | REPO | same | insert a value outside each fixed set: defect.status, task.state, condition.state, defect_fix.ci_conclusion, session_grant.grant_scope, common_code.state; **changed by M1X (section 43)**: the receipt case is an `operation` of `request_receipt` outside the set (the table has no `origin` column) | rejected with SQLState class 23 (CHECK retained as in old `S-C01`) | e2e.sh:S-C01 | yes |
| REPO-004 | REPO | same | inspect CHECK expressions | only IN-lists, comparisons, NULL-ness, `(a IS NULL) = (b IS NOT NULL)` style booleans of columns; no regex, no function call (length rules live in Java) | plan: ANSI only | yes |
| REPO-005 | REPO | `created_by` on project, task, defect, condition, defect_task, defect_relation, condition_link, common_code | read metadata | all eight NOT NULL | e2e.sh:S-C08 | yes |
| REPO-006 | REPO | repository save with `AuditorAware` bound to actor X via `ScopedValue` | insert, then update by actor Y | `created_by`=X after both; `@ReadOnlyProperty` keeps it out of UPDATE statements (SQL spy: no `created_by` in UPDATE) | e2e.sh:S-C04; P1,P5 | yes |
| REPO-007 | REPO | no actor bound | insert | fails loudly (no silent default author) | plan: `AuditorAware` | no |
| REPO-008 | REPO | aggregate with `@Version` | update twice from two stale copies | second fails with optimistic-locking failure; version increments by exactly 1 per update | plan: `@Version`, `VERSION_CONFLICT` | yes |
| REPO-009 | REPO | project counter column | `UPDATE project SET next_defect_no = next_defect_no + 1 WHERE code=?` then read in the same tx | returns previous+1; unknown code updates 0 rows (**changed by verifyrules, section 45**: the explicit number is taken by `takeDefectNo` and `takeTaskNo` in place of the former raise, VRF-020 and VRF-021; the test asserts the number that the next allocation returns after a taken number) | plan: id allocation | yes |
| REPO-010 | REPO | 32 threads, 20 allocations each, one project | allocate defect numbers concurrently | 640 distinct numbers, set equals 1..640 (no gaps, no duplicates) | plan: concurrent ids | yes |
| REPO-011 | REPO | allocation then `rollback` | allocate again | the rolled-back number is reused; committed numbers stay distinct | plan: counter in same tx | yes |
| REPO-012 | REPO | task counter, two projects | parallel allocation | independent per project; task and defect counters independent | plan: task ids | yes |
| REPO-013 | REPO | `id_counter` rows for history, defect_fix, defect_case, session_grant, session_key (no IDENTITY, no sequence) | 64 concurrent writers each inserting one row of each kind | ids distinct per table, no gaps after rollbacks, `ORDER BY id` equals insertion order inside one transaction | user decision 1 | yes |
| REPO-014 | REPO | mixed workload: random writes touching project counter, history counter and rows in varying program order | 100 parallel operations | no deadlock exception surfaces (lock order project counter -> row -> history counter is fixed); all complete | plan: counters | yes |
| REPO-015 | REPO | conditional grant check `UPDATE session_grant SET last_used_at=? WHERE id=? AND revoked_at IS NULL AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > ?)` | live, revoked, expired, expiry exactly equal to "now", never-expiring (NULL), other author, other scope, other project | 1 affected row only for live and never-expiring; 0 for the rest; `last_used_at` set to the injected clock. The plan SQL is fixed (decision 7m) to `revoked_at IS NULL AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > ?)`; NULL = no expiry passes (old `grant` had optional `--expires`) | sql `_begin`; plan: grant check | yes |
| REPO-016 | REPO | grant row locked by tx A's conditional UPDATE | tx B revokes the grant (UPDATE revoked_at) | B waits until A commits (A's write commits before the revocation); after B commits a new check affects 0 rows; reverse order: A sees 0 rows | plan: grant vs revoke; sql FOR SHARE comment | yes |
| REPO-017 | REPO | receipt table with PK (author, operation key) (changed by opkey: column `request_receipt.operation_key`, the table name is kept; changed by opkey round 2: the primary key `pk_request_receipt` is on `(author_id, operation_key)`, like `upload_staging` UNIQUE(owner, operation_key), OPK-020) | two inserts of the same pair (the same key with another author is accepted) | second raises a duplicate-key error mapped to `DuplicateKeyException` (SQLState 23) | plan: receipts | yes |
| REPO-018 | REPO | tx A inserted receipt R, not committed | tx B inserts R | B blocks; A commits -> B gets duplicate-key; A rolls back -> B succeeds | plan: duplicates serialise on key wait | yes |
| REPO-019 | REPO | JSON-as-text columns (`history.changes`, `history.before_data`, `request_receipt.response`) | write/read records with Korean, emoji, quotes, newline, backslash, 200 KB body, null elements `[null,"x"]`, `Z` instants | exact round trip through the `@WritingConverter`/`@ReadingConverter` pair; `NULL` stays `NULL` (not `"null"`, not `""`) | plan: CLOB JSON | yes |
| REPO-020 | REPO | `session_grant.active_key` UNIQUE | two live grants with same author/project/scope; revoke the first; grant again; many revoked rows | second live rejected; after revoke (`active_key` = `revoked:<id>`) a new live grant works; many revoked rows coexist | 10_tables session_grant_one_open | yes |
| REPO-021 | REPO | `condition_link.link_key` UNIQUE | duplicate (cond,task); duplicate (cond,defect); cond C with task `X` and with defect `X`; cond `OLD-PIPE-C`+task `A\|T` and cond `OLD-PIPE-C\|A`+task `T` | duplicates rejected; the alias pair coexists; the separator pair coexists (no false collision); NULL columns never in the key | e2e.sh:S-U16..S-U20; 10_tables condition_link | yes |
| REPO-022 | REPO | other UNIQUE/PK/FK | duplicate defect_fix (defect,repo,commit); duplicate (project,release,code); defect_relation `a = b` (V4: the order check `a < b` is gone, LDIR-03); PK (a,b,kind); child without parent for each FK incl. `history.corrects_id`, `defect.merged_into` | rejected with class 23 | 10_tables | yes |
| REPO-023 | REPO | DML account | `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE` on history; `UPDATE`/`DELETE` on schema_history | each fails with a permission error; `INSERT` works. Owner account is the only DDL account | e2e.sh:S-D06 (rewritten) | yes |
| REPO-024 | REPO | DML account | `CREATE TABLE`, `DROP TABLE`, `ALTER TABLE` | fail (no DDL privilege) | plan: DML vs owner account | yes |
| REPO-025 | REPO | 30 rows | query of n+1 rows for n = 1, 10, 29, 30, 1000 | at most n+1 rows returned; the limit is the row-limit clause of the product, with a placeholder and no number in the SQL text (changed by SQB: it was JDBC `setMaxRows(n+1)` with no clause in the text; on the PostgreSQL of the tests the statement ends with `FETCH FIRST ? ROWS ONLY`) | plan: row limit; user 2026-11-15 「sqlBuilder 좋은말할때 써라.」 | yes |
| REPO-026 | REPO | 7 rows with identical `created_at` | page size 2 with `created_at < ? OR (created_at = ? AND id < ?)` ascending and descending | every row exactly once, stable order, on both DBs | plan: cursor | yes |
| REPO-027 | REPO | titles `Login Bug`, `login bug`, `LOGIN BUG`, `100%_done`, `a\b` | `LOWER(col) LIKE LOWER(?) ESCAPE '\'` for keywords `login`, `LOGIN`, `100%`, `_done`, `a\b`, injection text `'; DROP TABLE project; --` | ASCII matched case-insensitively; `%`, `_`, `\` are literal; injection text is data (table survives) | plan: ledger_search; ANSI-009 | yes |
| REPO-028 | REPO | rows at `2026-11-04T23:59:59.999999Z`, `...05T00:00:00Z`, `...05T14:59:59Z`, `...05T15:00:00Z` | `BETWEEN` for a UTC day and a KST day (via explicit instants) | inclusive on both ends; row sets exactly as computed from instants (boundary cases around midnight UTC vs midnight KST) | user decision 4 | yes |
| REPO-029 | REPO | JVM default zone `Asia/Seoul` and DB session zone `Asia/Seoul` | insert `Instant 2026-11-05T10:00:00.123456Z`, read raw column text and `Instant` | stored value equals the instant in UTC (raw read shows `10:00:00.123456`), read-back equal; run also with zone `America/New_York`, DST gap/overlap instants (`2026-03-08T07:30:00Z`, `2026-11-01T06:30:00Z`) | user decisions 4, time zone | yes |
| REPO-030 | REPO | DB session zone not UTC | startup guard compares `LOCALTIMESTAMP` with `LocalDateTime` of the UTC `Clock` | guard reports a mismatch (see SVC-TZ-01); equal within tolerance when UTC | user decision: DB UTC | yes |
| REPO-031 | REPO | max-length text columns (4000 Korean characters, 2000, 1000) | insert and read | exact, no truncation (large columns are of the long text type, `TEXT` by default) | 10_tables | yes |
| REPO-032 | REPO | id candidates differing only by case (`abc-T-1`, `ABC-T-1`) | insert through the service/repository | revised by REH3: the second one is refused (REH3-ID-04, `DUPLICATE_KEY`) and, when two creates race, the unique key of the normalised id column lets exactly one through (REH3-ID-05); a lookup of a task or condition compares the id exactly in Java after the fetch, so a DBMS that ignores case never returns the sibling (REH3-ID-06) | decision 7m, revised by REH3 | yes |
| REPO-033 | REPO | empty-string policy | after running the SVC scenarios, scan every text column | no row holds `''`; optional columns hold NULL | user decision 6 | yes |
| REPO-034 | REPO | defect/task/condition data | `COUNT ... GROUP BY` status, severity, state; attention queries with `EXISTS`/`NOT EXISTS` | counts equal the row set; the five attention kinds appear; `VERIFIED_WITHOUT_CI` is never produced | v_attention; e2e.mjs:V1-att | yes |
| REPO-035 | REPO | `code_revision` single row | 16 threads increment by `UPDATE ... +1` | final value +16; monotonic | plan: `code_revision` | yes |

| REPO-038 | REPO | `before_data` column | `UPDATE` through DML account | denied (immutable like the rest of history) | user decision 3 | yes |

AND status <> 'MERGED'` | 1 affected row for D1, 0 for D2 (no `FOR SHARE`, no vendor syntax); `version` and `updated_at` of D1 unchanged, `case_seq` +1 | decision 7m (case vs merge) | yes |
| REPO-040 | REPO | tx A bumped `case_seq` of D1 (not committed) | tx B merges D1 (UPDATE of the same row: status, `merged_into`, `version`+1) | B waits for A to commit; after both commit the final row is MERGED with A's bump retained; reverse order: A's conditional UPDATE affects 0 rows after B committed | decision 7m; e2e.sh:S-R01 | yes |

| REPO-052 | REPO | a closed key | register another key for the author; register the old key for another author; close twice | both work; closed rows pile up and are never deleted (`revoked:<id>` in both key columns); a closed key cannot be closed again | D-0012 | yes |

| REPO-062 | REPO | (attention, one text) the five attention kinds with their defects and tasks | the attention list, the defect list filtered by `FIXED_WITHOUT_COMMIT`, the counts of `StatsQueryRepository` | the list, the filters and the counts are made from the one `AttentionSql` (the statistics repository that only the tests used is deleted); the list holds the six items of the case, the filter the one defect of its kind; asking a task kind of the defect list is an `IllegalArgumentException` | plan 3 (same fact in several places) | yes |

## 4. SCH: V-file schema runner (MIGRATION level, PostgreSQL)

`SchemaMigrator` applies `V{n}__*.sql` by `ResourceDatabasePopulator` and records `schema_history(version, file_name, sha256, applied_at, outcome)`. Only `ledger-cli migrate` runs it, with the owner account; the apps never run DDL.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCH-001 | MIGRATION | empty database | `migrate` | all V files applied in numeric order (V2 before V10); one `schema_history` row per file (`attempt` 1), `outcome=SUCCEEDED`, `script`, `digest` equal to an independent SHA-256, `applied_at` UTC `Z`; exit 0 | e2e.sh fresh install | yes |
| SCH-002 | MIGRATION | schema already applied | `migrate` again | nothing executed, no new history rows, schema digest unchanged, exit 0 | e2e.sh:S-I01,S-I02 | yes |
| SCH-003 | MIGRATION | applied V2 file edited afterwards | `migrate`, then app start | `migrate` exits non-zero naming the version; the app refuses to start (health DOWN, message names version only); `migrate` stops before it applies anything, and no row is added or changed for the version (a changed file is no attempt, RUN-013); data untouched; a digest mismatch of an already SUCCEEDED version is never retried | user decision 1; decision 7f; common format 1 | yes |
| SCH-004 [run on PostgreSQL, see 1.4a] | MIGRATION | V3 whose 3rd statement is invalid | `migrate` | exit non-zero; `schema_history` has V3 attempt 1 `FAILED`, V1..V2 `SUCCEEDED`; V4 not applied; app refuses to start. PostgreSQL leaves no partial V3 objects (transactional DDL): the test asserts that the objects of the first two statements of V3 do not exist | e2e.sh:S-U03 (rewritten) | yes |
| SCH-005 | MIGRATION | after SCH-004 (V3 latest outcome FAILED) | `migrate` again without fixing, then after fixing the V3 file | each run retries V3 as the next attempt and inserts one `schema_history` row (attempt 2 `FAILED`, then attempt 3 `SUCCEEDED`; the rows of the earlier attempts are kept, RUN-015); V4 is not applied until V3 is `SUCCEEDED`; after the fix V3 and V4 apply and the final schema digest equals a clean run | e2e.sh:S-U03; decision 7f | yes |

| SCH-009 | MIGRATION | two `migrate` processes at once on an empty DB | run concurrently | final schema complete; exactly one SUCCEEDED row per version (attempt 1); the loser of a version waits in the database, gets the duplicate key and goes on: it is never refused and never half-applies a file (the overlap is made certain and observed in RUN-012) | e2e.sh:S-U04,S-U05 (concurrent upgrade) rewritten | yes |

## 5. DRV: JDBC driver registration (no bundled driver)

Driver registration is stored outside the database (the DB cannot be opened before a driver exists) in `/etc/<name>/jdbc-driver.yaml` (mode 0640, readable by the service user), read through `spring.config.import`. Registration is performed through the CLI command `driver-register` and the admin web driver page (setup mode included); the effective driver changes only after restart.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DRV-001 | CLI | readable test-driver jar (the PostgreSQL driver jar of the tests) and class `org.postgresql.Driver`, a PostgreSQL JDBC URL | `driver-register --jar=<path> --class=<name>` (changed by cli2: `--name=value`, and the jar is inside the allowed directory `ledger.cli.driver.directory`; test `DriverRegisterTest`) | accepted; stored record has absolute path, class, SHA-256 of the jar (equals independent digest), registered_at UTC `Z`; output shows path, class, digest (never DB password) | new: user decision 2 | no |
| DRV-002 | CLI | path does not exist / is a directory / unreadable / not absolute / contains `..` | register | `DRIVER_INVALID` (P) naming the reason; nothing stored (changed by cli2: the CLI shares the checks of the admin web: `..` and links are resolved with `toRealPath` and the resolved path must be inside the allowed directory, so `..` that stays inside is accepted as its real path and `..` that leaves is refused; the text is the one fixed text, the reason is in the log) | new | no |
| DRV-003 | CLI | file that is not a ZIP (text file renamed `.jar`), empty file, truncated jar | register | `DRIVER_INVALID`; nothing stored | new | no |
| DRV-004 | CLI | valid jar, class name absent from the jar; class exists but does not implement `java.sql.Driver`; class with no public no-arg constructor | register | (changed by cli2: one fixed text for the three, the specific reason is in the log, as ADM-060) `DRIVER_INVALID` each, message distinguishes the three; nothing stored | new | no |
| DRV-005 | CLI | driver class loads but `acceptsURL(configured JDBC URL)` is false (the PostgreSQL driver with a URL of another DBMS, for example `jdbc:other:x`) | register / validate | `DRIVER_INVALID` "driver does not accept URL"; URL value in the message is sanitised (no password) | new | no |
| DRV-006 | SERVICE | registered PostgreSQL driver jar | build the DataSource | chain is `URLClassLoader` -> `Driver` instance -> `SimpleDriverDataSource` -> Hikari; a connection is obtained and a query runs | new | yes |
| DRV-007 | SERVICE | after startup | inspect `DriverManager.getDrivers()` and the call graph | the registered driver is not registered in `DriverManager` (list empty or unchanged); no `DriverManager.getConnection` anywhere (see ARC-027) | new | yes |
| DRV-008 | SERVICE | registered jar changed on disk after registration (different SHA-256) | start the app | startup fails with a message naming the path and "digest mismatch"; no connection attempted | new (P: integrity check at start) | no |
| DRV-009 | SERVICE | registered jar deleted before start | start MCP server / CLI command / admin | MCP server and CLI fail fast with the path in the message; the admin starts in setup mode (ADM-042) | new | no |
| DRV-010 | SERVICE | running app, new driver registered | check running pool | running pool unchanged; after restart the new driver is used (proved with a distinguishing driver build) | new: "applied after restart" | no |
| DRV-011 [run on PostgreSQL, see 1.4a] | SERVICE | the PostgreSQL driver registered, then registered again from a second copy of the jar | restart each time | each start uses the registered driver; both pass SVC smoke (`save` + `ledger_get`) | new | yes |
| DRV-012 | SERVICE | no registration at all (no `jdbc-driver.yaml`) | start mcp-server; run each CLI command except `driver-register` | every one fails fast with a clear reason naming `driver-register` (`DRIVER_NOT_CONFIGURED`), exit non-zero, no vendor stack trace, no connection attempt; `driver-register` itself works | new; decision 7g | no |
| DRV-013 | ARCH | built artifacts (`bootJar` of mcp-server, admin-web, cli) | scan every nested jar and class | mcp-server and admin-web: zero `META-INF/services/java.sql.Driver`, zero classes implementing `java.sql.Driver`, no `org.postgresql`, `org.h2` or any other driver package (the PostgreSQL driver only on the test class path). cli (changed by unit SETUP, SET-041): the one driver is the H2 driver that reads the seed bundle, nothing of another DBMS | new; changed by SETUP | no |
| DRV-014 | ARCH | Gradle dependency graph | resolve `runtimeClasspath` of all apps | contains no JDBC driver artifact, except the H2 driver on `ledger-cli` and `ledger-seed` (changed by unit SETUP, SET-040: H2 only reads the seed bundle and the reason is written in the build file) | new; changed by SETUP | no |
| DRV-015 | ADMIN | admin session of the console (the driver page is a console page; the setup mode has only the login page, PSK-SET-01; run by `DriverPageTest`) | submit driver registration form with the DRV-002..005 invalid inputs | each rejected with `DRIVER_INVALID` ProblemDetail/validation message, CSRF required, nothing stored (no file, no audit row); the text of a refused jar or class is the one fixed text of ADM-060 (the specific reason is in the server log only), the blank-jar and blank-class messages stay specific; the jar must be in the allowed directory (ADM-060) | new | no |
| DRV-016 | ADMIN | admin session of the console, valid jar (run by `DriverPageTest`) | register | stored with SHA-256, page shows "takes effect after restart"; page lists path, class, digest; the `DRIVER_REGISTERED` audit row names the owner and the class | new | no |
| DRV-017 | CLI | registration of a jar that is group/world-writable | register | **changed by SFR-030 (section 42)**: registered as it is, the loader does not look at who may write the jar or its directory (`DriverBoundaryTest.drv017_aJarThatOthersCanWriteIsLoaded`, `DriverRegisterTest.acceptsALooseDirectory`). The old rule refused it (P: code is loaded into the process); the personal edition trusts the whole PC, even against another account of it | new (security); section 42 | no |
| DRV-020 | CLI | `driver-register` run as root for service user `ledger` | register | `/etc/<name>/jdbc-driver.yaml` exists with mode 0640, group = service group, content has jar path, class, SHA-256, registered_at UTC `Z`; written atomically (temp file + move, never wider than 0640); second registration replaces it | decision 7g | no |
| DRV-021 | SERVICE | yaml file present | start mcp-server, admin, CLI | all three read it through `spring.config.import` (no custom parser); a malformed or incomplete yaml fails fast naming the missing key; secrets (DB password) are not stored in this file | decision 7g | no |
| DRV-022 | ADMIN | admin in setup mode | open any page other than login and driver registration, e.g. `/defects`, `/api/overview`, `/grants` | refused (503 `DRIVER_NOT_CONFIGURED` or redirect to the driver page), nothing is read from the database; login works with the admin credential; after a successful registration the page says "restart required" | decision 7g | no |
| DRV-023 | CLI | `driver-register` with the DRV-002..005 invalid inputs | register | no file written/changed (an existing valid yaml stays byte-identical) | decision 7g | no |
| DRV-024 | SERVICE | registration file whose `jar` is not a path (it holds a NUL character), and one whose `jar` is relative | start mcp-server, admin, CLI | `ledger.jdbc.driver.jar` is a `java.nio.file.Path` bound by Boot: a value that is not a path stops the start with the Boot binding failure naming `ledger.jdbc.driver.jar` (the product has no `InvalidPathException` handling); a relative path binds and is refused by `Path.isAbsolute()` as `DRIVER_INVALID` "must be absolute" | cleanup: the jar is a Path, not a String | no |
| DRV-018 | SERVICE | two drivers in one jar (the test driver jar with the classes of the fake drivers) and `class` selects one | register | exactly the named class is instantiated; others never loaded | new | no |
| DRV-019 | SERVICE | class loader lifecycle | context closes | the `URLClassLoader` is closed, no file handle on the jar remains (jar can be deleted on Windows hosts) | new | no |
| DRV-025 | SERVICE | a registered jar of two fake drivers; `DriverLoader.load` returned the driver of the first class; then the registered file is overwritten with another jar | ask the loader of the driver for the second class (not loaded yet) and look at where the first class came from | the jar is read once into memory and the classes come from a private copy of exactly those bytes: the code source of the driver class is not the registered file, the second class still loads from the old bytes, the overwrite changes nothing (audit D7, TOCTOU between the digest and the load) | audit D7 | no |
| DRV-026 | SERVICE | a registered jar; `DriverLoader.load` returned the driver (still open) | delete the registered file, then use the driver | the delete succeeds (also on Windows: the loader holds the private copy, not the registered file) and the driver still answers `acceptsURL` | audit D7 | no |
| DRV-027 | SERVICE | the private copy in the temporary-file directory | `describe`; `load` then `close`; a refused `load` (digest mismatch, not an archive, class absent) | `describe` leaves no new file; `load` leaves exactly one new file while the driver is open (readable and writable by the owner only on a POSIX file system) and none after `close`; a refused `load` leaves none | audit D7 | no |
| DRV-028 | SERVICE | **removed by SFR-030 (section 42): the owner of the jar and of its directory is not looked at any more, `FileTrust` is deleted; the text below is the old rule.** POSIX file system: a jar that is owned by neither the process user (the owner of the private copy, which the process creates) nor the owner of its directory | `load` / `describe` | `DRIVER_INVALID`; a jar owned by the process user is accepted. **NOT_RUN at module level (reason):** the refusal needs a file of another owner (a second OS user or root with `chown`), which `Files` alone cannot make in a unit test; the accepting side is exercised by every loader test. It is checked at the test-server stage (E2E), where the jar is root-owned and the service runs as its own user | audit D7 | no |
| DRV-029 | SERVICE | an existing jar whose path contains `${` (a directory called `${x}`) | `describe`, and `load` with its registration | `DRIVER_INVALID` with a reason naming `${`; nothing is loaded (a Spring placeholder would be resolved when the registration file is read back through the configuration) | audit U7 | no |
| DRV-030 | DRIVER | **changed by SFR-030: every mode below loads (`drv030_aDirectoryThatOthersCanWriteIsNotRefused`), the sha256 is the jar's; the Then column is the old rule.** (audit2 N4) a jar in a directory that the group (`rwxrwx---`, `rwx-w----`) or others (`rwx---rwx`, `rwx----w-`) can write; POSIX file systems, `NOT_RUN` where there are no POSIX permissions | `describe` | `DRIVER_INVALID` naming the directory and "writable"; the same jar in a `rwx------` directory is accepted | audit2 N4 | no |
| DRV-031 | DRIVER | **changed by SFR-030: every entry loads (`drv031_aJarOrDirectoryThatAnotherAccountCanWriteIsNotRefusedByAcl`); the Then column is the old rule.** (audit2 N4) ACL view (the Windows file systems; `NOT_RUN` elsewhere): the jar, or its directory, has an ALLOW entry for `Everyone` with `WRITE_DATA`, `APPEND_DATA`, `DELETE`, `WRITE_ACL` or `WRITE_OWNER`; and an entry with read rights only | `describe` | the write-like entries are `DRIVER_INVALID` ("can be changed by"), for the jar and for the directory; the read-only entry is accepted; with the original ACL back the jar is accepted (the tests make their files owner-only with `OwnerOnlyFiles` first: a Windows file inherits the entries of its parent, and the parents of the test directories have entries for other accounts, so every test that hands a jar to the loader or the registration does that) | audit2 N4 | no |
| DRV-032 | DRIVER | **removed by SFR-030: the ACL of the directory of the jar is not looked at, so an empty ACL is no reason of a refusal (the test could not be kept).** (audit2 N4, S3) ACL view: the directory of the jar has an ACL without any entry (an empty ACL, or a NULL DACL: nobody or everybody may use it) | `describe` | `DRIVER_INVALID` ("without any entry"); the ACL is put back by the test afterwards | audit2 N4, S3 | no |
| DRV-033 | DRIVER | **changed by SFR-031: a link is followed, the jar loads and the digest is the jar's (`drv033_aSymbolicLinkToAJarIsFollowed`); the jar is still opened once and its file key, size and modification time are compared before and after the read ("changed while it was read"); the owner and permission rules named below and the `NOFOLLOW_LINKS` are the old rule.** (audit2 N4) a symbolic link to a jar (where links can be made; skipped with `TestAbortedException` otherwise) | `describe` | `DRIVER_INVALID`: the jar is opened once with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, its attributes and its protection are read right after the open (the JDK gives no attributes of an open channel, so the file key, size and modification time are read again after the read and must be unchanged), and the bytes come from that open channel; there is no `Files.readAllBytes` of the path and no check by path after the read; owner and permission rules: the owner of the jar and of its directory is the process user or an administrative account (`root`, `SYSTEM`, `Administrators` where they exist), and nobody else may write | audit2 N4 | no |
| DRV-040 | REPO | (F3, principle 4.4) a registration file with the old registration; `DriverRegistrationFile.register(registration, auditRow)`, the audit row a real `owner_audit` row in the PostgreSQL schema of the test, written by a call that opens and commits its own transaction (the test plays the `@Transactional` service method) | that call commits | the new file is written before the transaction (outside it); while the transaction runs the old file is still the registration; after the call has returned (the commit) the new file has replaced it by one atomic move; the row is committed; no temporary file stays | design (principle 4.4; plan 1 F3) | yes |
| DRV-041 | REPO | as DRV-040, the commit is refused (the work is rolled back) | `register` | the failure of the commit reaches the caller; the file is as it was; the temporary file is removed; the row is not there | design (F3, invariant c) | yes |
| DRV-042 | REPO | as DRV-040, the audit row cannot be written | `register` | the failure reaches the caller unchanged; the file is as it was; the temporary file is removed | design (F3, invariant c) | yes |
| DRV-043 | REPO | as DRV-040, the target of the move cannot be replaced (a directory that is not empty) | `register`, commit succeeds | the row is committed (it records the attempt); the replacement fails after the commit: logged at ERROR with the cause and thrown as `InfrastructureException` INTERNAL_ERROR with the `IOException` as cause, never swallowed; no temporary file is left; an atomic move that the file system does not support fails the same way (`ATOMIC_MOVE`, no copy; no case: the test file systems all support it) | design (F3, invariants b and d) | yes |
| DRV-044 | REPO | a transaction is already running (the caller must hold none: the audit row opens its own) | `register` | `IllegalStateException`: the new file would be written inside the transaction; nothing is written | design (F3, invariant a) | yes |
| DRV-045 | REPO | no database (the admin web in setup mode): the callback is a log line or nothing | `register` | the audit callback runs, the file is replaced at once, no temporary file stays | design (F3) | yes |
| DRV-046 | UNIT | (F6, `ApplicationContextRunner`) `LedgerJdbcProperties` built by hand and bound from `ledger.jdbc.*` with a password in the setting and in the JDBC URL (`?password=`, `//user:password@`) | `toString()` | no password in the text; the URL is printed without it, the account, the pool size and the driver registration are printed | design (plan 1 F6, like `LegacySourceProperties`) | no |
| DRV-050 | SERVICE | (`ApplicationContextRunner`) a bean behind `DriverRegisteredCondition`; nothing registered | start | the bean is not made, the context starts | design (the one condition of the admin web and the CLI) | no |
| DRV-051 | SERVICE | as DRV-050, profile `embedded` | start | the bean is made | design | no |
| DRV-052 | SERVICE | as DRV-050, the PostgreSQL driver jar registered with its SHA-256 and class | start | the bean is made | design | no |
| DRV-053 | SERVICE | as DRV-050, a registration the loader refuses: a SHA-256 that is not the jar's, a jar that is not there, a class that is not in the jar | start | the bean is not made, the context starts | design | no |
| DRV-054 | SERVICE | a program that overrides `needsDatabase` to false (the hook of the CLI condition) | start with a usable registration, then with the `embedded` profile | no bean with a registration (the program does not need the database); the bean with `embedded` | design | no |

## 6. SVC: application services (SERVICE level)

Database: the local PostgreSQL (1.4a); every case runs once. Fixtures: project `e2e`/`E2E`; actors `claude:e2e-author` (grants defect, task, fix, verify on the project), `codex:e2e-reviewer` (fix, verify), `gemini:e2e-nogrant` (none). Channel AUTHOR = write tools, REVIEWER = `ledger_review`, `ledger_review_correct`. Time comes from an injected fixed UTC `Clock`.

### 6.1 SVC-GATE: write gate (`WriteGate.open(actor, operationKey, channel, lookup)`; changed by opkey: was `requestId`)
Order asserted by SVC-GATE-17 and SVC-GATE-31 (changed by D-0012): verify-needs-reviewer, attestation present, attestation parsed and author format, **signed attestation: live registered key, signature, tool and operation key of the call, time window**, project known, grant, then reason, then the operation key (changed by opkey: refused when absent, empty or over 128 characters), then receipt, then business rules. The signature checks sit after the format check and before the project lookup, so the cases that pair "no attestation / bad format" with a later failure (SVC-GATE-17) keep their winner, and nothing about projects or grants is learned by a caller that cannot prove its author. The tool name is the `ToolScope` bound by the MCP tool binding around the tool call (unbound is a mismatch); the operation key is the one of the call (of a batch: of the whole batch, every operation is checked against it).

Test support: the service tests pass the plain author of the actors below; `SigningAttestationVerifier` (one place of the test support) signs it with the key of the author, registered as the owner does, and hands the signed text to the real `SignedAttestationVerifier`, so every call of every service test runs the whole verification. A text that already is a token (SVC-GATE-20..35) goes to the real verifier as it is. Actors of the fixtures all have a registered key, `gemini:e2e-nogrant` too: it is refused for the missing grant (SVC-GATE-03), not for the missing key.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SVC-GATE-01 | SERVICE | actor null | any write service (save, transition, record_fix, record_case, link, correct, batch, review, review_correct) and whoami | `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED`, nothing written, nothing in `request_receipt` | e2e.mjs:H2 | no |

| SVC-RCP-09 | SERVICE | 16 threads, same R, same payload | all call at once | exactly one commit, 1 history row, 1 receipt; every caller that succeeds receives an equal response; every other caller ends with the translated `DUPLICATE_KEY` (rewritten by the unit txpure, principle 4.4: a call at the same moment meets the unique key of the operation key and is not answered from a nested write) | plan: concurrent duplicates | yes |
