| SVC-RCP-14 | SERVICE | batch with operation key B | replay B | whole stored `results`; also per-op derived keys make the ops idempotent individually (changed by opkey: ids became keys, OPK-005; changed by opkey round 2: the batch has a receipt of its own under (author, B), operation `BATCH`, with the digest of all operations, and the operations keep receipts under `derive(i)`, OPK-019) | e2e.mjs:E-22 | no |
| SVC-RCP-16 | SERVICE | (TXA-04) `ReceiptService.reserve` | read the type of its answer | not an `Optional`: a sealed type whose permitted subclasses are exactly the records `Reserved` and `Replayed`; the behaviour (first call writes, a replay answers the stored response) is that of SVC-RCP-01..15 (red before the fix: the answer was an `Optional`, the two states being "empty" and "present") | principle 3.3, 9 item 35 | no |
| SVC-RCP-17 | SERVICE | (TXF-001, rewritten by the unit txpure) a receipt of operation key R that is reserved and finished in a transaction | `reserve` of R again in the same transaction, then another operation key reserved and finished | the second `reserve` reads the receipt and answers `Replayed` with the stored response (no insert is tried: no duplicate key, no savepoint, no nested write); the second key commits with its response (red on 8800df2: the answer came from a `DuplicateKeyException` that a NESTED `ReceiptWriter` took back) | principle 4.4 | no |
| SVC-RCP-18 | SERVICE | (txpure) a committed receipt of R with a stored response; a `ReceiptService` whose repository finds no receipt (the read ran before the other call committed) | `reserve` of R in a transaction; then `reserve` of R through the real service | the first ends with the translated `DUPLICATE_KEY`, nothing of it stays written (one receipt, the response of the winner untouched); a sequential repeat answers `Replayed` with the response of the first call (red on 8800df2: the first answered `Replayed`) | principle 4.4 | no |

### 6.9a LDIR: the direction of a relation kind (defect PG_LEDGER_MCP-D-0016)
The direction is a property of the code of the relation kind (`common_code.direction`, V4): `GIVEN` keeps the two ends in the order the caller gave them (`caused_by`: a is caused by b; `blocks`: a blocks b), `CANONICAL` keeps the canonical order (a sorts before b), so that (a, b) and (b, a) are one relation (`same_site`, `same_family`, `same_fix_unit`; `regression_of` is defined by the order of the ids). A kind added later declares its direction too. V1 `ck_defect_relation_order` (a < b) is replaced by `ck_defect_relation_distinct` (a <> b) in V4.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LDIR-01 | UNIT | a catalogue of relation kinds: `caused_by` GIVEN, `same_site` without direction, a retired GIVEN kind; a `DEFECT_CATEGORY` code with GIVEN | `CodeCatalog.directionOf`, `new CommonCode` | no direction is CANONICAL; a retired kind keeps its direction; an unknown code has none; a code of another group cannot be GIVEN (`VALIDATION_FAILED`) | D-0016 | no |
| LDIR-02 | UNIT | defects D1, D2 | `DefectRelation.between` with GIVEN in both orders, the same defect twice | the ends stay as given, (a, b) and (b, a) are two relations with their own history ids; the same defect twice is `VALIDATION_FAILED` | D-0016 | no |
| LDIR-03 | REPO | a CANONICAL relation (d2, d1) and (d1, d2); a GIVEN relation in both orders; two equal ends | `LinkRepository.add`, `relationsOf`, `countRelations`, a raw insert with a = b | CANONICAL is one row, GIVEN two rows (descending order allowed), the counts per kind are right, a = b is refused with class 23 | D-0016 | yes |
| LDIR-04 | SERVICE | defects D1 < D2 | link `caused_by` (D2, D1), then (D1, D2), then (D2, D1) again; `blocks` (D2, D1) | the first is stored as `D2\|D1\|caused_by`, the second is another row, the repeat is `NOTHING_CHANGES`; the history entity id is the stored order | D-0016 | no |
| LDIR-05 | SERVICE | `caused_by` (D2, D1) stored | unlink (D1, D2), then unlink (D2, D1) | the first is `NOTHING_CHANGES` (the other direction is not stored), the second removes the row and keeps it as `before_data` | D-0016 | no |
| LDIR-06 | SERVICE | `same_site`, `same_family`, `same_fix_unit`, `regression_of` | link (D2, D1), link (D1, D2), unlink in the other order | stored as `D1\|D2\|kind`, the second link is `NOTHING_CHANGES`, either order removes it | D-0016 | no |
| LDIR-07 | SERVICE | a relation kind added with direction GIVEN; one added without a direction | link, retire the kind, link and unlink again | the new kind keeps the order of the caller; once retired a new link is `CODE_INVALID`, a command without a direction keeps the direction, the unlink still finds the row in the stored order; a kind without a direction is canonical | D-0016 | no |
| LDIR-08 | SERVICE | `blocks` given as (other, d) with the later id first | `DetailService.defect` of d and of other | the relation view names the end of the defect itself: d is `B`, other is `A` | D-0016 | no |

### 6.13 SVC-BEF: `history.before_data`
`before_data` = JSON of the full entity record as it was immediately before the change, written in the same transaction as the `changes` diff. Field names equal column names (P); instants end in `Z`.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SVC-BEF-01 | SERVICE | create defect, task, condition, case, link, new fix commit | read history rows | `before_data` NULL for every CREATE/LINK | user decision 3 | no |
| SVC-BEF-02 | SERVICE | defect edit | read the EDIT row | `before_data` equals a snapshot read just before the call (all columns incl. `version`=expected, old `updated_at`, `created_by`); `changes` diff still present; current row differs | new | yes |
| SVC-BEF-03 | SERVICE | task and condition edits | read | same for each entity (condition without `updated_at`) | new | no |
| SVC-BEF-04 | SERVICE | defect/task/condition transitions through transition and review | read | `before_data` has the previous state and version; leaving MERGED shows the old `merged_into` | new | no |
| SVC-BEF-05 | SERVICE | record_fix: (a) new commit on OPEN defect (b) new commit by another session on SOURCE_FIXED (c) CI update of an existing commit (d) `mark_source_fixed=false` | read history | (a) fix CREATE: NULL; defect TRANSITION: defect before_data (OPEN, old owner); (b) defect EDIT: before_data of defect; (c) defect_fix EDIT: before_data = old fix row; (d) defect EDIT with before_data; (e) **exception, verifyrules review**: a new commit by the owner or a CI update with the owner and the status unchanged saves only `updated_at` and the version of the defect, writes no history row of the defect and so stores no defect before_data (a fix row only, with its own before_data in the CI case); the README names the exception | new | no |
| SVC-BEF-06 | SERVICE | link then unlink | read | LINK NULL; UNLINK `before_data` = the removed link row (P: removal is a modification of existing state) | new | no |
| SVC-BEF-07 | SERVICE | record_case; correct | read | NULL (they only insert history/case rows); the `case_seq` bump on the defect row is the case number (`DefectRepository.nextCaseSeq`), not a modification of the defect: no defect history row, no `before_data`, `version` and `updated_at` unchanged | new; decision 7m | no |
| SVC-BEF-08 | SERVICE | any op fails after `before_data` was prepared (injected exception after the row update) | call | zero history rows, zero receipts, entity unchanged, counters unchanged | user decision 3 | yes |
| SVC-BEF-09 | SERVICE | concurrent edits with the same expected version | two threads | winner's `before_data.version` = expected; loser `VERSION_CONFLICT`, no history | user decision 3 | yes |
| SVC-BEF-10 | SERVICE | replay of an EDIT | replay | no second history row, no second `before_data` | new | no |
| SVC-BEF-11 | SERVICE | defect with 4000-char Korean behaviour | edit | `before_data` stores it exactly | REPO-019 | yes |
| SVC-BEF-12 | SERVICE | default `ledger_history`/`ledger_defect` reads | read | no `before_data` unless `include_before=true` (see MCR-H11) | new | no |

### 6.17 SVC-RACE: races
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SVC-RACE-01 | SERVICE | latch-driven interleavings: merge of D5 into D2 starts, record_case on D5 starts (a) while merge waits at its end (b) after merge commit | run both | after (a) and (b): the merge commits; the case is either refused with `MERGED_DEFECT: <D5> was merged into <D2>` or recorded before the merge and then visible as a case of D2; invariant: no case on a MERGED defect has a history id greater than that defect's merge TRANSITION row; no case remains that is invisible to the target. Mechanism (decision 7m): `record_case` runs `UPDATE defect SET case_seq = case_seq + 1 WHERE id = ? AND status <> 'MERGED'`; affected rows 0 -> `MERGED_DEFECT`; the merge updates the same row so the two serialise; `case_seq` does not bump `version` | e2e.sh:S-R01,S-R02,S-R03 | yes |
| SVC-RACE-09 | SERVICE | defect D1 with `version` 3 | 20 `record_case` calls, sequentially and then 20 in parallel | `case_seq` +20, 20 cases, `version` still 3, no defect history row, a concurrent `ledger_save` with `expected_version` 3 still succeeds (the bump does not cause `VERSION_CONFLICT`) | decision 7m | yes |
| SVC-RACE-02 | SERVICE | two transitions with the same expected version | run concurrently | exactly one commits, the other `VERSION_CONFLICT` (never a raw SQL error) | sql 40001 | yes |
| SVC-RACE-03 | SERVICE | two edits with the same expected version | run | same | sql save | yes |
| SVC-RACE-04 | SERVICE | two record_fix on one OPEN defect, different commits | run | defect SOURCE_FIXED, exactly one OPEN->SOURCE_FIXED TRANSITION history row, every successful call has its fix row; losers may get `VERSION_CONFLICT`, nothing else | sql record_fix "no write skew" | yes |
| SVC-RACE-05 | SERVICE | two reviewers verify the same SOURCE_FIXED defect | run | exactly one VERIFIED; the other `VERSION_CONFLICT` or `TRANSITION_INVALID` | new | yes |
| SVC-RACE-06 | SERVICE | two identical links | run | one OK, one `NOTHING_CHANGES`; no duplicate-key leak | sql link | yes |
| SVC-RACE-11 | SERVICE | (txpure, principle 4.4) an OPEN defect; a `record_fix` and a transition to DISPUTED with the same expected version, 30 rounds | run concurrently | there is no row-lock statement before the writes any more (`RowLockRepository` removed): both writes save the defect row (conditional on its version) before they take a counter (id, history), so they cannot wait for each other; each call succeeds or ends with `VERSION_CONFLICT` or `TRANSITION_INVALID`, never a raw deadlock error; a `defect_fix` row exists exactly when the fix succeeded (a guard, not red on 8800df2 where the lock statement serialised the two calls: it fails when the lock statement is removed without the reorder in `RecordFixOperation`, where the counters were taken before the defect row) | principle 4.4 | yes |
| SVC-RACE-07 | SERVICE | write while the grant is revoked in parallel | run 200 rounds | never a write committed after the revoke commit; REPO-016 at service level | sql FOR SHARE (rewritten) | yes |
| SVC-RACE-08 | SERVICE | code-set vs save using the new code | run | save uses either old or new code set consistently, no exception other than `CODE_INVALID` | new | yes |
| SVC-RACE-10 | SERVICE | F9: two transactions, tx A runs the gate of a write (the key check passed, not committed), tx B closes the key of the author (revoke) | latch-driven: B starts after A verified; then A commits; then repeat with B committed first | B does not wait for A (rewritten by the unit txpure, principle 4.4: the key is read, not locked; no statement of the write can decide the race, so the ceiling is documented in `SignedAttestationVerifier.verifyForWrite`): the close commits at once, A commits afterwards (the window is the time of A's transaction); a write that verifies after B committed is `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`; 100 rounds with a write and a revoke in parallel: the write is either written or refused with `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID` and left nothing, and every write after the close is refused. The grant check (SVC-RACE-07) is a real conditional UPDATE and keeps its waiting | audit F9; txpure | yes |

### 6.18 SVC-PRN: development principles of the application module (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit W3)
Source of the cases: plan 2 (`ags-3446-solid-plan.md`), items F1, F4, F5 and the application items of 2.2 to 2.6. The behaviour that is not listed here is kept as it was and stays covered by the cases above (every test of this module runs against the refactored code unchanged in meaning).

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SVC-PRN-01 | SERVICE | F1: a task whose history holds imported TRANSITION rows (**changed by M1X (section 43)**: rows with `source_ref`, not legacy rows) with a `from_state` that is NULL, a `from_state` that is no task state (`WEIRD`) and a `to_state` that is no task state, next to one real REPORTED_DONE transition by session A | the task detail is read | no exception; `completed_by` is session A (a transition with an unknown state is not a report); with no real report at all `completed_by` is null | plan 2 F1 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-02 | SERVICE | F1: the same task history as SVC-PRN-01, the task is REPORTED_DONE or in progress | a transition of the task (to the next state) is written | the transition is accepted or refused by the rules of the state machine, never by a `NullPointerException` or an `IllegalArgumentException`; an unknown state in the history is not a report, so a reporter of the same session as the imported TRANSITION rows (**changed by M1X (section 43)**: rows with `source_ref`) is not blocked by them | plan 2 F1 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-03 | SERVICE | F4: a save that creates a task with the explicit id `<CODE>-T-` plus 19 digits, and one with 18 digits | save | the 19 digit id is stored as a free id and the task counter of the project is not raised (it has no number); the 18 digit id raises the counter to its number; no `NumberFormatException` in either case (**changed by verifyrules, section 45**: the 18 digit id is a number above the next number, so it is refused with `ID_NUMBER_AHEAD` and the counter stays; the number of an issued form is taken only up to the next number, VRF-026; the 19 digit free id is unchanged) | plan 2 F4; D-0022 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-04 | SERVICE | F4: a history call whose cursor is well formed for this call but whose id is `12x`, empty, or 19 digits (`9999999999999999999`) | history with that cursor | `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `cursor` (`CURSOR_DAMAGED`), never a `NumberFormatException`; a cursor with an 18 digit id is read (the page is empty or later) | plan 2 F4 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-05 | MIGRATION | F4: a legacy task whose id is `<CODE>-T-` plus 20 digits and one whose id is `<CODE>-T-` plus 4 digits | import and verify | the import is not stopped by a `NumberFormatException`; the 20 digit id has no number (counter unchanged by it), the 4 digit id counts as before | plan 2 F4 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-06 | SERVICE | F5: a defect, a task and a condition read at version 1, then edited (version 2) by a committed write | the stale row (version 1) is saved through the one save of the write operations | `VERSION_CONFLICT` whose arguments are `<entity> <id> 1 2`: the version that the stale row was read at and the version in the database now, never `?` | plan 2 F5 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-07 | ARCH | F5: the main sources of the application module | scan for the code `VERSION_CONFLICT` | only `Rows.java` makes the conflict of a save: `ledger_save`, `ledger_transition` and `record_fix` reach it through the one save of `Rows`, so none has a copy that puts a placeholder in the arguments (a race that loses the update cannot be provoked deterministically; SVC-PRN-06 runs the very statement that loses it). The one other file is `CommonCodeService.java` (TXF-002): the version-conditional update of a `common_code` row is not a row that `Rows` manages (defect, task, condition) and its arguments are another shape (`common_code`, `<group>/<code>`, the versions), so it is a creation point of its own, accepted here | plan 2 F5 | no |
| SVC-PRN-08 | UNIT | the contract vectors of the author format (`ledger-attestation` test fixtures `contract/author-vectors.json`: valid, invalid, null) | each is given to the domain `AuthorId` | every valid text is accepted (`vendor()` and `session()` split it at the colon); every invalid text is refused with `AUTHOR_ID_FORMAT` and a null with `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED`; `AuthorId.matches` agrees; the same vectors are run by the hook against its `Author` (HK) | plan 2 30, brief | no |
| SVC-PRN-09 | UNIT | the contract vectors of the tool names (`contract/write-tool-vectors.json`) | compared with the domain `ToolContract` | `ToolContract.WRITE` and `ToolContract.READ` equal the `write` and `read` lists of the vectors, in order | plan 2 79, brief | no |
| SVC-PRN-10 | SERVICE | a batch whose single operation of each kind lacks one required key (transition without `to` and without `expected_version`, record_fix without `commit_sha`, record_case without `observation`, link without `b`, correct without `history_id`, save without `fields`), also given as JSON `null`; and, without a database, an operation of each `BatchOpKind` that has no key at all | batch; the validator on the record | `VALIDATION_FAILED` on the path `ops[0].<key>` with the reason `REQUIRED`, details `op_index` 1 and `op`; an operation without any key violates exactly `BatchOpKind.requiredKeys()` (snake case); a missing key in a later operation names `ops[i]` of that operation and rolls the earlier ones back (SVC-BAT-06) | plan 2.6 | partly |
| SVC-PRN-11 | SERVICE | a receipt that is not there (`finish` for an operation key that was never reserved) | `ReceiptService.finish` | `InfrastructureException` `INTEGRITY_VIOLATION` (no `IllegalStateException`) | plan 2.5 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-12 | SERVICE | whoami with a value that is not an author (`not-an-author`) and with a signed attestation whose author claim has no author format | whoami | the answer is `authorFormatOk=false` with the text, no grants, no exception used for the answer; every other failure of the attestation (unsigned, unknown key, bad signature) is still the error of the gate | plan 2.5 | yes |
| SVC-PRN-13 | UNIT | the validation translator (a plain class used by the method validation of the services and by the batch) | a violation of the property `maxRows` of a record, and a violation under the prefix `ops[2]` | the path is `max_rows` (Jackson snake case) and `ops[2].<key>`; issues are sorted by path; the first names the failure | plan 2.6 | no |

## 7. MCW: MCP write-tool contract (MCP Java SDK client -> Boot server, PostgreSQL)

Ten write tools: `ledger_save, ledger_transition, ledger_record_fix, ledger_record_case, ledger_link, ledger_correct, ledger_batch, ledger_review, ledger_review_correct, ledger_whoami`. Tool names, input keys, `_hostAttestation` and error tokens are the external contract.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCW-001 | MCP | started server | `tools/list` | contains all ten write tools; contains no tool named `execute_sql*` or `search_objects*` (writing or reading) | e2e.mjs:E-01,E-02 | no |
| MCW-002 | MCP | each write tool schema | inspect | `_hostAttestation` is an optional string (not in `required`); `operationKey` is a declared required string property (changed by opkey, OPK-001); `author_id`, `request_id` and `batch_request_id` are not declared properties; unknown properties are not allowed | e2e.mjs:H1 | no |
| MCW-003 | MCP | each of the ten tools | call without `_hostAttestation` | `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED` for save, whoami and the other eight (transition, record_fix, record_case, link, correct, batch, review, review_correct); nothing written | e2e.mjs:H2 (both blocks) | no |
| MCW-004 | MCP | whoami with `_hostAttestation=AUTHOR` and `author_id=NOGRANT` | call | **changed by opkey (section 44; before: the deprecated `author_id` was accepted and ignored)**: `author_id` is refused as `UNKNOWN_ARGUMENT` (whoami has only `_hostAttestation`); whoami with `_hostAttestation=AUTHOR` alone: `author` is AUTHOR and `grants` has the 4 AUTHOR grants (OPK-006) | e2e.mjs:H3 | no |
| MCW-005 | MCP | save with only the deprecated `author_id=AUTHOR` (**changed by opkey (section 44)**: with a valid `operationKey`, the other arguments valid) | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` `UNKNOWN_ARGUMENT` for `author_id` (was `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED`: no argument `author_id` exists any more); nothing written (OPK-006) | e2e.mjs:H3 | no |
| MCW-006 | MCP | arguments `titel`, `authorId`, `Author_Id`, `_hostattestation`, extra nested key in `fields` | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming each unknown argument; (changed by opkey: `author_id` is no longer an accepted legacy key, `request_id` and `batch_request_id` are unknown too); `_hostAttestation=""` -> `AUTHOR_ID_FORMAT` | plan: unknown argument rejected | no |
| MCW-007 | MCP | `fields` as JSON object and as JSON text form (**changed by opkey (section 44; before: accepted as "JSON text form")**) | call both with the same `operationKey` | the object is written; the text form is `VALIDATION_FAILED` `WRONG_TYPE` (path `fields`, OPK-008) and writes nothing; no receipt replay between the two | plan: object and legacy string | no |
| MCW-008 | MCP | `ops` as JSON array and as JSON text form (**changed by opkey (section 44; before: accepted as "JSON text form")**) | `ledger_batch` | the array runs; the text form is `VALIDATION_FAILED` `WRONG_TYPE` (path `ops`, OPK-008), nothing written | plan | no |
| MCW-009 | MCP | `fields` as invalid JSON string, `"[]"`, `"7"`, `"null"`, `null`, `{}`; `ops` as `"{"`, `{}`, `[]` | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED`, nothing written (changed by opkey: every text for an object or array parameter is `WRONG_TYPE`, also the text `null`, which is a plain text and no longer "missing"; the real `null` of a required parameter stays `REQUIRED`; OPK-008) | plan; sql save/batch | no |
| MCW-010 | MCP | whoami with granted attestation | call | keys `session_user` (login label of the channel, P), `author`, `author_format_ok=true`, `can_verify_login=false`, `grants[]{project,scope,expires_at}` | e2e.mjs:E-06 | no |
| MCW-011 | MCP | whoami with ungranted / malformed attestation | call | ungranted: empty `grants`; malformed (`claude`): `author_format_ok=false` | sql whoami | no |

| MCW-024 | MCP | tools invoked as `ledger_get`-style read tool with `_hostAttestation` | call | read tools do not need it; property is not declared (see MCR-001) | plan | no |
| MCW-026 | MCP | body above the configured limit sent with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` (no `Content-Length`), and a chunked body exactly at the limit | call | above: HTTP 413 / `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (problem detail), nothing written, the body is not read beyond the limit + 1 byte; at the limit: accepted. One servlet filter (counts the bytes, rejects through the `HandlerExceptionResolver` to the one advice) also answers the declared `Content-Length` case of MCW-022; Tomcat `maxPostSize` (form parsing) and `maxSwallowSize` (aborted uploads) do not apply | security audit (Tomcat 11 HTTP connector documentation) | no |
| MCW-027 | MCP | `ledger_batch` with `ops:[{"op":"drop"}]` and with an op that has no `op` key | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED`; unknown op: `UNKNOWN_VALUE` at `ops[0].op` with the allowed ops (`save`, `transition`, `record_fix`, `record_case`, `link`, `correct`); missing: `REQUIRED` at `ops[0].op`; neither the text nor the structured content has `org.pgledger` or `BatchOp`; other Jackson failures carry a fixed reason, not the library text | security audit: internal class name in the error result | no |

## 8. MCR: MCP read-tool contract (12 tools)

Tools: `ledger_get, ledger_defect, ledger_task, ledger_history, ledger_search, ledger_defects, ledger_tasks, ledger_conditions, ledger_attention, ledger_stats, ledger_projects, ledger_codes`. Input/output keys other than `max_rows`, `cursor`, `truncated`, `next_cursor`, `include_before` are provisional `(P)`; each case's semantic is binding. List tools return `items[]`, `truncated`, `next_cursor`.

### 8.1 Common behaviour
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCR-001 | MCP | the 12 read tools | inspect schemas | each declares `outputSchema`; `structuredContent` validates against it; none declares `_hostAttestation` or `author_id`; unknown args rejected | plan: `generateOutputSchema` | no |
| MCR-002 | MCP | list tools (`ledger_history, ledger_search, ledger_defects, ledger_tasks, ledger_conditions, ledger_attention, ledger_projects, ledger_codes`) | `max_rows` = 1 and 1000 | accepted | plan | no |
| MCR-003 | MCP | same | `max_rows` = 0, -1, 1001, "a", 50.5 | `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `max_rows` | plan | no |
| MCR-004 | MCP | 120 rows | no `max_rows` | 50 items, `truncated=true`, `next_cursor` present | plan: default 50 | no |
| MCR-005 | MCP | exactly `max_rows` rows exist | list | `truncated=false`, `next_cursor` absent/null | plan: row limit of n+1 rows (SQB) | no |
| MCR-006 | MCP | `max_rows+1` rows exist | list | `truncated=true`, `next_cursor` present, `items.length = max_rows` | plan | no |
| MCR-007 | MCP | each list tool, 137 rows, `max_rows` 10 | follow `next_cursor` until absent | every row exactly once, stable order, last page `truncated=false` | plan: cursor | no |
| MCR-008 | MCP | page 1 fetched, then 5 new rows inserted and 5 existing rows edited | fetch page 2.. | no duplicate, no skipped original row (cursor stability); new rows appear only where the sort order puts them (newest-first lists: not in later pages; history by id ascending: at the end) | plan: cursor stability | no |
| MCR-009 | MCP | garbage, truncated, other tool's cursor, cursor with changed filters | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` (P: cursor bound to tool + filters) | DOM-031 | no |
| MCR-010 | MCP | any read call | compare row counts, versions, `updated_at` before/after 100 calls | unchanged (`@Transactional(readOnly=true)`); no attestation required; no grant needed | e2e.mjs:E-31,E-32 (rewritten) | no |
| MCR-011 | MCP | server | call `execute_sql_reader`, `search_objects_reader`, `ledger_codes` | the first two: unknown-tool protocol error with no side effect; `ledger_codes` exists (the old "removed" check is inverted, see section 18) | e2e.mjs:K1,E-31 | no |
| MCR-012 | MCP | timestamps in any output | inspect | every instant ends with `Z` | user decision 4 | no |
| MCR-013 | MCP | no bearer / wrong bearer | any read tool | HTTP 401 | plan | no |

| MCR-T02 | MCP | unknown task id | call | `NOT_FOUND` | plan | no |

### 8.6 Lists, attention, stats, projects, codes
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |

## 11. CLI: `ledger-cli`

Commands (enum, `ApplicationArguments`): `migrate, project-add, code-set, codes, grant, revoke, grants, secret-set, import-legacy, mcp-check, driver-register`. Every command except `driver-register` needs a registered driver (DRV-012). Exit codes: 0 success, 1 failure, 2 usage.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CLI-001 | CLI | no argument / unknown command / missing arguments | run | exit 2, usage lists every command (**changed by M1X (section 43)**: the command order is `secret-set, token-generate, driver-register, migrate, setup, key-register, ...`; the three 1.x commands are not commands of `ledger-cli`, they are `import-1x`, `retire-1x` and `import-secrets-1x` of `ledger-migrate-1x`; changed by FUP-USG: only when no command is known, that is no word or an unknown word; a known command with wrong arguments or options lists its own usage line only) | ledger-admin usage | no |
| CLI-002 | CLI | web-less Boot app | start any command | no server port opened; process exits | plan | no |
| CLI-003 | CLI | empty DB | `migrate` | exit 0, SCH-001 outcome | e2e.sh fresh | yes |
| CLI-004 | CLI | migrated DB | `migrate` again | exit 0, nothing applied, data digest unchanged | e2e.sh:S-I01..S-I03 | yes |
| CLI-005 | CLI | `project-add e2e E2E "End-to-end test project"`; invalid id/code/title; duplicate | run | success prints project; invalid exit 1 `VALIDATION_FAILED`; duplicate `DUPLICATE_KEY` | ledger-admin project-add | no |

## 12. SEC: secret store (`ledger-cli secret-set`, configtree)

File locations are test directories (configurable); POSIX permission assertions run on Linux hosts, other hosts mark them NOT_RUN honestly.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-001 | CLI | no secrets directory | `secret-set <name>` with hidden input | directory created with mode 0700; file `<name>` created with mode 0600 (via `Files` + `PosixFilePermissions`, mode set at creation, never widened and narrowed later); content equals the input byte for byte | plan: secret store | no |
| SEC-002 | CLI | the same name again | `secret-set` | replaced atomically (temp file 0600 + move); mode stays 0600; no window with wider mode | plan | no |
| SEC-003 | CLI | input source is not a console (piped stdin), or `Console` unavailable | `secret-set` | refuses (exit 1) with a message; never reads the value from arguments, environment or a pipe; the value is not echoed | plan: `Console.readPassword` | no |
| SEC-004 | CLI | commands `secret-get`, `secret-show`, `secret-list`-with-values | run | unknown command (exit 2); no code path prints a stored secret; `secret-set` prints only the name and path | plan: no get | no |
| SEC-005 | CLI | names `../x`, `a/b`, empty, uppercase, 200 chars, `.hidden` | `secret-set` | refused; only `^[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$` accepted (P) | plan | no |
| SEC-006 | CLI | existing directory with mode 0755 or file with 0644; a symbolic link in place of the secret file | `secret-set`, `store`, `load`, `exists` | **changed by SFR-010 / SFR-011 (section 42)**: the existing path is used as it is (the personal edition trusts the whole PC); the directory keeps its mode, the secret is read, and the replacement is a new 0600 file; a link is followed when read and a store replaces the link, not the file it points at. The old rule refused and named the path and mode (fail closed) | plan; section 42 | no |
| SEC-007 | SERVICE | secrets directory with files `ledger.mcp.bearer-token` style names (P) | start app with `spring.config.import=configtree:<dir>` | properties bound into `@ConfigurationProperties` records; missing required secret fails startup naming the property, never a value | plan | no |
| SEC-008 | SERVICE | running app | inspect `/actuator/*`, logs, error responses, thread dumps in logs | the secret value never appears; Actuator `env`/`configprops` not exposed | plan | no |
| SEC-009 | E2E | rotate a secret with `secret-set`, restart | call MCP with old and new bearer | old refused, new accepted | plan | no |
| SEC-010 | E2E | **replaced by SEC-026, SEC-027 and M1X-030 (section 43): the installers know nothing of 1.x; `import-secrets-1x` of `ledger-migrate-1x` moves the 1.x environment values into the secret store.** Was: `install` on a host that has the old env keys (`DBHUB_AUTH_TOKEN`, admin token, DB passwords) | run installer | values moved into the secret store (0600), env file keeps only references, old env backed up 0600, nothing printed | plan: env -> secrets | no |
| SEC-011 | E2E | unit file and env file after install | scan | no secret-looking literal (32+ hex/base64 strings, DSN with password) | plan | no |
| SEC-012 | CLI | a file system with neither POSIX permissions nor ACLs (a zip file system in the test) | `secret-set` / the store | fails with an explicit message (`NON_POSIX_FILESYSTEM`) instead of storing with default permissions, and creates nothing. Changed by install: a file system with ACLs but without POSIX permissions (the Windows file systems) is no longer refused, it is protected by an ACL (SEC-016 .. SEC-021) | plan; install: Windows | no |
| SEC-013 | CLI | secrets directory or target file owned by another user than the running process (e.g. created by root while the services run as the service account) | `secret-set`, `check`, `load`, `store` | **changed by SFR-010 (section 42)**: used as they are, the check passes, the secret is read and replaced; the code `FOREIGN_OWNER` and its message are removed (nothing uses them any more). A real owner cannot be set without a privilege, so the test takes the process account to be root through the `ProcessUser` stub (POSIX only; skipped on Windows) | main review of 00f8324; section 42 | no |
| SEC-014 | CLI | parent of the secrets directory (`/etc/<instance>`) missing or not a directory | `secret-set` | refused before the prompt, exit 1 `PARENT_MISSING` naming the parent; only the secrets directory itself is ever created (the installer creates the parent) | main review of 00f8324 | no |
| SEC-015 | SERVICE | a temporary file left in the secrets directory by an interrupted `secret-set` | start an app with `spring.config.import=configtree:<dir>` | the leftover is not bound as a property: temporary names start with `..` (Boot 4.1.2 configtree reads single-dot names but skips `..` names; a characterization test pins this) | main review of 00f8324 | no |
| SEC-016 | CLI | **changed by PCS-030: the ACL has two entries, the owner (everything) and LocalService (read), not the owner alone.** a default file system with ACLs and without POSIX permissions (the test is skipped on any other file system: the capability decides, never the name of the operating system, ARC-033); no secrets directory | `secret-set <name>` (store and command) | the directory and the file are created with an ACL whose every entry is an ALLOW entry of their owner (the current user), holding read, write and delete rights, nothing inherited from the parent remains (an ACL that was merged with inherited entries is replaced by the owner-only one before a byte is written); the content is the exact UTF-8 bytes; the output has the name and path and never the value | user decision: install on Windows too | no |
| SEC-017 | CLI | **changed by PCS-030: the same two entries.** as SEC-016, the same name again | store | replaced through a new file and an atomic move; the new file has the owner-only ACL; no temporary file is left | SEC-002 for ACLs | no |
| SEC-018 | CLI | as SEC-016, an existing directory whose ACL also allows another account (SYSTEM) | `check`, `store` | **changed by SFR-010**: used as it is, `check` and `store` pass and the ACL of the directory is not narrowed; the stored file is a new owner-only file. `LOOSE_PERMISSIONS` stays only for what the store has just created and cannot make owner-only (its message says so) | SEC-006 for ACLs; section 42 | no |
| SEC-019 | CLI | **changed by PCS-032: the replacement has the owner and service entries.** as SEC-016, an existing secret file whose ACL also allows another account | `load`, `store` | **changed by SFR-010**: read and replaced; the replacement is owner-only | SEC-006 for ACLs; section 42 | no |
| SEC-020 | CLI | as SEC-016, a directory owned by an account other than the current process user | `store` | **removed by SFR-010**: the owner of an existing path is not looked at, so there is nothing to refuse; the case cannot be written with a stub account on a file system with ACLs (the store takes ownership of what it creates for the account it is given, SEC-016). The rule is shown by the absence of the check and by SEC-013 (POSIX stub) | SEC-013 for ACLs; section 42 | no |
| SEC-021 | CLI | the default file system and a zip file system | choose the protection | POSIX permissions when the file system supports them, otherwise ACL, otherwise none; the choice is by the attribute views of the file system | ARC-033 | no |
| SEC-022 | CLI | a secret directory | `exists(<name>)` of the store (used by `token-generate`) | false before, true after the secret is stored; it refuses like `check` (a missing parent is `PARENT_MISSING`, a wrong owner or wider access is refused and never reported as present) | install: keep an existing token | no |

## 13. HK: client hooks (one executable jar, three clients)

Clients: Claude, Codex, Antigravity. One module `ledger-client-hook` builds one executable jar (a Spring Boot application without a web server, since the user decision of 2026-11-15; Jackson inside); the first argument is the client enum `claude|codex|antigravity`; it is launched as `java -jar ledger-client-hook.jar <client>`, reads host JSON on stdin and writes the host's JSON on stdout, stateless. Vendor prefixes: `claude`, `codex`, `gemini`. Client manifests/templates call the same jar with their own argument. (Decision 7m replaces the earlier single-file-source idea; no per-client source files exist.)
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HK-001 | HOOK | each client; each of the 10 write tool names in the client's naming (`mcp__<server>__ledger_<op>`; Antigravity `mcp:<server>:ledger_<op>`) with input containing nested data, Korean text, `__proto__` key, `author_id` | run | output adds `_hostAttestation` = a signed token (ATT-001) whose claims are author `<vendor>:<host id>`, tool `ledger_<op>`, the `operationKey` of the call and the issue time of the injected clock, and whose signature verifies with the public key printed for that author; preserves every other field unchanged (deep equal), with no exception (changed by opkey: the hook no longer adds a `request_id`, HK-023, OPK-009); Claude: `updatedInput` only and no auto-approval decision; Codex/Antigravity: allow decision, no permission override | claude-hook:CH-01; host-attestation:S01 | no |
| HK-002 | HOOK | no `author_id` argument | run | none is created (changed by opkey: the tools have no `author_id` argument) | claude-hook:CH-02; S12 | no |
| HK-003 | HOOK | an `author_id` argument that the model sent | run | (changed by opkey) it is left in the arguments as the model sent it and never copied into `_hostAttestation`; the hook does not judge it, the server refuses it as an unknown argument (OPK-006) | S12 | no |
| HK-004 | HOOK | own `_hostAttestation` in the model input with values null, `""`, false, 0, `{}`, `[]`, the expected value, another id | run | explicit deny mentioning "reserved"; no overwrite/updatedInput | CH-03; S02 | no |
| HK-005 | HOOK | host id missing, null, `""`, number, object, 129 chars, whitespace, `other:id`, `bad:id`, `x\n`, `x\r\n`, Korean | run | deny, no fallback to agent/child id | CH-04; S03 | no |
| HK-006 | HOOK | ids `x`, `a._-Z9`, 128 chars | run | accepted and used as is | CH-07; S04 | no |
| HK-007 | HOOK | read tool names (`ledger_get`, `ledger_history`, `ledger_search`, `ledger_defects`...), `ledger_save_extra`, `run_command`, `ledger_save` without MCP prefix, other server tools | run | no output (Claude: `{}`), input untouched; the new read tools are never stamped | CH-05; S05 | no |
| HK-008 | HOOK | malformed JSON; empty stdin; non-object arguments (null, `[]`, text, 7) | run | Claude: exit 2 on malformed JSON; Codex/Antigravity: explicit deny and exit 0; empty stdin no output | CH-06; S06 | no |
| HK-009 | HOOK | invocations with ids A, B, A | run | each uses the supplied id; no state between runs | S07 | no |
| HK-010 | HOOK | client manifests/templates (`hooks.json`, `plugin.json`) | read | (changed by attest-fix, F2) matcher regex is the structural shape `mcp__<server>__[<prefix>_]ledger_<op>` with the ten known operations (Claude, Codex) and `mcp:[<server><sep>]...ledger_<op>` / `call_mcp_tool` (Antigravity), for any server name incl. isolated sink names, and none of the read tools or of a name that does not end in `ledger_<known op>`; the exact rule (prefix equal to the server segment) is the Java code of HK-032, so the matcher is a superset of what is signed; every command is `java -jar <path>/ledger-client-hook.jar <claude\|codex\|antigravity>` with the right argument and the path exists after install; plugin names | S08; S10; S13 (product part) | no |
| HK-011 | HOOK | Antigravity MCP name candidates `mcp:test:ledger_save`, `mcp:test/ledger_save`, `mcp:test_ledger_save`, `mcp:ledger_ledger_save`, `mcp:test:test_ledger_save` | run | all stamped, for the server `test` / `ledger` (changed by attest-fix, F2: `mcp:ledger_save` names no server, so there is no key to sign with: it passes untouched like any other name) | S09; F2 | no |
| HK-012 | HOOK | Antigravity `call_mcp_tool` envelope with `Arguments` as object and as JSON string, extra keys | run | only `Arguments` overwritten, original type kept, envelope untouched; invalid `Arguments` (null, `[]`, 7, `{`, `"text"`) deny; non-ledger ToolName passes; (changed by attest-fix, F2) the ToolName must be `ledger_<op>` or `<ServerName>_ledger_<op>`, and the ServerName (1 to 100 characters of `A-Za-z0-9._-`) is the server of the key; `app_ledger_ledger_<op>` under another ServerName passes untouched | S11; F2 | no |
| HK-013 | HOOK | Codex namespace `mcp__codex_apps__app_ledger_ledger_<op>` | run | all 10 handled, as before; (changed by attest-fix, F2: the audience is the app, so the key is the one of the server identity `codex_apps.<app>`, here `codex_apps.ledger`, and the owner registers the key printed by `session-key codex_apps.ledger <author>`); `mcp__test__ledger_<op>` and `mcp__test__test_ledger_<op>` are handled too; read tool names, an unknown operation (`app_ledger_ledger_save_extra`), `app_ledger_save`, another pseudo server (`mcp__other__app_ledger_ledger_save`) and a malformed app segment pass untouched; a look-alike app `app_evil_ledger_<op>` is signed for under `codex_apps.evil` with its own key, which never verifies under the key of `codex_apps.ledger` | S10; F2 | no |
| HK-014 | HOOK | JSON with numbers `12345678901234567890` (changed by audit2 N2 (RFC 7493 section 2.2): an integer whose magnitude is beyond 2**53-1 has no exact canonical form, so a call with `12345678901234567890`, `9007199254740992`, `9223372036854775807` or any other such integer is denied `ARGUMENTS_NOT_SIGNABLE`, nothing is signed and no key is created, HK-038; `9007199254740991`, `-9007199254740991`, decimals such as `0.10` and `12345678901234567890.0` and `1e300` are still signed and passed on as sent: RFC 8785 canonicalises a decimal by its double and the server reads it as a double too; the "big integers keep their exact value" expectation of this row no longer holds for integers beyond the range), `1e300` (changed by attest-fix (RFC 8785 §3.2.2.3 and §3.2.2.2): every other number is signed by its double and passed on as the original text, but `1e999` overflows a double and a lone surrogate escape is invalid Unicode, so a call with either is denied `ARGUMENTS_NOT_SIGNABLE` and nothing is signed, HK-038; the lone-surrogate pass-through of the old row no longer holds), `-0`, `0.10`, escapes, surrogate pairs, deep nesting (100 levels), duplicate keys, 1 MB body | run | Jackson-based round trip: numbers keep their exact value (big integers and decimals are not turned into doubles), strings and unicode preserved, unknown fields passed through untouched; no crash; within the hook time budget | new (Jackson fidelity in the hook jar) | no |
| HK-015 | HOOK | JVM default charset and locale changed (`-Dfile.encoding=windows-949`, `LC_ALL=C`) | run with Korean input | output bytes are UTF-8 and identical | new | no |
| HK-016 | HOOK | stdout/stderr | run all above | stdout only JSON, stderr empty, exit 0 (except Claude malformed JSON) | S: `stderr === ''` | no |
| HK-017 | HOOK | cold start of `java -jar` | run | completes inside the host hook time limit (5 s on the test host); budget recorded | host-attestation spawn timeout 5000 | no |
| HK-018 | HOOK | hook jar | inspect contents | (changed by the user decision of 2026-11-15, the hook becomes a Spring Boot application: the jar is the executable jar of the Spring Boot plugin, no longer one jar with the dependencies merged in) the module's classes in `BOOT-INF/classes`, the launcher of Spring Boot, and nested jars in `BOOT-INF/lib` of nothing else than the classes of `org/pgledger/attestation/` (the shared token format, JDK only), Jackson (core, annotations, databind), Spring Boot with the Spring Framework libraries it needs, and its logging (SLF4J, Logback); no JDBC driver or pool, no data access, no web server or web framework, no signature or module descriptor, no secret file; the only configuration files are `application.properties` and `logback-spring.xml` of the module; the licence and notice texts of the dependencies are kept under `META-INF/third-party/<jar>/`; hook code uses no network, process, reflection or JDBC API; file system APIs are used only by the classes of package `org.pgledger.hook.keys` (the key store); `System` is used only by `Main` (the exit code) and `ProcessChannels` (stdin, stdout, stderr), and no class calls `System.getenv` or `System.getProperty`: the environment variable `LEDGER_HOOK_KEY_DIR` and the properties `user.home` and `user.name` are bound by Boot into the record `KeyStoreProperties` (HK-051; changed by the Spring decision of 2026-11-15; changed by D-0012: the hook now keeps keys; changed by attest-fix, F8: the key file is read only through one channel opened with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, never with `Files.readAllBytes`); no hook class uses a web, data access or transaction API of Spring | new | no |
| HK-020 | HOOK | first argument `claude`, `codex`, `antigravity`; `Claude`, `gemini`, empty, missing, two arguments (`session-key` takes exactly a server and an author argument: changed by attest-fix, F2) | run | the three enum values select the matching input/output dialect; any other value or a missing argument exits 2 with the usage message `usage: java -jar ledger-client-hook.jar <claude|codex|antigravity> \| session-key <server> <author>` (changed by attest-fix, F2) on stderr and no stdout; the vendor prefix of the author in the signed token follows the enum (`claude:`, `codex:`, `gemini:`) | decision 7m; D-0012 | no |
| HK-022 | HOOK | each of the 10 tools in every naming variant of the three clients that HK-032 accepts (including `mcp__codex_apps__app_ledger_ledger_<op>`, signed for the server identity `codex_apps.ledger`) | run | the `tool` claim of the token is the canonical `ledger_<op>` whatever the host spelling | D-0012 | no |
| HK-023 | HOOK | **changed by opkey (section 44; was `request_id`, see OPK-009)**: `operationKey` absent / null / blank / text (spaces, upper case) / number / boolean / object / array; whoami and the artifact tools | run | absent, null: the claim key is "" and the arguments are handed on unchanged except `_hostAttestation` (the server then refuses the call; the hook makes no key up); text (also blank, spaces, upper case): kept and signed exactly as it is; any other type: deny `OPERATION_KEY_INVALID` (was `REQUEST_ID_INVALID`), nothing signed; whoami and the artifact tools: claim key "" and no `operationKey` is added | D-0012 | no |
| HK-024 | HOOK | key folder empty; two authors; two servers; second process | first call for an author on a server; second call; call for another author; call of the same author for another server | the first call creates exactly one key file; later calls use the same key (same public key); another author or another server gets another file and key (changed by attest-fix, F2); nothing else is written anywhere | D-0012 | no |
| HK-025 | HOOK | a file system with the POSIX attribute view (capability, not OS name) | first call | the key folder is `rwx------`, the key file `rw-------`, both from creation (extended by attest-fix, F6: where the ACL view exists, an ACL that grants the owner alone, see HK-035) | D-0012; ARC-033 | no |
| HK-026 | HOOK | POSIX view; key file widened to `rw-r-----`, or the folder to `rwxr-xr-x`; a key file that is a link | call | **changed by SFR-020 (section 42)**: the key is used as it is, the call signs, the mode is not repaired and the file is not touched (`session-key` prints the public key). The old rule denied `SESSION_KEY_UNAVAILABLE` (`TOO_OPEN`). A key file that is a link is followed (SFR-021) | D-0012; section 42 | no |

Runs against services started from the real artifacts (systemd units or `java -jar`) on the test host, PostgreSQL embedded or the host's PostgreSQL. Portal steps that need the user ("Ledger (test)" sync) are manual gates, reported as NOT_RUN until the user performs them.

## 15. ARC: ArchUnit and source scans (ARCH level)

Rules come from the plan's "기준" section and the later user decisions. Allow-lists are explicit data in the test (a changed allow-list needs review).

## 16. ANSI: static check of SQL

Scope: (a) every SQL string literal in the `repository` package, (b) every `V{n}__*.sql` file. Matching is token-based (word boundary, case-insensitive, after stripping comments and string literals), so `limit_value` or a string `'top'` is not a violation.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ANSI-001 | ANSI | repository SQL strings | scan for the plan's banned tokens | zero hits for: `LIMIT`, `ROWNUM`, `TOP`, `ILIKE`, `~`/`~*`/`!~` regex operators, `::` casts, `RETURNING`, `ON CONFLICT`, `MERGE`, `ON DUPLICATE KEY`, `FOR UPDATE`, `FOR SHARE`, `WITH RECURSIVE`, `jsonb`/`json_*`/array functions (`unnest`, `array_agg`, `string_agg`), `now()`, `gen_random_uuid()`, `pg_advisory*`, `nextval/currval/NEXT VALUE FOR`, partial-index `WHERE` on `CREATE INDEX`, `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, row-value comparison `(a,b) < (?,?)` | plan: ANSI list | no |
| ANSI-002 | ANSI | every `V{n}__*.sql` | same scan plus DDL rules | zero hits of the ANSI-001 tokens | user decision 1 | no |
| ANSI-003 | ANSI | every V file | scan | no `CREATE TRIGGER`, `CREATE PROCEDURE`, `CREATE FUNCTION`, `CREATE VIEW`, `CREATE SEQUENCE`, `IDENTITY`, `GENERATED ... AS IDENTITY`, `SERIAL`, `AUTO_INCREMENT`, `DO $$`, `CREATE EXTENSION`, `CREATE ROLE`; only whitelisted statement types: `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, `ALTER TABLE` (ANSI-015), `INSERT` (seeds), `UPDATE` (rows of a seed that a later V file changes), `GRANT` | plan; user decision 1 | no |
| ANSI-004 | ANSI | PostgreSQL after `migrate` | query each catalog (`pg_trigger`/`pg_proc` for the schema; `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TRIGGERS`/`ROUTINES`) | zero triggers, zero routines, zero sequences owned by the schema | plan: completion criterion | yes |
| ANSI-005 | ANSI | every identifier in V files | check | lowercase, at most 30 characters, no quoted identifiers, no reserved words (`condition`, `release`, `user`, `type` renamed or checked against a reserved-word list) | AGS decision (identifiers lowercase, 30 chars) | no |
| ANSI-006 | ANSI | column types in V files | check | only from the allowed set (`VARCHAR(n)`, the long text type that the placeholder `${long_text}` stands for (decided by the dialect of `SqlBuilder`, RUN-001 .. RUN-004; the files never write a type name for it), `INTEGER`, `BIGINT`, `TIMESTAMP`, `DECIMAL`); no BOOLEAN/BIT, no `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE`, no `UUID`, `JSON(B)`, `TEXT`, `SERIAL` | user decisions 4,5 | no |
| ANSI-007 | ANSI | UNIQUE / PRIMARY KEY definitions | check | every column of a UNIQUE constraint is `NOT NULL` (normalised key columns `active_key`, `link_key`); no partial or expression index | plan: unique rule | no |
| ANSI-008 | ANSI | CHECK constraints in V files | check | no function calls, no regex; only IN-lists, comparisons, NULL-ness (matches REPO-004) | plan | no |
| ANSI-009 | ANSI | dynamic SQL builders in repositories | scan and run | values are always bind parameters; no string concatenation of caller input into SQL (also the injection corpus of REPO-027 and MCR-S06) | security | yes |
| ANSI-010 | ANSI | the checker itself | feed a corpus: each banned token alone, inside a comment, inside a string literal, as a substring of an identifier, in lower/upper/mixed case, split over lines | exactly the real uses are reported (mutation proof: removing a rule makes a seeded violation test fail; clean SQL has no false positive) | test-engineering | no |
| ANSI-011 | ANSI | candidate additional bans (proposed for ratification, not in the plan): `FETCH FIRST`, `OFFSET`, `\|\|` concatenation, `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`/`SYSDATE`/`GETDATE`, `LIKE` without `ESCAPE`, `LENGTH()` | run | reported as warnings listing hits; promoted to failures only after ratification | plan: time from `Clock` | no |
| ANSI-012 | ANSI | V files | check | no session/vendor settings (`SET ...`, `ALTER SESSION`, `PRAGMA`), no `COMMENT ON` | plan | no |
| ANSI-013 | ANSI | V files | run on PostgreSQL | the same text executes without a branch (no `dbms:`-style conditional, no per-DB file variant) | plan: no `dbms:` branch | yes |
| ANSI-014 | ANSI | every V file and every repository SQL string | scan | no `DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, `DEFAULT LOCALTIMESTAMP`, `DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE`, `DEFAULT NOW()`, `SYSDATE`, `GETDATE`, or any other DB-side time default or time function in SQL (`CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, `LOCALTIMESTAMP`, `CURRENT_DATE`, `CURRENT_TIME`); catalogue check: no timestamp column has a column default; every time value is bound from the application UTC `Clock` | user decision: times from app UTC Clock | yes |
| ANSI-015 | ANSI | every V file with an `ALTER TABLE` (V4 `relation_direction`) | check each statement | only `ADD COLUMN` of a plain type (ANSI-006), `ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK (...)` without function call or regex (ANSI-008) and `DROP CONSTRAINT`; table and column or constraint names lower case, at most 30 characters, not reserved (ANSI-005) | D-0016 | no |

## 17. MIG: the 1.x import (`ledger-migrate-1x`, command `import-1x`, formerly `ledger-cli import-legacy`)

The rows of this section now run in the module `ledger-migrate-1x` (section 43): `import-legacy` reads `import-1x`, and the 1.x vocabulary of the rows (`LEGACY_*`, `legacy_ref`, `REQUEST_ID_LEGACY`, request receipts) reads as decisions 4 and 5 of section 43 say. The IDs are unchanged.

Source = the old PostgreSQL schema `ledger` as left by the old installer at `9fb62fe` (built in tests from the old `sql/*.sql` copied into the test resources, plus the data written by the fixtures below). Target = new schema; source and target may be the same PostgreSQL database (a schema each) or different databases. The source connection is read-only.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MIG-001 | MIGRATION | rich source (project, task, defect incl. merged and UNRATED, defect_fix, defect_case, defect_task, defect_relation, condition, condition_link, session_grant incl. revoked/expired, history incl. IMPORT/LEGACY ops, request_receipt, common_code) | import | row count per table equals source; no extra rows; command prints counts and exit 0 | plan: 표별 행 수 | yes |
| MIG-002 | MIGRATION | same | per-table digest | SHA-256 over canonical row serialisation ordered by key (all columns, instants as UTC `Z`, empty-string rule of SVC-NORM applied identically on both sides and documented) equal source vs target; ids of history, fix, case, grant preserved (changed by import: the digest is the SHA-256 over the lines `<canonical key> TAB <SHA-256 of the canonical row> LF` ordered by the canonical key of the new table as a plain string comparison, so no collation of either DBMS takes part; the canonical row is `n;`, `s<length>:<text>;`, `l<number>;`, `t<UTC time, six fractional digits, Z>;` per column in the column order of the new table; the legacy side is the legacy row as mapped to the new table's shape (empty text as NULL, tokens in upper case, derived columns), the new side is the table read back; a test recomputes the digest of one table from this text alone) | plan: 요약값 | yes |
| MIG-003 | MIGRATION | **changed by M1X (section 43)**: runs in `ledger-migrate-1x` (`import-1x`), the vocabulary is that of M1X-050: history rows with `changes`, `source_ref` (the 1.x `legacy_ref`), `IMPORT`/`IMPORTED_NOTE`/`IMPORTED_CHANGE` (the 1.x `LEGACY_NOTE`/`LEGACY_CHANGE`), `actor_assurance` `IMPORTED_*` (the 1.x `LEGACY_*`), `login`, `operation_key` (changed by opkey: the 1.x column is `request_id`, the tool copies it into `operation_key`, OPK-018), `corrects_id` | import | every field equal (JSON equality of `changes`); `corrects_id` references intact; **`before_data` is NULL on every imported row** (never fabricated) | user decision 3; plan | yes |

| MIG-005 | MIGRATION | live, revoked and expired grants | import, then whoami and writes | (changed by import: grants are imported without a session key, so none is usable until the session's key is registered; the key-less write is MIG-030) the grants arrive with `active_key` set (`session\|project\|scope` for live and expired-but-unrevoked, `revoked:<id>` for revoked), `grant_scope` in upper case, `last_used_at` NULL; counts equal; `session_key` stays empty; once the key is registered whoami lists the live grants only (revoked and expired are not listed) | plan | yes |

## 18. Reconciliation: every old ID -> new ID or dropped

Disposition: **kept** = same behaviour, new mechanism; **rewritten: reason** = the old case asserted a PostgreSQL/DBHub/Node mechanism and is restated as the behaviour the user relies on; **dropped: reason**; **UNCLASSIFIED** = the plan and decisions do not say what happens (listed in 18.5, no guess made).

### 18.5 Open items after the second-round decisions
Items 1 to 13 of the former unclassified list were decided by the main agent (decision 7 in section 1.4) and are now concrete cases: setup-domain/nginx/cloudflared/SSL bundle (E2E-016..018), one-click wrapper (E2E-019..021), PostgreSQL dump before migrate (E2E-022), MCP health (ADM-045, ADM-046), CSRF (ADM-013, ADM-014; custom `Sec-Fetch-Site`/`Origin` checks dropped), FAILED schema file retry (SCH-005, SCH-020, SCH-021), driver registration yaml and setup mode (DRV-009, DRV-012, DRV-020..023, ADM-042), `match` ANY/ALL (MCR-S02), LEGACY receipts (MIG-004, SVC-RCP-15, REPO-041), legacy `''` (MIG-023, SVC-NORM-02), `category-add` dropped (CLI-006), three behaviours "unchanged by design" (DOM-019, SVC-FIX-16, SVC-COR-05), plan fixes (REPO-015, REPO-039, REPO-040, SVC-RACE-01, SVC-RACE-09, DOM-036, REPO-032, hook jar HK-001..HK-021, ARC-034).
Still unclassified or provisional (not decided, not guessed):
1. Generation of the Origin TLS certificate (the old script created a 10-year ECDSA certificate): decision 7a covers the SSL bundle configuration only; E2E-018 uses a test certificate and says nothing about who creates it in production.
2. Legacy entity ids (decided by REH3, section 27: the ids of 1.x are kept; only ids that differ in case alone, or have a space or a control character, or are longer than 120 characters, stop the import).
3. Names and keys marked `(P)` (read-tool input keys, `match`, error tokens of the registry) are provisional literals, not open behaviour.

## 19. DOC: documentation (README.md, README.ko.md, release note format, release notes)

Level DOCS. Class `org.pgledger.cli.docs.DocsTest` in `ledger-cli` (it sees `ToolContract`, `CliCommand` and its usage bundle; the root of the repository is the system property `ledger.docs.root` that the test task sets). Every list that has to mirror the code is derived from the code in the test (tools from `ToolContract`, commands and their syntax from `CliCommand` and the `usage.*` texts, modules from `settings.gradle.kts`, client directories from `clients/`, configuration keys from the `@ConfigurationProperties` declarations); nothing of that is copied. One case = one invocation of a parameterized test; "both" = once per language (EN: `README.md`, KO: `README.ko.md`, and the English and Korean part of a file that holds both). The tests are written before the documents; against the README of the integration head 61f2a91 (the DBHub text, no Korean README, no format file, no release note) they must fail.
Writing rules checked here (project documentation rules): the main README is English and links the Korean one; the Korean one links back; release notes and their format are one file each with both languages; a release note follows the format; formal wording (not testable, reviewed).
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DOC-001 | DOCS | `README.md`, `README.ko.md` | read the first 12 lines of each | each exists; the English one contains a link to `README.ko.md`, the Korean one a link to `README.md` | project rule: English first, Korean linked | no |
| DOC-002 | DOCS | both READMEs, `ToolContract.all()` | collect the first cells of every table whose first header is `Tool` (KO `도구`) | exactly the 27 names of the contract (10 write tools, 5 attachment tools, 12 read tools): none missing, none extra (so a removed tool in a table fails) | plan: tools; brief | no |
| DOC-003 | DOCS | both READMEs, the removed tools `execute_sql_reader`, `search_objects_reader` | every line that contains the name | there is at least one such line (the migration text names the replacement), none starts a table row, each contains the word `removed` (KO `제거`) | brief: no removed tool as available | no |

## 20. INS: installer (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, install stage)

Decisions this section applies (user, verbatim where quoted): the installers are the one non-Java exception, a thin `deploy/install.sh` (「아니다 2로 하자.」) and a thin `deploy/install.cmd` (「윈도우에도 깔수있잖아? 그러니 install.cmd 도 만들어줘.」), and everything else is `java -jar ledger-cli.jar <command>`; the product SQL is ANSI only, so creating the database accounts (a DBMS-specific statement) is an operator step with the DBMS's own tools before the installation, and the product applies only ANSI `GRANT`s (「쿼리는 ansi sql 방식으로 짜고, 특정 dbms 기능은 사용 금지야. 트리거도 사용 금지야.」); no Cloudflare dependency (「클라우드플레어에 종속이 안되었으면 좋겠어.」); macOS is not a target (「맥은 제외해도 돼」).
Design after 「애초에 개인피시에 깔면 서비스에 등록하는것만으로도 충분해. 그리고 os 권한분리? 대체 왜?」 and 「응 그렇게 하나로 줄여」: **one operating system service account** runs the MCP server and the admin web (Linux: the system user `pg-ledger-mcp`, never root; Windows: the predefined account LocalService), so that the secrets of the ledger (the admin token, the database password) are readable by it alone and not by the account of the person or the agents; **two database accounts**: the application account (both servers and the owner commands of `ledger-cli`, the least privilege of `db-templates/dml-grants.sql`: no DDL, no `UPDATE` or `DELETE` on the history) and the DDL account (`migrate`, `import-legacy`, the grants; its password is typed, hidden, and never stored).
Targets (user: 「개인 / 윈도우서버 / 리눅스서버」), told apart by render options of the Java tool and not by branches of the scripts: a **personal PC** is `install.cmd` with the defaults (loopback only, no reverse proxy, no TLS); a **Windows server** is `install.cmd` with public host names, behind a reverse proxy that the operator has prepared; a **Linux server** is `install.sh` (systemd) with the optional nginx site. A Linux desktop that is a personal PC follows the Linux procedure.
Tests run on the test PostgreSQL (1.4a): the owner account cannot create roles, so the application account is the login of `ledger.test.app-user`, granted inside the schema of the test. The tests of the rendering and the scripts need no root, no Linux and no Windows.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INS-007 | REPO | the file `db-templates/dml-grants.sql` | read as text | only `GRANT` statements of the rights `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` on tables to `${dml_account}`: no `ALL`, `PUBLIC`, `GRANT OPTION`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, `TRIGGER`; `${dml_account}` is the only placeholder | ANSI-001, user decision: ANSI only | no |

## 20a. PRN: development principles of the admin web and the MCP server (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit W5)
Source of the cases: plan 2 (`ags-3446-solid-plan.md`), item F3 (admin) and the admin and MCP items of 2.1 to 2.8 and section 3, and the input records of `ledger_save` / `ledger_batch` that unit W3 deferred to this unit. Behaviour that is not listed here is kept as it was and stays covered by the cases of sections 7 to 10 (their tests run against the refactored code unchanged in meaning). The pinned digests of SVC-DIG-01 were computed on the code before the change (commit of the unit's first run) and must stay equal after it.

## 21. SYS: system test of the packaged product (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit IT)

Reason (user: 「근데 어차피 mcp랑 dbms랑 연동테스트 해봐야하는거아님?」): every test of the MCP server in section 7 to 9 runs in the JVM of the test with the `embedded` profile, so the production wiring of the server (the DataSource of the registered JDBC driver jar, the schema guard, the application account) is never run against a DBMS. These cases run the **packaged** product against the real DBMS (the PostgreSQL of 1.4a) along the real install path, with the commands of the product wherever one exists. The subject under test is the built jar and its startup wiring, which a context inside the test JVM cannot show; so each product jar is started as a **separate Java process** (the Java of the running test JVM, `ProcessBuilder`, one process at a time apart from the server), the server is reached by the MCP Java SDK client over HTTP, and the writes are signed by the real hook jar as Claude Code starts it (`PreToolUse` `updatedInput`). The `embedded` profile is never activated and no test of this section starts the server in the test JVM.

Setting: module `ledger-system-test` (test code only; its `test` task needs the `bootJar` of `ledger-cli` and `ledger-mcp-server` and the hook jar, and is part of `build` and `check`). The test database is the one of 1.4b; a fresh schema name is reserved with `TestDatabase.reserveSchemaName()` (the leftover sweep of 1.4a cleans it if a run is killed) and the test drops it at its end. The instance home is a temporary directory in the Windows layout of `render-deploy` (`--platform=windows --home=<temp>`), so the cases run where drive paths exist; on a host without them the class is skipped and says so (a gap, see below). Connections: the PostgreSQL limit is 10 per account; the server pool is 2 (`--ledger.jdbc.pool-size=2`), a command line tool has a pool of 2 and runs alone, the test JVM holds one owner connection at a time and does not use the shared pool of `TestDatabase`. Secrets: the two passwords are read only through `TestDatabaseSettings`; a password reaches a child process only as the environment variable of that child (`LEDGER_JDBC_PASSWORD`, the relaxed binding of `ledger.jdbc.password`) or, for the owner account of `migrate`, on the standard input of that process (`--stdin`, the product's own way, SEC-025); never on a command line, in a file the test writes, in a log or in a message (SYS-012).

Install steps that are **not** run here and why: `prepare-host` and `seal-host` (they close the home to SYSTEM and Administrators and hand the secrets to LocalService: an elevated prompt is needed, INS-044, INS-045, INS-038); the WinSW executables and `sc.exe` (the services are not registered; the server is started with the Java arguments that the rendered WinSW configuration holds, so the settings, the configuration import and the secret directory of the service are what runs); `deploy/install.cmd` itself (INS-038); `secret-set` of the database password (the rule of this section is that no password is written to a file; the secret directory is exercised by the token of `token-generate`, which the server and `mcp-check` read from it); the admin web (not part of this unit).

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SYS-001 | SYSTEM | an empty temporary instance home, the JDBC URL and application account of the test database, the reserved schema, a free loopback port | `java -jar ledger-cli.jar render-deploy --platform=windows --home=<home> --java=<java> --jdbc-url=... --app-account=<app> --schema=<schema> --mcp-port=<port>` as a process | exit 0; `<home>/etc/application.yaml` holds the URL, the application account and the schema, `<home>/etc/mcp.yaml` the port, `<home>/lib/pg-ledger-mcp.xml` the Java arguments of the MCP service (`-jar`, the `spring.config.import` of the four sources); no rendered file holds a password | brief: the install path starts with the product's own render | PG |
| SYS-002 | SYSTEM | the rendered home | `token-generate ledger.mcp.token --ledger.secrets.home=<home>/etc` as a process | exit 0; the token is a file of `<home>/etc/secrets` at least 16 characters long, it is not in the output, and a second run keeps it | brief: `token-generate` | no |
| SYS-003 | SYSTEM | the PostgreSQL driver jar of the build, copied into `<home>/etc/drivers` (the one directory a jar is taken from; `prepare-host` does that copy on a host) | `driver-register --jar=<copy> --class=org.postgresql.Driver` as a process | exit 0; `<home>/etc/jdbc-driver.yaml` names the class and the SHA-256 of the jar, which equals the SHA-256 the test computes | brief: registered JDBC driver jar | no |
| SYS-004 | SYSTEM | the registered driver, the owner account, the schema that does not exist yet | `migrate --owner-account=<owner> --stdin --app-account=<app>` as a process, the owner password on its standard input | exit 0; with the owner connection of the test: the schema exists, all tables of `V1`..`V3` exist, `schema_history` has one SUCCESS row per file with the SHA-256 of the file as the product stores it, the grants of `dml-grants.sql` are applied to the application account (it holds INSERT on `history` and no UPDATE or DELETE); then the step of the DBMS that the template leaves to the operator, `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA` to the application account, is run by the test with the owner connection | brief: migrate as the DDL account with grants | PG |

| SYS-011 | SYSTEM | the end of the run, also when an earlier case failed | stop the server (destroy, wait, forcibly when it does not end); drop the reserved schema and the second name with the owner connection | the server process has ended; neither schema exists; the connection of the test is closed | brief: no process and no schema is left | PG |
| SYS-012 | SYSTEM | the end of the run | scan every file below the instance home (the settings, the secret directory, the registration, the rendered services) and every captured output of the processes, as bytes, for each of the two passwords | no file and no output holds either password: a password was only in an environment variable or on a standard input | brief: secrets never on a command line, in a file, in a log | no |

Gaps of this section (stated in the report of the unit): the database password is not read from the secret directory by the server (SYS-006 uses the environment, the secret directory is exercised by the token); the services are not run by WinSW or as LocalService (INS-038); the admin web is not started; `migrate` of an already up-to-date schema by the application account is a no-op that exits 0 (it needs no right), so SYS-009 uses a schema that does not exist; the class runs only where `render-deploy --platform=windows` can write a drive path (it is skipped elsewhere).

## 23. SET: first setup, the seed bundle and the install defaults (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit SETUP)

Written before the code of the unit. Decisions this section applies (user, verbatim): 「dbms 설치가 전제라니까?」, 「h2는 초기 셋팅값이나 뭐 이것저것 담아두는 용도로만 쓰고 dbms에 마이그레이션하는 목적으로만 써.」, 「1. 장부 mcp를 깃헙에서 처음 다운받은 사람은 그럼 초기셋팅을 어떻게 해줄건데? 2. 마이그레이션은 마이그레이션 툴을 제공해야하지 않겠어? 근데 마이그레이션도 기준이 필요할거잖아. h2는 그런목적으로만 쓰라고.」, 「쿼리는 ansi sql 방식으로 짜고, 특정 dbms 기능은 사용 금지야. 트리거도 사용 금지야.」, 「java로 할수있는걸 외부프로그램으로 굳이 하지 말란소리야.」, 「근데 나만사용하겠냐고. github에 공개리포지로 올라간다는게 무슨의민지는 알기나해?」 (the product is designed for unknown users: no provider name and nothing of the maintainer's own hosting in the code, the defaults or the texts).

Design: the initial data lives in text files of `ledger-cli` (`src/seed/defect-categories.tsv`, `src/seed/install-defaults.properties`). The build runs a Java program (module `ledger-seed`, `SeedBuilder`) that writes them into the H2 file `ledger-seed.mv.db` in the build output; the file is packed with `ledger-cli` and is never committed. H2 is the format of this bundle and nothing else: it is on the runtime class path of `ledger-cli` only (and of `ledger-seed`, which `ledger-cli` uses); the ledger database is always a DBMS the operator installs. `ledger-cli setup` migrates the DBMS (the existing `migrate` path), checks that the application account can use the schema, adds the seed codes that are missing through `CommonCodeService` (the service of `code-set` and of the admin web: `code_revision` and the owner's audit trail stay), and compares the seed with the database by counts and by a SHA-256 digest. The privilege that lets an account use a schema is a statement of the DBMS and stays out of the product SQL: `setup` only says in a generic sentence which step the operator has to do, and ends with exit code 3 (a step of the operator is missing). Severity and relation kinds stay in `V3` (product rules depend on them). The tests of the cli run on the test PostgreSQL (1.4a) in a schema of their own; the application account is `ledger.test.app-user`.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SET-001 | UNIT | the text source `ledger-cli/src/seed/defect-categories.tsv` | parse | exactly the 14 codes of `DEFECT_CATEGORY` (ARC, CON, DAT, DENY, DIAG, DOC, EXC, GATE, HOST, PKG, RES, SEC, TST, VND), each with a Korean and an English name and description, every code of the format of `CodeValue`, every text inside the limits of `TextRule` (name 200, description 500), no code twice | plan 3, SETUP decision | no |
| SET-002 | SEED | the bundle that the build made (`SeedBundle.packaged()` on the class path of the tests) | read the codes | the same 14 codes and texts as the text source, state `ACTIVE`, sort order ascending in the order of the file (10, 20, ...), group `DEFECT_CATEGORY` (a group of `CodeGroup`); the digest of the bundle is the digest computed from the text source | plan 3 | no |
| SET-003 | SEED | the bundle | read the install defaults | `instance-name` `pg-ledger-mcp`, `mcp-port` 8765, `admin-port` 8865, and no other name: the set of names is closed | user: defaults in one place | no |
| SET-004 | SEED | the bundle and the text sources | scan every name and value (and the bytes of the bundle file) for a secret | no password, token, key or secret word in any name; no value looks like a credential (no `user:password@`, no long random string); no provider, cloud or host name of an operator's own environment in any text | brief: no secrets, public product | no |
| SET-005 | SEED | `SeedBuilder` with a temporary directory of text files | build; read back with `SeedBundle`; build with a bad file (a row with five columns, a duplicate code, an empty name, a name of the defaults with a space, a missing file) | the read-back equals the input; a bad file stops the build with a message naming the file and the line, and writes no bundle | plan 3 | no |
| SET-006 | ARCH | the repository tree | list the tracked sources | no `*.mv.db` file outside a `build` directory: the binary is generated, never committed; `ledger-cli` packs the generated file (`processResources` takes it from the task that makes it) | brief: the binary is never committed | no |

| SET-008 | INSTALL | **changed by PCS (PCS-002): the targets are Linux, a personal PC and a Windows server without the nginx site; the golden digests are taken again for the files that remain.** the parameters of the three targets (Linux with the nginx site, a personal PC, a Windows server), the default name and the default ports | render, once with the options left out and once with the values written out (`--name=pg-ledger-mcp --mcp-port=8765 --admin-port=8865`) | the two outputs are byte-identical; both equal the digest taken from the renderer before the unit (the golden digests of the test): the defaults moved to the bundle, the rendered files did not change | brief: byte-identical | no |

| SET-010 | CLI | a migrated schema without codes, the application account let into the schema (the test does what the operator does), the owner account as the connection of the run | `setup --app-account=<app>` | exit 0; the schema is current, the grants are applied (`migrate` path), the 14 codes are in `DEFECT_CATEGORY` with the texts of the seed, `code_revision` has moved, the owner's audit trail has one `CODE_SET` row per code recorded as `cli:<process user>`; the output reports the counts (14 in the seed, 14 present, 14 equal, 0 changed) and the equal digests | brief: through the existing code-set service path | PG |
