## 24. TLS: automatic certificates (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit TLS)

**Removed by PCS (section 45, unit pcscope; user, verbatim: 「nginx 사이트 생성, ACME(인증서 발급), 갱신 타이머를 없앤다」 and 「PC·서버 둘 다, 공개는 운영자」): every case of this section (TLS-001 .. TLS-070) is dropped; the code, the commands `tls-issue` and `tls-renew`, the nginx site, the renewal unit and timer and the acme4j dependency do not exist any more. The rows stay below as the record of what existed; none is run.**

Written before the code of the unit. Decisions this section applies (user, verbatim): 「자동 인증서 추가」, 「클라우드 플레어가 쓰기 쉬워서 썻던건데 거의 그 편의성을 구현해줄수있으면 난 안써도 되지.」, 「근데 나만사용하겠냐고. github에 공개리포지로 올라간다는게 무슨의민지는 알기나해?」, 「java로 할수있는걸 외부프로그램으로 굳이 하지 말란소리야.」 (the product is for unknown users: standard ACME only, no provider-specific code and no DNS or CDN vendor API in the code, the defaults, the units or the texts; no certbot or other external program).

Design: `ledger-cli` is a standard ACME client (RFC 8555) built on the maintained Java library acme4j (Apache-2.0; it brings jose4j, Apache-2.0, and Bouncy Castle, MIT-style, for the certificate request). The keys are generated with the JDK (EC P-256). The CA directory URL is an option, the default is the production directory of Let's Encrypt. The proof is HTTP-01 only: the token file is written below a web root (`<webroot>/.well-known/acme-challenge/<token>`) that nginx serves on port 80. `tls-issue` orders a certificate for the two public host names; `tls-renew` orders a new one when the certificate expires within a threshold (default 30 days) or does not cover exactly the two names, and otherwise does nothing and says so. The result is `fullchain.pem` and `privkey.pem` (standard PEM) in the TLS directory next to `account.key`; any proxy can use them. With `render-deploy --tls-acme` the nginx site holds a port-80 server block for each own host name (challenge location, redirect to https) and the 443 blocks with the ACME PEM paths; the 443 blocks are written only once the certificate files exist (before the first issuance nginx could not load them), so the first issuance is: render (port 80 only), reload nginx, `tls-issue`, render again (now with the 443 blocks), reload. Renewal is a systemd service and timer rendered by `render-deploy --tls-acme`; the service runs as the service account and reloads nginx afterwards with `ExecStartPost=+/usr/bin/systemctl reload nginx` (one fixed command outside the sandbox, no argument from the service account, no sudo rule, no polkit rule, no capability for the renew process). The ACME flow is tested against an in-process HTTP test double of a CA (`StubCertificateAuthority`, test sources) that speaks the RFC 8555 endpoints the client uses; the real CA path is unverified until the rehearsal on the test server against the Let's Encrypt staging directory.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TLS-001 | CLI | the test double of a CA (directory, nonce, account, order, authorization, challenge, finalize, certificate), an empty TLS directory, two host names | `tls-issue --mcp-host --admin-host --acme-directory=<double> --agree-tos` | exit 0; the double saw, in this order, the directory, a new account (key given as `jwk`, terms agreed, signature, nonce and `url` of every request verified; the nonce comes from the `Replay-Nonce` of the answers), an order for exactly the two DNS identifiers, both authorizations, a trigger of the `http-01` challenge of each, the finalize with a CSR that names both hosts and holds the public key of `privkey.pem`, and the download of the certificate; `fullchain.pem` is the chain the double issued (leaf first, then the issuer) and its public key is the one of `privkey.pem`; the output names the files and the expiry | RFC 8555 | no |
| TLS-002 | CLI | as TLS-001 | the double validates a challenge | at that moment `<webroot>/.well-known/acme-challenge/<token>` exists and holds exactly the key authorization (`<token>.<base64url SHA-256 thumbprint of the account key>`, no newline); after the run no token file is left | RFC 8555 8.3 | no |
| TLS-003 | CLI | the double offers a token of `../../escape`, `a/b`, `a\b`, an empty one, a 300 character one, and one with a character outside `A-Za-z0-9_-` | `tls-issue` | exit 1 `TLS_CHALLENGE_TOKEN` for each; nothing is written outside the web root (the parent of the web root is unchanged), no certificate file is written, and the challenge is never triggered | RFC 8555 8.3 (token alphabet) | no |
| TLS-004 | CLI | no `account.key` yet, no `--agree-tos` | `tls-issue` | usage refusal (exit 2) `TLS_TERMS_NOT_AGREED` before any request to the CA (the double saw none) and before any key is made; with `account.key` present the same command goes on without the flag | brief: terms only with an explicit option | no |
| TLS-005 | CLI | `--acme-email=<address>` given, then not given; a second run with the account key of the first | `tls-issue` | the new account carries `mailto:<address>` only when the option is given; the second run uses the same account key (the double sees the same `jwk`) and creates no second key; an address with a space or a missing `@` is `INVALID_OPTION_VALUE` (exit 2) | brief: optional contact | no |
| TLS-006 | UNIT | the option reader | default, an `https://` URL, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/dir`, `http://localhost/dir`, `http://ca.example.org/dir`, `ftp://x`, `file:///x`, a URL with a space, with `%`, with a password part (`user:pw@`) | the default is exactly `https://acme-v02.api.letsencrypt.org/directory` (the one constant of the product); https and the loopback http (a CA on this host) are accepted; every other value is `INVALID_OPTION_VALUE` (exit 2) and the value is in no message | brief: CA URL an option | no |
| TLS-007 | CLI | `fullchain.pem` and `privkey.pem` of an earlier issuance (known bytes), then the double fails: an authorization that becomes `invalid`, a finalize that answers 500, a certificate download that answers 500, a nonce the double refuses twice | `tls-issue` and `tls-renew` | exit 1 `TLS_ISSUE_FAILED` naming the step, never a key or a response body; both files are byte for byte those of before, no temporary file (`..tls-*`) is left in the TLS directory, no token file is left | brief: a failed renewal leaves the old files | no |

## 25. W7: architecture rules of the development principles and the follow-ups of the plan (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit W7)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: plan 2 section 5 (`ags-3446-solid-plan.md`) and the lines marked W7 in plan 3 (`ags-3446-plan3.md`). User statements this section applies (verbatim): 「그냥 100% 자바로 구현안되어있으면 넌 뒤짐. 근데 예외란 존재하긴해야돼. 근데 그거 내가 납득이 가능해야돼.」 (every entry of an allow list carries a reason a reader accepts), 「java로 할수있는걸 외부프로그램으로 굳이 하지 말란소리야.」, 「근데 나만사용하겠냐고. github에 공개리포지로 올라간다는게 무슨의민지는 알기나해?」 (a public product for unknown users).

Design of the rules: one place per rule. The rules are the methods of `PrincipleRules` in the test fixtures of `ledger-attestation` (a module that knows no other module, so that `ledger-domain` can depend on it without a cycle); each module's `ArchitectureTest` calls them for its production classes and its main sources, with its own allow list `rule -> key -> reason`. A rule returns findings (a key and a text); a finding that is not on the allow list, an entry without a reason and an entry that no finding uses any more are all problems. The bytecode rules see what ArchUnit sees (calls that are in the class files; a call through an interface is not followed); the source rules read the main sources with comments and the content of string literals blanked, as `CodingScan` does. The rules apply to production code (main sources); tests are not held to them.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ARC-PRN-01 | ARCH | the production classes of each module | find every method annotated `@Bean` whose return type (the raw type) is a type of `org.pgledger..` | none, except the listed ones: a class of ours is a component (`@Component`, `@Service`, `@Configuration` with constructor injection) and Spring finds it; a `@Bean` method of our own type needs a reason on the allow list (a value decided from the beans that exist, a value that depends on a setting of the program that the class itself cannot know, a class of the database module built from run-time arguments, an object made by a loader of run-time classes) | plan 2 section 5, principle 31 | no |
| ARC-PRN-02 | ARCH | the production classes of each module | find every field, constructor parameter and method parameter of the raw type `Optional` (a record component is a field and a constructor parameter) | none (an `Optional` is the answer of a method, never a stored or passed value); synthetic methods (lambda bodies) are not looked at | plan 2 section 5, principle 35 | no |
| ARC-PRN-03 | ARCH | the production classes of each module | find every call of `Optional.get()` | none (`map`, `orElseGet`, `orElseThrow`, `ifPresent` instead) | plan 2 section 5, principle 35 | no |

| ARC-PRN-05 | ARCH | the production classes of each module | for every method that is `@Transactional` (or in a class that is), follow the calls to methods of our own classes that are in the class files, and find a call of a write of `java.nio.file.Files` (`write`, `writeString`, `newOutputStream`, `newBufferedWriter`, `copy`, `move`, `delete`, `deleteIfExists`, `createFile`, `createDirectory`, `createDirectories`, `createTempFile`, `createTempDirectory`, `createLink`, `createSymbolicLink`, `setAttribute`, `setPosixFilePermissions`, `setLastModifiedTime`, `setOwner`) or of the constructors of `FileOutputStream` and `FileWriter` | none, except the listed methods: a file that is written inside a transaction stays changed when the commit fails (F3); the write belongs outside the transaction or after the commit. ArchUnit attributes the calls of a lambda body to the method that holds the lambda, so a write that is registered for after the commit in a lambda of a `@Transactional` method is found too (the allow list names it with that reason); a call through an interface is not visible and is not found | plan 2 section 5, F3 | no |

## 26. REH2: findings of the second rehearsal on the test server (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit REH2)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the second rehearsal of the migration on a Linux server (PostgreSQL 16, a 1.0.0 ledger, 2.0 at `1e15f1d`). Two defects. (1) `install.sh` runs `setup`, which adds the 14 seed codes through the code service and so writes `owner_audit` rows; the README order (install, then `import-legacy`) was refused with `DUPLICATE_KEY` (`ledger data in owner_audit`), because the check of an empty schema counted every `owner_audit` row. (2) After the rollback of the README (the `<unit>.1x-backup` put back in place of the new unit) the backup still existed and equalled the restored 1.x unit; the next `prepare-host` stopped with `Could not write ... (FileAlreadyExistsException)`.

The rule of the import (REH2-MIG-01 .. 06): the import accepts a schema that holds only what `migrate` and `setup` made, which is the seed codes (`common_code`, a table that is designed to hold other rows: the code of the same key is overwritten with the legacy values) and the `owner_audit` rows of operator actions that make no ledger data (`CODE_SET`, `DRIVER_REGISTERED`). It refuses with `DUPLICATE_KEY` (CLI: `IMPORT_TARGET_NOT_EMPTY`, the message shape is unchanged: the tables that hold data are the argument) when a table of the ledger data has a row (`project`, `task`, `defect`, `defect_fix`, `defect_case`, `defect_task`, `defect_relation`, `condition`, `condition_link`, `session_grant`, `history`, `request_receipt`), when `session_key` has a row, or when an earlier import happened: `owner_audit` is counted only for the operation `IMPORT`. The other operations leave a row in a table that is counted anyway (`GRANT_ISSUED`/`GRANT_REVOKED` a grant, `KEY_*` a session key, `PROJECT_ADDED` a project), so no counted state is lost by the narrower rule.

The rule of the backup of 1.x (REH2-INS-01 .. 04): a file that this command did not render is moved to `<file>.1x-backup`; when the backup is already there and has the same bytes as the file, the file is the one the backup was made of (the rollback copied it back), so the backup stays as it is and the file is replaced; when the bytes differ, nothing is changed and the command refuses naming both files.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 27. REH3: findings of the third rehearsal on the test server (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit REH3)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the third rehearsal of the migration on a Linux server (PostgreSQL 16, the 1.x ledger of the test server, 2.0 at `3d5aee9`). `install.sh` ran (`setup` included), then `import-legacy` refused: `1263 value(s) of the legacy ledger cannot be imported as they are ... Nothing was written.` The production ledger of 1.x has the same shapes (read-only statistics: 901 tasks of which 658 have an id outside the old pattern, ids up to 109 characters with lower-case letters and the characters `#-./:_`, 245 conditions of which 219, a condition code of 79 characters, a CI reference of 533 characters, two tasks with `purpose` or `success_condition` `''`, no two ids that differ only in case). Two decisions of the second round were not the user's: (j) a legacy `''` in a NOT NULL column stops the import, and (m) entity ids are accepted only by an upper-case regex. The user's requirements are: migrate the existing ledger, never invent a value, keep the names and keys of the write tools, stay vendor-neutral ANSI SQL. Both decisions are revised (section 1.4 item 7, DOM-036, REPO-032, SVC-NORM-02, MIG-023, MIG-028 above).

Decisions of this unit:
1. **The id of a task and of a condition** is 1 to 120 characters without whitespace (any Unicode separator) and without a control or format character; it is the whole domain of 1.x, so every 1.x id imports unchanged. The id of a defect keeps its format (`<CODE>-D-<number>`). No JSON schema of a tool carries a pattern for an id (the arguments are plain strings, `ToolCatalog` derives them from the Java types), so the public contract of the tools is unchanged; the refusal reason of a malformed id is renamed from `MUST_BE_UPPER_CASE_ID_MATCHING` to `MUST_BE_ID` (argument: the longest length, 120).
2. **Case does not depend on the DBMS.** (a) A new id of a task or condition is refused when an existing id of the same kind equals it ignoring case: `DUPLICATE_KEY` (the existing code for "this id is taken"; the argument names the existing id, so the refusal says which id it collides with; a validation code would say the text is malformed, which it is not). The refusal is race-safe without a trigger or a function index: the tables `task` and `release_condition` get a column `id_key` (the id in upper case by `String.toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT)`, filled by the application, 360 characters because a case mapping can lengthen a text) with a UNIQUE constraint, so two creates that differ only in case collide on the key whatever the DBMS collation is, and the loser gets `DUPLICATE_KEY` from the insert. (b) Every lookup of a task or a condition by id compares the id exactly in Java after the fetch, so a DBMS that ignores case in `=` never returns the sibling.
3. **Empty text.** `purpose`, `success_condition`, `behavior`, `root_cause` and `condition_text` were `NOT NULL ... CHECK (length <= 4000)` in 1.x, so `''` was allowed there. The 2.0 columns are nullable; `''` (and a blank text) is stored as NULL (a DBMS of the Oracle kind cannot hold `''`), and the tools return `""` for such a field, as 1.x did. A new write follows the `save()` of 1.x: the field has to be given (absent or `null` on a create is `REQUIRED`), and may be empty.
4. **Column lengths** that 2.0 widens to hold the data of 1.x: `release_condition.code` 64 to 200, `defect_fix.ci_ref` 500 to 2000 (and the limits of the fields `code` and `ci_ref` with them). The id columns are 120 and the other measured columns (`release_name` 100 against 45, `goal_label` 200 against 114) already hold the 1.x data. `task.origin_ref` (`text` in 1.x, up to 9408 characters in production) becomes a long text column and its write limit 20000 characters (REH3-LEN-02).
5. **`link_key`** is derived by `ConditionLink.linkKey()` (the length of the condition id, the id, `T` or `D`, the target id), exactly as for a new link; the 207 rows failed only because their condition id failed the old pattern.
6. **`driver-register` before `migrate`.** When the schema is not up to date (not migrated, dropped, an upgrade pending) the registration file is written and the audit row is not (the audit table may not exist); the command says so. `migrate` and `setup` record the pending registration afterwards: when the registration file names a driver for which the owner's audit trail has no `DRIVER_REGISTERED` row of the same class, jar and digest, one row is written (actor `cli:<process user>`, detail with the time of the registration). A database that cannot be reached is still `DATABASE_FAILED`.
7. **`prepare-host`** says, when it left an existing `<unit>.1x-backup` with equal bytes as it was, that the backup was kept (a different message than the first run, which says it moved the previous file).

## 28. REH4: findings of the fourth rehearsal on the test server (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit REH4)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the fourth rehearsal of the migration of a 1.x 1.0.0 host (2.0 at `ab68ce1`): the import and the verification passed, but the clients reach the 1.x server through a public host name, `DBHUB_ALLOWED_HOSTS` of the 1.x environment file named it, and the 2.0 install rendered `allowed-hosts` of the MCP server with the two loopback names only, so every client request got HTTP 403 right after the migration. Three more findings: the 14 `Replace existing Tool` WARN lines at every start of the MCP server, the wording of the seed check after `import-legacy`, and `--mcp-host` that could not be given without `--admin-host`.

Decisions. (1) A host name that 1.x allowed is never lost silently. The installer takes `--from-1x-env=<file>` (the file it already checks and reads for the tokens) to the check phase `render-deploy --check`, where Java, run by root, reads `DBHUB_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (the MCP server) and `PGLEDGER_ADMIN_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (the admin web, 1.1.0) of the file and compares the names with `--mcp-host` and `--admin-host`; the loopback names (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `[::1]`) need no option. A name the options would drop refuses the command before anything is changed (`LEGACY_HOSTS_DROPPED`, exit 2) and names the host names (not secrets) and the option to add them to; no other value of the file is printed or kept. The rendered files stay a function of the options alone (a later run without the 1.x file renders the same files). (2) `--mcp-host` and `--admin-host` take one name or a comma list (`--mcp-host=a.example.org,b.example.org`); each is a name of the same plain form; the nginx `server_name` and `allowed-hosts` hold the same names. Either option may be given without the other; the nginx site then holds the blocks of the names that are given. (3) The MCP server registers its tools only through the catalog (the annotation scanner of Spring AI is off), so nothing is registered twice and no WARN is logged. (4) The seed check says that a code that differs from the seed was changed by the operator or was taken from 1.x by `import-legacy`; the counts and the exit codes are unchanged.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REH4-HOST-01 | CLI | a 1.x environment file with `DBHUB_ALLOWED_HOSTS=ledger-test-mcp.jaeseong.dev,localhost,127.0.0.1`, the two tokens and a database password in it; the other options of a Linux server without `--mcp-host` | **changed by M1X (section 43)**: runs in `ledger-migrate-1x`: `stop-1x --env=<file> --mcp-host=.. --admin-host=..` (here without `--mcp-host`; M1X-016, M1X-017) | **changed by M1X (section 43)**: exit 2, `HOSTS_DROPPED` (a code of the tool; was `LEGACY_HOSTS_DROPPED`) with the arguments `ledger-test-mcp.jaeseong.dev` and `mcp-host`, before any process call; no call and no file is changed; neither the output, the usage text nor the refusal holds a token or the password of the file | rehearsal 4 | no |

## 29. FUP: clean-up before the release of 2.0.0 (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit FUP)

Written before the code of the unit. User decision: 「그냥 100% 자바로 구현안되어있으면 넌 뒤짐.」 and 「java로 할수있는걸 외부프로그램으로 굳이 하지 말란소리야.」 The 1.x implementation (the shell and Node programs `bin/ledger-admin`, `bin/ledger-admin-server.mjs`, `bin/mcp-call.mjs`, the web files `web/*`, the DBHub settings `mcp/dbhub.toml`, the SQL files `sql/*.sql`, and `tests/*.mjs`, `tests/*.sh`) was kept in the tree during the development of 2.0; this unit removes it. The tag `v1.1.0` keeps the 1.x source.

Decisions. (1) Nothing of the removed files is read by Java or Gradle code except `tests/category-legacy-fixture.json`, which `LegacyFixtures` opened as `../tests/...` relative to the working directory of a test JVM; it moves to the test fixture resources of `ledger-database` and is read as a class path resource, so it no longer depends on the working directory. The other mentions of the old paths are comments and a display name; they now say that the path is the one of 1.x (tag `v1.1.0`). (2) The usage text of a refused command line (a usage error, exit 2) lists the usage line of the command that was refused, not the lines of all commands: a user who mistyped an option of `migrate` needs the syntax of `migrate`. When no command is known the whole list is the right answer, because the user then has to choose a command: `CliRunner` throws `MISSING_COMMAND` (no word) and `UNKNOWN_COMMAND` (a word that is no command) before a command exists, and these two keep the whole list. Every other usage error is thrown after the command is known (the options check and the command itself, `CliRunner.run`) and carries that command. The text comes from the same message bundle as before (`usage.<command>`), in the language of the user. (3) The client READMEs are English (`README.md`) and Korean (`README.ko.md`), the English one links the Korean one, both describe the 2.0 behaviour (one hook jar for the three hosts, the Ed25519 signature of the session key, the key registered by the owner, the refusal `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED`), and the three `plugin.json` carry the version `2.0.0`.

### 29.1 FUP-NGX: the port 80 blocks of `--tls-acme` are hardened

**Removed by PCS (section 45; user, verbatim: 「nginx 사이트 생성, ACME(인증서 발급), 갱신 타이머를 없앤다」): every case of this section (FUP-NGX-01 .. FUP-NGX-07) is dropped together with the nginx site. The rows stay as the record; none is run.**

User: 「그러면 80으로 공격 안들어오게 서버 설정 해줄래?」. The port 80 block that `--tls-acme` writes for each own host name (`NginxSite.challenge`) had two locations only. It now serves the proof file and nothing else, limits what a request may be and how often it may come, and the 443 blocks of that site hide the version. The contract of the class stays (TLS-022): no `default_server`, no other `server_name` than the own names, no other site touched, no `http` block and no include; the only top-level lines are the two shared memory zones of the instance (their names carry the instance name, so no other site and no other instance is affected). Directives and their contexts (https://nginx.org/en/docs/): `limit_req_zone` and `limit_conn_zone` http; `limit_req`, `limit_req_status`, `limit_conn`, `limit_conn_status`, `client_max_body_size`, `default_type`, `server_tokens` http, server, location; `client_header_timeout`, `client_body_timeout` http, server; `keepalive_timeout` http, server, location; `limit_except` location; `try_files` server, location. The token of the HTTP-01 proof is base64url without padding (RFC 8555 section 8.3: `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`). Without `--tls-acme` nothing of this is rendered, and the digests of DeployGoldenTest are unchanged.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FUP-NGX-01 | INSTALL | the automatic site (`--tls-acme`), several own host names | render the port 80 block of a name | per block, in this order: `server_tokens off;`, `client_max_body_size 1k;`, `client_header_timeout 10s;`, `client_body_timeout 10s;`, `keepalive_timeout 10s;`, `limit_req zone=<instance>-acme-req burst=20 nodelay;`, `limit_req_status 429;`, `limit_conn <instance>-acme-conn 20;`, `limit_conn_status 429;` (red before the fix: the block has the two locations only) | user: protect port 80 | no |
| FUP-NGX-02 | INSTALL | the same | read the locations of a port 80 block | (a) `location ~ "^/\.well-known/acme-challenge/[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$"` with `limit_except GET { deny all; }` (GET includes HEAD, so only these two methods pass), `root <web root>;`, `default_type text/plain;`, `try_files $uri =404;`; (b) `location /.well-known/acme-challenge/` with `return 404;` for any other request below that path; (c) `location /` with `return 301 https://<the name of the block>$request_uri;`; no `$host` anywhere in a port 80 block, no `proxy_pass`, and the web root is the only directory that is served | RFC 8555 8.3 | no |
| FUP-NGX-03 | INSTALL | the automatic site, pending (no certificate files) and issued | render | every 443 block has `server_tokens off;` too (issued); the pending site has the port 80 blocks only; the operator's certificate site (no `--tls-acme`) has `server_tokens off;` in each of its two 443 blocks (right after `server_name`, red before the fix: the line was written for the automatic site only, so the responses showed `Server: nginx/<version>`), and still has no port 80 block, no zones and no limits | user; Linux test server 2026-11-15 | no |
| FUP-NGX-04 | INSTALL | the automatic site of the instance `pg-ledger-mcp` and of the instance `ledger-two` | render both | the two lines `limit_req_zone $binary_remote_addr zone=<instance>-acme-req:1m rate=10r/s;` and `limit_conn_zone $binary_remote_addr zone=<instance>-acme-conn:1m;` stand once, at the top of the file before the first `server` block (the file is included into the `http` context); the zone names differ between the instances; every zone used by `limit_req` / `limit_conn` is declared in the same file; a comment gives the reason for the numbers (the certificate authority checks from several places at the same time, each from its own address, a few requests per address and order, so 10 requests per second with a burst of 20 and 20 connections per address are far above that) | user; nginx documentation | no |
| FUP-NGX-05 | INSTALL | TLS-022 (all three modes) | read | unchanged: only the own names as `server_name`, no `default_server`, no `server_name _`, no `http {`, no `include `, no `upstream`, no `worker_`; the comments of the site do not contain these words either | class contract | no |
| FUP-NGX-06 | DOCS | both READMEs, the section Automatic certificate | read | the section says what a port 80 block does (it serves the proof file for a valid token with GET and HEAD, answers 404 to every other request below the proof path, redirects the rest to https at the fixed name, limits the size and the time of a request, limits the rate and the connections per address with the answer 429, hides the version) and what it does not (it takes no `default_server`, no other site is touched, no global directive is set; the two zones carry the name of the instance); one recommendation: when this is the only site that uses port 80 on the host, add a default server of your own that closes requests for unknown names or addresses, for example `listen 80 default_server; listen [::]:80 default_server; server_name _; return 444;`, and the product does not create it; the code spans of the two languages are the same set (DOC-018) | user | no |
| FUP-NGX-07 | INSTALL | the rendered site on the test server | `nginx -t` (done by the main agent: nginx is not installed on the development PC) | the file loads. The directives with a syntax risk that the render tests cannot check: the quoted regular expression of the token location (a backslash before the dot is kept by the nginx parser), `limit_except GET { deny all; }` inside the regular expression location, `limit_req ... nodelay` with `limit_req_status`, and the two zone declarations at the top of an included file | the user's request | no |

## 30. BLD: Gradle build optimization (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit GRD)

Written before the change of the build. User: 「글고 그래들 빌드 최적화도 해줘.」 Criteria of the owner of the build machine: the Gradle daemon stays on; the configuration cache, the build cache and parallel execution are switched on in the `gradle.properties` of the repository; resource limits (`--max-workers`, `-Pledger.test.forks`, `-Pledger.test.maxHeapSize`, `-Pledger.test.activeProcessorCount`) are command arguments only and never a default of the repository; no `--rerun-tasks`, no `-x`, no switching off of the incremental build or of a cache; `JAVA_HOME` is fixed per command; file watching keeps its default; no toolchain is downloaded (the build has no toolchain resolver plugin and keeps none). The commands below are given without the resource limits of a machine; on a machine with few resources they are run with the limits of the Build and test section of the README as arguments.

Decisions. (1) The tests of the modules that use the test database (`ledger-database`, `ledger-application`, `ledger-mcp-server`, `ledger-admin-web`, `ledger-cli` with its `serverTest`, `ledger-system-test`) are never up to date and, with the build cache on, are never loaded from it and never stored in it: the state of the database is an input that Gradle cannot see (it was `upToDateWhen { false }` only, which a cache does not respect). Both are stated, with the reason, in the build script. (2) The tests that touch no database (`ledger-domain`, `ledger-attestation`, `ledger-seed`) are cacheable, so every file their tests read is declared as an input: the source scans of the architecture tests read `src/main/java`, `src/test/java` and the `build.gradle.kts` of the module by a path relative to the module, and no class file or class path entry shows a change of a comment in them. (3) The tests of `ledger-client-hook` start the real jar as processes and measure and record the wall time of each start (HK-017); the time of the machine is an input that Gradle cannot see, so this test task may be up to date (as before) but is not cached. Its undeclared inputs (the hook jar, the staged plugin directories, the `clients` directory) are declared. (4) Every task works with the configuration cache: no task action reads `Project` or a `Configuration` at execution time (the check of the runtime class paths, the system property of the PostgreSQL driver jar, the paths of the jars of the packaged product). (5) The check of the runtime class paths keeps its meaning (the forbidden groups of DRV-014; the H2 driver allowed on `ledger-seed` and `ledger-cli` only) and becomes one task `verifyRuntimeClasspath` per module, wired to the `check` of that module; the resolved artifacts of the runtime class path are its input. `./gradlew verifyRuntimeClasspath` and `./gradlew check` still run it for every module. (6) The 6 Kotlin DSL delegate declarations (`val x by creating` / `by registering`) are replaced by the form that Gradle 10 keeps. (7) With parallel execution on by default, the test tasks of several database modules would run at the same time, and each test JVM opens up to 10 connections to the one test database role, whose connection limit two concurrent builds already exceeded. That is a constraint of one shared external resource, not a resource limit of a machine, so it belongs in the build: one shared build service (`ledgerTestDatabase`, `maxParallelUsages = 1`) is used by every test task of the database modules (`ledger-database`, `ledger-application`, `ledger-mcp-server`, `ledger-admin-web`, `ledger-cli` with `test` and `serverTest`, `ledger-system-test`), so these run one after the other whatever the number of workers; no other task is limited by it.

## 31. WSEC: taking the secrets over on Windows and a run that fails (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit WSEC)

**Removed by PCS (section 45; user, verbatim: seal-host 「없앰」): `seal-host` and the hand-over of the secrets to the service account do not exist any more, so there is nothing to take back and no privilege to enable: every case of this section (WSEC-01 .. WSEC-13) is dropped. The rows stay as the record of the incident; none is run. What remains true is the idea of WSEC-10 .. WSEC-12 (a `prepare-host` that fails puts the owner and the ACL of what it changed back as it found them, `WindowsPlatform.putBack`); the new rows PCS-034 .. PCS-036 hold it for the ACL of the folders (the secrets are never changed by the run).**

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the Windows test server (an elevated Administrator, High integrity). `install.cmd`, run again over an installation whose secrets `seal-host` had handed to LocalService, exited 1 in `prepare-host` with `AccessDeniedException` on `etc\secrets`. Before it failed, `prepare-host` had already closed the home, `lib`, `etc` and `etc\drivers` to SYSTEM and Administrators, so LocalService lost `lib`, and the two services that `install.cmd` had stopped could not start again (WinSW: error 267, the working directory `lib` is invalid). `seal-host` alone then granted `lib`, `etc` and `logs` again and failed on the first secret file. The first installation (no secrets yet) succeeded.

Causes. (1) `WindowsPlatform.prepare` set the ACL of the secrets folder first (the folder loop, `setAcl`), and `setAcl` needs `WRITE_DAC`, which Administrators do not have on a folder that LocalService owns and whose ACL gives them `Rc,S,RA` only; the owner of the folder is the one who may write its ACL, so the secrets had to be taken over first. (2) The JDK enables no privilege in `setAcl`, and only `SeRestorePrivilege` in `setOwner` (java.base, `sun.nio.fs.WindowsAclFileAttributeView#setOwner`, which enables it for the one `SetFileSecurity` call); `SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege`, which opens a file for `WRITE_OWNER` against its ACL, is never enabled, and an elevated token holds both disabled. (3) `prepare` closed every folder before the step that can be refused, and a failure left them closed. (4) `install.cmd` stopped the services and, on any later failure, left them stopped.

Decisions. (1) `WindowsPrivileges` enables `SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege` and `SeRestorePrivilege` on the token of the process with `OpenProcessToken`, `LookupPrivilegeValueW` and `AdjustTokenPrivileges` (the foreign function API of Java 25, no external program), for the one operation, and puts the token back; `ERROR_NOT_ALL_ASSIGNED` is read in the same call, and a refusal of the file system while the token does not hold them is a `PrivilegeNotHeldException` (HOST_FAILED naming the path; its text says to run in an elevated prompt). The meaning of the ACLs does not change (closed, taken back, handed over). Without the manifest attribute `Enable-Native-Access: ALL-UNNAMED` (or the flag `--enable-native-access=ALL-UNNAMED`) Java 25 prints a warning on the first call and goes on; the jar carries the attribute (DPL-08), so `install.cmd` gives no flag. (2) `WindowsAccess.reassign` makes the installer the owner whoever owns the path, writes the ACL as its owner, and then hands the path over to the owner it is given; `save` and `restore` read and put back the owner and the ACL. (3) `prepare-host` reads what it will change, takes the secrets back first (the folder, then each file), then closes the home and its folders, then copies; a failure at any step puts back what it changed, the last first, and reports the failure that happened (what could not be put back is attached to it). `seal-host` takes the folder first as well (an administrator cannot list a sealed folder), hands over the files and then the folder. (4) `install.cmd` notes which of the two services run before it stops them (`sc.exe query`, STATE 4), runs the steps after the stop in one routine, and on a failure gives the service account its access again (`seal-host`) and starts only the services that ran, then exits 1. (5) `install.sh` is unchanged: it stops no service, and its `prepare-host` closes nothing to the service user (the directories are its own from the start), so it has neither defect.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WSEC-01 | CLI | the host of an earlier installation (all folders, two secrets), `WindowsAccess` and the copy replaced by recorders, any operating system | `seal` | the grants of `lib` (read and execute), `etc` and `logs` (modify) skip the secrets folder, as before; then the secrets folder is taken (installer), each secret is handed to the service account with its ACL (full; Administrators only attributes, ACL and synchronize), then the folder; nothing else is touched | incident: `seal-host` alone failed on the first file | no |
| WSEC-02 | CLI | the same host | `prepare` | the secrets folder and each file in it (name order) are taken over by the installer (owner and ACL; ownership first); the secrets folder is never closed like the others (no `set` for it); the home, `lib`, `etc`, `etc\drivers` and `logs` are closed as before; the two WinSW executables are copied | incident: `etc\secrets` was closed with `setAcl`, which needs `WRITE_DAC` | no |
| WSEC-03 | CLI | a `PrivilegeNotHeldException` for the secrets folder | `HostFiles.failure` | HOST_FAILED with the path and `PrivilegeNotHeldException` as arguments, the refusal as the root cause | decision (1) | no |
| WSEC-04 | CLI | Windows, an elevated prompt (skipped otherwise: an assumption) | enable the two privileges, close, enable one again | `allHeld()` is true both times (the token is put back in between) | decision (1) | no |
| WSEC-05 | CLI | Windows, a prompt that is not elevated (skipped in an elevated one) | enable the two privileges | no exception; `allHeld()` is false | decision (1): a clear refusal, not a crash | no |
| WSEC-06 | CLI | Windows, elevated; a file owned by SYSTEM whose ACL is what `seal-host` leaves (SYSTEM full, Administrators attributes, ACL and synchronize) | `setAcl` of the JDK; `WindowsAccess.reassign` to the installer; `reassign` back to SYSTEM | `setAcl` of the JDK is `AccessDeniedException` (the defect); after `reassign` the installer owns and reads the file; after the second the owner is SYSTEM and the ACL is SYSTEM and Administrators in that order | incident | no |
| WSEC-07 | CLI | Windows, elevated; an installation made by `prepare-host` and sealed by `seal-host` to the real LocalService | `prepare-host` again | exit 0; the secrets folder and the secret are the installer's, each with one ACL entry, and the secret is readable (red before the fix: `HOST_FAILED`, `AccessDeniedException` on the secrets folder) | incident | no |
| WSEC-08 | CLI | the host of WSEC-01 | `prepare` | in this order: the secrets folder is taken back (installer as owner, ACL `takenBack`), then each secret file in name order, then the ACL `closed` of the home, `lib`, `etc`, `etc\drivers`, `logs`; nothing is put back (red before the fix: the folders are closed first) | incident: the host stayed closed | no |
| WSEC-09 | CLI | nothing exists (a first installation) | `prepare` | no path is read; the home and its folders are closed in order; the secrets folder is made last and is the installer's alone; nothing is put back | incident | no |
| WSEC-10 | CLI | the host of WSEC-01, the file system refuses the second secret (`AccessDeniedException`) | `prepare` | the refusal itself is thrown; no ACL is written (nothing is closed), the jars are not copied, no WinSW executable exists; all eight paths that were read (home, `lib`, `etc`, `etc\drivers`, secrets, `logs`, the two files that were reached) are put back, the last first | incident: the host stayed closed | no |
| WSEC-11 | CLI | the same, the copy of the jars fails after the folders were closed, and the put-back of the secrets folder fails too | `prepare` | the failure of the copy is thrown with the failure of the put-back attached; all eight paths are put back (one failing does not stop the others), the last first | incident | no |
| WSEC-12 | CLI | the sealed installation of WSEC-07, the path of `lib\ledger-cli.jar` is a folder with a file in it, so the copy of the jars fails | `prepare-host` again | HOST_FAILED; the owner and the ACL of the home, `lib`, `etc`, `etc\drivers`, `logs`, the secrets folder and the secret are the same as before the run (red before the fix: the folders stay closed) | incident: the services could not start | no |
| WSEC-13 | INSTALL | `deploy/install.cmd` | read | the state of each service is read with `sc.exe query` (STATE 4) before the stop; every step after the stop is in the routine `:steps`, which is called with `|| goto :fail`, and no line between the stop and the routine exits the script with 1; `:fail` runs `seal-host %RENDER%` and then starts the service, for a service that ran only (`if defined UP_%%S`), and exits 1; the order of the steps and the budget of lines are INS-035 and INS-046 (red before the fix: the exits after the stop end the script and leave the services stopped) | incident | no |

WSEC-04, WSEC-06, WSEC-07 and WSEC-12 need an elevated prompt on Windows and are skipped (an assumption) elsewhere (WSEC-05 needs a prompt that is not elevated); they run on the Windows test server with `./gradlew :ledger-cli:test --tests "org.pgledger.cli.deploy.WindowsSecretsTest"` in an elevated prompt. The real red and green of the incident are `install.cmd` run again over a sealed installation, before and after the fix.

## 33. HLP: shared helpers (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit helpers)

Written before the code of the unit. Decisions: `ledger-attestation` (JDK only, the lowest module) holds the one SHA-256 helper; `ledger-domain` and `ledger-seed` depend on it (ARC-009 is changed to say so; the hook jar still holds no domain code). The Spring modules take ids and trace ids from one injected `IdGenerator`. `LedgerHook` (a program started per call, no Spring) keeps its own `UUID.randomUUID` until a separate decision.

## 34. TXD: transaction boundaries, shared resources and null handling (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit txdb)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the principle audit of 2026-11-15 (principles 2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.7, 3.9, 4.4). The transaction is declared on a service method (one use case, one method); repositories, entry points and the file of a registration open none; a file is written outside a transaction and replaced after the commit.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TXD-001 | SERVICE | an owner identity and a driver class; the caller holds no transaction | `DriverAuditService.recordRegistered` | one `owner_audit` row `DRIVER_REGISTERED` with the actor, the class as subject and the given detail, committed by the service method itself | audit finding 1 (principle 4.4) | yes |
| TXD-002 | SERVICE | a registration whose row is in the trail | `DriverAuditService.recordIfMissing` for the same jar and digest, then for another jar, then for that one again | false; true with a row whose detail is the jar, the digest and `registered_at=<time of the registration>`; false (a repeated call writes nothing) | audit finding 1 | yes |

## 35. SQB: the SQL that differs by database product (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit sqlbuilder)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: user 2026-11-15, 「너 혹시 dbms가 postgresql만 있다고 생각하는거야?」 and 「sqlBuilder 좋은말할때 써라.」 The ledger runs on the DBMS the user installs (PostgreSQL, Oracle, Db2, SQL Server, MariaDB, MySQL; H2 only as the format of the seed bundle), so the SQL that differs by product is chosen from the product the database reports. Decisions: the AGS `SqlBuilder` (AGS repository `jaeseongs95/sennard-source`, commit `1ad1b8e7`) is ported to `ledger-database` (`org.pgledger.database.repository.SqlBuilder`, a component that takes the one `DataSource` bean); the row limit of the six query repositories (nine statements) is `max + 1` rows in the clause of the product with a bound placeholder (it was JDBC `setMaxRows`); the AGS store failures become the `INFRASTRUCTURE` codes `DATABASE_PRODUCT_UNREADABLE`, `DATABASE_PRODUCT_UNSUPPORTED` and `DATABASE_PRODUCT_MISMATCH` (English and Korean messages); the product name is read in the `driver` package (`DatabaseProductName`), the one place where the JDBC exception is caught (ARC-036). No DBMS other than the PostgreSQL of the test PC is installed for the tests: the clauses of the other products are checked as text (SQB-002) against the documentation links in `SqlBuilder`.

## 36. TXF: transaction boundaries that the review of the service layer found outside it (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit txfollow)

Written before the code of each finding. Source: the review of the transaction boundaries (TX-03, TX-04, TX-05, TX-07, TX-08, and the enforcement advice) on the integration branch (principles 3.3, 3.4, 3.9, 4.4, 9 items 37 and 39). The rule of every case: a transaction is declared on a service method; nothing opens one by hand (no `TransactionTemplate`, no `TransactionAspectSupport`, no savepoint of our own); a lost race is not run again; the database decides it (a unique key, a conditional statement) and the loser answers from what the winner committed. SQL stays ANSI: the ledger runs on any DBMS that the user installs. One finding is one commit.

**Rewritten by the unit txpure (principle 4.4, 2026-11-15).** The unit txfollow solved TX-03, TX-04 and TX-08 with nested writer beans (`Propagation.NESTED`), the catch of a `DuplicateKeyException` to continue, lock-only UPDATEs and `RequiredTransaction`; the rule forbids all of them. What holds now: one use case is one service method and one `@Transactional` (default propagation); repositories only join; no lock statement; a race is decided by the write of the use case itself (a conditional UPDATE by its row count, or a unique key); the loser answers from the winner's committed result when it reads it in its own statement path, or ends the call with the translated business error (`DUPLICATE_KEY`, `VERSION_CONFLICT`) and the whole transaction rolls back; a rare simultaneous first creation ends with the translated unique-key error (accepted). Where a row below still names a nested writer, `RequiredTransaction` or `holdLive`, it describes 8800df2 and the text after "rewritten" says what holds now.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TXF-001 | ARCH, SERVICE | `ReceiptService.reserve` and `SessionKeyService.write` made a savepoint by hand (`TransactionAspectSupport.currentTransactionStatus()`, `createSavepoint`, `rollbackToSavepoint`, `releaseSavepoint`) around the insert that other calls race for | the rule TXF-001 (`ArchitectureRules.noHandMadeTransactionControl`) over the application module; SVC-RCP-17 and SVC-KEY-20; the existing race cases SVC-RCP-09 .. 11 and SVC-KEY-07, 13, 17, 18, 19 | no class uses `TransactionAspectSupport` or a savepoint method (red before the fix: the rule finds `ReceiptService` and `SessionKeyService`); (rewritten by the unit txpure: the nested writer beans `ReceiptWriter` and `SessionKeyWriter` of 8800df2, each a `@Service` with `Propagation.NESTED`, are removed; TXP-001 forbids the device) the receipt is read first and inserted in the transaction of the write; the close and insert of a key are steps of the one `SessionKeyService` method; a duplicate key at the insert ends the call with the translated `DUPLICATE_KEY` and the whole transaction rolls back (SVC-RCP-17 and 18, SVC-KEY-20); where a read before the write answers (a stored receipt, a live key of the same author) the answer is the idempotent one | principle 4.4, 9 items 37 and 39 | no |
| TXP-001 | ARCH | the production classes of every module (`PrincipleRules.all`, the rule runs in the ArchitectureTest of each module) | `@Transactional` with `Propagation.NESTED`, `REQUIRES_NEW` or `MANDATORY` (also `jakarta.transaction.Transactional` with `TxType.REQUIRES_NEW` or `MANDATORY`) on a class or a method | none is found; `PrincipleRulesTest` plants one of each in `principlefixture.Planted` (`nested`, `requiresNew`, `mandatory`) and the default propagation in `Clean.Registrations` (red on 8800df2: the four writer beans were `NESTED`) | principle 4.4 | no |
| TXP-002 | ARCH (source) | the main sources of every module | a SQL string literal with a self-assignment (`SET version = version`, `SET note = note`) or a parameterized pin-style equality assignment (`SET col = :param` where `WHERE` also constrains `col = :param`, including lists or splits over literals); `SET n = n + 1` is not | none is found outside the 5 explicit `PRINCIPLE_ALLOWED` ANSI row-pin guards (`ProjectPinImpl`, `SpecPinImpl`, `TaskPinImpl`, `CriterionPinImpl`, `IssueMergeGuardImpl`); `PrincipleRulesTest` plants both forms and the clean forms (red on 8800df2: `RowLockRepository`, `holdLive`) | principle 4.4; R4/R5/R6/R7 | no |
| TXP-003 | ARCH | the production classes of every module | a class with `@Transactional` (class or method) whose name ends in `Writer`, `Recorder`, `Entry` or `Coordinator` | none is found; `PrincipleRulesTest` plants `Planted.PlantedWriter` (red on 8800df2: the nested writers). The structural form (a service that only other transactional services call) is not decidable inside one module, whose ArchUnit import does not hold the callers of another module, so the rule is by name | principle 4.4 | no |
| TXF-002 | SERVICE, MIGRATION | `CommonCodeService.set` read the code, then wrote it without a condition (`codes.update(wanted)`, the result ignored): two owner actions on the same code let the later one overwrite the earlier one silently; the code was created in two places (`set` and `addIfAbsent`) and a concurrent add broke on a `DuplicateKeyException` that nobody caught | SVC-CC-11 (two sets that read the same version, the other action committing on a second thread between the read and the write), SVC-CC-12 (8 threads `addIfAbsent` of one new code), SVC-CC-13 (8 threads `set` of one new code), SCH-036, SVC-CC-01 .. 10 and SET-023 as before | the row carries a `version` (V5) and the update is `UPDATE ... SET ..., version = version + 1 WHERE code_group = ? AND code = ? AND version = <the version read>`; an update that affects no row is `VERSION_CONFLICT` (args `common_code`, `<group>/<code>`, the version read, the version now) with nothing overwritten, no revision bump and no audit row (SVC-CC-11, red before the fix: the later write wins); the one creation path is `create` in the one `@Transactional` method (read first, then the insert; no nested writer bean, no savepoint, no catch-and-continue): a creation at the very same moment as another call breaks the primary key and ends with the translated `DUPLICATE_KEY` (SVC-CC-16, deterministic: the read of the service saw no row; red on 8800df2 because `addIfAbsent` answered empty and `set` changed the row of the winner), so 8 concurrent calls leave one row and one `added` audit row, exactly one call adds the code, and every other call answers empty, or ends with `DUPLICATE_KEY` (`set`: or `VERSION_CONFLICT`), never a raw exception (SVC-CC-12 and 13, rewritten by the unit txpure; the earlier outcome "the loser reads the row of the winner" was the NESTED device that principle 4.4 forbids) | principle 4.4 (rewritten by the unit txpure), 9 item 39 (one creation point; a conditional update or the unique key decides; the loser ends with the translated error) | no |
| TXF-003 | SERVICE, ARCH | `SetupCommand` seeded the codes one by one: a service call (a transaction) for each of the 14 codes, so a failure in the middle left a part of the seed behind | SVC-CC-14, SVC-CC-15 (the service), ArchitectureTest TXF-003 (the command), SET-010, SET-011, SET-012, SET-023 as before | `CommonCodeService.seed(List<CodeCommand>, OwnerIdentity)` is one `@Transactional` method that adds every code that is absent (`addIfAbsent` for each, so revision bump and audit row as before) and answers how many it added: a list with one invalid code adds nothing, with no revision bump and no audit row (SVC-CC-14, red before the fix: there is no such method; the old loop kept the codes before the invalid one); a code that exists is left as the owner left it and a second seed adds none (SVC-CC-15); `SetupCommand` calls `seed` once and never `addIfAbsent` (ArchitectureTest TXF-003, red before the fix: the command called `addIfAbsent` per code); the output and the report of `setup` are those of SET-010 .. 014 | principle 4.4 (one use case is one service method and one transaction) | no |
| TXF-004 | SERVICE, MIGRATION | `DriverAuditService.recordIfMissing` (called by `PendingDriverAudit` of `migrate` and `setup`) read the trail and inserted the row when it found none, with no unique key: two runs at the same time could both find none and write the row twice | DriverAuditServiceTest TXF-004 (a run whose read missed the row; 8 threads; rewritten by the unit txpure: the nested-write case is gone), TXD-002 as before, SCH-037 | the registration is claimed by a unique key (V6 `driver_audit_key`: the SHA-256 of class, jar, jar digest and time of the registration, 64 hex characters, so the primary key fits an index on every DBMS) in the one `@Transactional` method `recordIfMissing`: it reads the trail (a row of this registration answers false), inserts the key and then the row; there is no nested writer bean (`DriverAuditWriter`, `Propagation.NESTED`, removed by the unit txpure: principle 4.4) and no catch-and-continue: a run whose read missed the row breaks the unique key and ends with the translated `DUPLICATE_KEY` (red on 8800df2: it answered false), writing nothing; a second call writes nothing (TXD-002); 8 concurrent calls write one row, exactly one answers true, each other answers false or ends with `DUPLICATE_KEY`, never a raw exception; a registration with another time is another registration | principle 4.4, ANSI SQL only (no vendor feature: the unique key is a plain primary key) | no |
| TXF-005 | WIRING, REPO | `GrantApplier` (a `@Component`) ran the several `GRANT` statements of the template with `DatabasePopulatorUtils` outside any transaction: a failure in the middle left a part of them applied | SchemaWiringTest SCH-031 (the grant step is an AOP proxy), `GrantStepTest` TXF-005 (twice, through the container), INS-010 as before, ArchitectureTest ARC-013 of the database module | `GrantApplier` is a `@Service` with `@Transactional` and the populator joins its transaction (the connection comes from `DataSourceUtils`, as for `SchemaFileApplier`); red before the fix: the bean is no proxy. The step is safe to run again: the second run succeeds and the account has the same privileges (`information_schema.table_privileges`, on the test PostgreSQL), and `migrate` and `setup` run it on every call. The class comment says why this matters, with the sources: MySQL lists `GRANT` among the statements that implicitly commit, Oracle commits before and after every DDL statement and `GRANT` is one, so a partial grant is possible there; PostgreSQL documents that privileges "are added to those already granted"; the repeat of a grant is not checked for the other products | principle 4.4 | no |
| TXF-006 | ARCH, REPO | the review counted the gaps of the enforcement: `TransactionTemplate` was refused in five modules and `TransactionAspectSupport` in none, ARC-PRN-05 (a transaction that writes a file) saw only `@Transactional` methods and missed the lambda of a `TransactionTemplate`, and of the repositories that lock a row or count and then write only `IdAllocator` refused a call without a transaction | ArchitectureTest TXF-001 in every module (`ledger-domain`, `ledger-attestation`, `ledger-client-hook`, `ledger-database`, `ledger-application`, `ledger-admin-web`, `ledger-mcp-server`, `ledger-cli`: `noTransactionTemplate` and `noHandMadeTransactionControl`), PrincipleRulesTest (the planted `Planted.writeInATemplate`, `Planted.savepointByHand`) | no production class of any module uses `TransactionTemplate`, `TransactionAspectSupport` or a savepoint method; `ARC-PRN-05` treats a method that runs a `TransactionTemplate` as a transaction (red before the fix: `Planted.writeInATemplate` was not found). (Changed by the unit txpure: the part that made repositories refuse a call without a transaction, `RequiredTransaction.check` and `TransactionRequiredTest`, is removed. The methods it listed were lock devices or used with them (`RowLockRepository`, `holdLive`, `DefectCaseLockRepository`); what stays is the case number of the defect (`CaseSequence.nextCaseSeq`), `GrantStateRepository`, `ReceiptCompletionRepository.finish` and `CodeRevisionRepository.bump`, which only join the transaction of the calling service method, and a repository carries no `@Transactional`: ARC-013 of the database module, TXD-003.) | principle 4.4, 9 item 37 | no |

## 37. RET: removing the 1.x database objects after the move to 2.0 (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit legacyretire; the rows run in `ledger-migrate-1x` since the unit migratetool, command `retire-1x` for `retire-legacy`, section 43)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the user, 2026-11-15, 「dbms에 트리거, 사용자함수, 프로시저 등등 만든거 있으면 다 걷어내라그래」, and the decision 「전부 한 번에」: the logic, the old tables, views and the schema go in one run, after the move, a `pg_dump` backup of the 1.x database and a restore test (no separate retention step). 1.x (tag v1.1.0) made 5 objects in `sql/10_tables.sql` and 24 in `sql/20_api.sql` of 1.x (PL/pgSQL functions, a trigger, views, SECURITY DEFINER) in the schema `ledger`, and four database roles (`ledger_owner`, `ledger_author`, `ledger_reviewer`, `ledger_reader`); 2.0 has none of them (`AnsiRulesTest`) and `import-legacy` only reads that schema, so nothing removed them. Decisions. (1) The command `retire-legacy [--confirm=<sha256> --backup-tested]` never runs by itself. Without `--confirm` it lists, writes nothing and prints the SHA-256 of the list; with `--confirm` and the flag `--backup-tested` (an acknowledgement: the command cannot check the backup) it drops in one transaction. (2) The list comes from the `information_schema` wherever it has the data; `pg_catalog` is read only for the identity arguments of a function, the owner of an object, the roles and the sessions, in the one class `LegacySchemaRepository`, which is written for the 1.x source only (the ledger of 2.0 stays ANSI). (3) Order of the drop: triggers, views, tables (one statement: foreign keys), functions and procedures, sequences, the schema, the roles. This differs from the first design (functions before views and tables) on purpose: a table of 1.x has CHECK constraints that call `ledger._author_ok`, so the function cannot go first. (4) Preconditions, each a refusal that names what is wrong: the schema is not the schema of 2.0 (the legacy schema holds no `schema_history`), `import-legacy --verify-only` matches, no other session is connected as a 1.x role or holds a lock in the schema, the account owns the objects. (5) A role stays, and is listed with the reason, when it is the running account, an account of the 2.0 settings, has members outside the 1.x set or owns an object outside the schema. Not verified by a test: dropping a role (the owner account of the tests may not create one, and a role belongs to the whole cluster); every test names its roles with `ledger.import.source.roles`.

## 38. DBN: the ANSI SQL boundary of the personal edition (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit dbneutral)

Written before the code of each item. Scope (user decisions 「ANSI SQL만 유지」, 2026-11-15, and 「SqlBuilder 방언이 정함」, 2026-11-16): the SQL is ANSI and uses the JDBC standard; the dialect points are three, the row limit, the long text declaration and the encoding probe (D-0047, RUN-023 .. RUN-031), all chosen by `SqlBuilder` from the product of the connection; PostgreSQL is the DBMS that is tested and guaranteed; no tailoring per product is done outside these three places. Only defects that exist on every DBMS, PostgreSQL included, are cases. One item is one commit.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DBN-001 | REPO | `SchemaHistoryRepository.tableExists` gave the schema and the table name to `DatabaseMetaData.getTables` as they are, but both arguments are LIKE patterns (JDBC): `_` is a wildcard | `SchemaNamePatternTest` DBN-001: a schema whose name differs from the ledger schema only where the name has `_` holds a `schema_history`; the ledger schema holds only `schemaxhistory` | `tableExists` is false (red before the fix: true, the neighbour schema and the other table matched the pattern); after `schema_history` is created in the ledger schema it is true; the name is escaped with `DatabaseMetaData.getSearchStringEscape()` (`SchemaHistoryRepository.exactPattern`) | JDBC javadoc of `getTables` | PostgreSQL |
| DBN-002 | REPO | **changed by M1X (section 43)**: DBN-002 moves to the tool and runs in `ledger-migrate-1x` as `LegacySourceNameTest`; `LegacySourceRepository.missingSteps` asked for the `_migration` table the same way | `SchemaNamePatternTest` DBN-002 (now `LegacySourceNameTest`): the neighbour schema holds `_migration`, the empty legacy schema does not | all four steps are reported missing (red before the fix: the neighbour matched, so the statement read `<schema>._migration` and failed) | JDBC javadoc of `getTables` | PostgreSQL |
| DBN-003 | SERVICE | the PostgreSQL driver refuses more than 32767 bind parameters in one statement; the lists that become an `IN` list are: `GetService.get` ids (3 statements), the `ids` of `HistoryQueryRepository.page`, the history ids of `HistoryRepository.findByIdIn` and the parents of `MergeChainRepository` | DBN-003 (`ReadQueryTest`, get with 200 and 201 ids), MCR-H15 (history with 200 and 201 ids) | a list from a caller is bounded at the entry: `GetService.MAX_IDS` and `HistoryService.MAX_IDS` are 200 and the 201st is refused with `VALIDATION_FAILED` / `MUST_HOLD_ITEMS` (args `1`, `200`) before any SQL runs; `findByIdIn` takes at most `MaxRows.MAX + 1` = 1001 ids (the page the query just returned); the parents of one level of a merge chain are defects of the ledger itself (ceiling: 32767 defects merged at one level of one chain; no input reaches it). The base already bounded every list: only the test for `get` is new, no limit is changed | JDBC driver limit (pgjdbc) | PostgreSQL |
| DBN-004 | REPO | the row-lock and conditional-update logic is written for READ_COMMITTED, but `DriverDataSources.create` left the isolation of the pool to the default of the DBMS | `DriverBoundaryTest.dbn004_thePoolIsReadCommitted` (the real chain on the test PostgreSQL) | the pool is configured with `HikariConfig.setTransactionIsolation("TRANSACTION_READ_COMMITTED")` (the JDBC standard name) and a pooled connection reports `Connection.TRANSACTION_READ_COMMITTED` (red before the fix: `HikariDataSource.getTransactionIsolation()` is null; PostgreSQL's default is READ COMMITTED, so the connection check alone would pass on the base) | JDBC `Connection.setTransactionIsolation` | PostgreSQL |

## 39. PSK: the owner's passkeys, the sign-in choice and the approval of every owner write (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit passkey)

Written before the code of each step. Source: user 2026-11-15, 「로그인 OAuth·passkey 택1, 승인 passkey」 and 「이메일 한개만 등록 가능하게」; the AGS personal edition for the approval (a passkey assertion over a challenge derived from the change, `AdminWebAuthn`, `AdminIssuedIntents`, `AdminReset`); the common plan of the three products: Spring Security 7.1.2 WebAuthn (`spring-security-webauthn`, WebAuthn4J 0.31.10, Apache-2.0) for sign-in, registration and the approval check (`WebAuthnRelyingPartyOperations.authenticate` over request options that the server makes from the change, user verification required), the credential storage implemented here on ANSI tables (base64url `VARCHAR`, no BLOB, no boolean, no UPDATE or DELETE of a credential: a revocation is a row of its own), and the one-time code of the owner's shell carried by `oneTimeTokenLogin`. The sign-in is chosen by `ledger.admin.sign-in` (`oidc` or `passkey`); the OIDC owner is one e-mail address. The admin token no longer signs in a browser: it stays the bearer of the read-only JSON endpoints (no endpoint changes the ledger). No browser, no authenticator is used by the tests: the software authenticator `SoftwarePasskey` writes the registration and the assertion as a browser does.

| ID | Type | Subject | Steps | Expected | Source | Manual |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PSK-DB-01 (SCH-038) | MIGRATION | V7 `owner_passkey_user`, `owner_passkey`, `owner_passkey_revocation`, `owner_passkey_code` and the check constraint of `owner_audit` | insert a credential twice and with a flag of 2; insert a revocation twice; insert a code twice; read the columns; insert the four new audit operations | the credential id is the key (the second insert is SQLState class 23), a flag outside 0 and 1 is refused, a credential has one revocation row at most, a code hash is unique; no column of the four tables is a BLOB, a binary type or a boolean; `owner_audit` takes `PASSKEY_CODE_ISSUED`, `PASSKEY_REGISTERED`, `PASSKEY_REVOKED` and `PASSKEY_RESET`; the migrator applies V1..V7 and records V7 (SCH-001); the grant template names the tables (the account has no UPDATE on a credential and no DELETE on any of them) | task brief | yes |
| PSK-SVC-01 | SERVICE | `PasskeyService.issueCode` | issue two codes | 20 characters of the alphabet without look-alikes in five groups of four; expires 15 minutes later; only the SHA-256 of the code is stored; every code is another | task brief (SecureRandom, short TTL, stored hashed) | yes |
| PSK-SVC-02 | SERVICE | `PasskeyService.spendCode` | a missing, empty, unknown, expired and used code; the code in lower case without dashes | spent once whatever the letter case and the dashes; every other case is `PASSKEY_CODE_INVALID` | task brief | yes |
| PSK-SVC-03 | SERVICE | a new code | issue a code, issue another | the earlier unused code is `PASSKEY_CODE_INVALID`, the new one works | task brief | yes |
| PSK-SVC-04 | SERVICE | reset needs the flag | two live passkeys; a code without the flag; a code with it | without it the passkeys stay and no `PASSKEY_RESET` row is written; with it every live passkey gets a revocation row (`closed=2`), no credential row is deleted or changed, and `PASSKEY_CODE_ISSUED` and `PASSKEY_RESET` rows name the actor | task brief (removing existing passkeys needs an explicit flag; each reset is logged) | yes |
| PSK-SVC-05 | SERVICE | `PasskeyService.save` | save a passkey, save the same credential again with other values | stored with its fingerprint (four groups of four hex digits) and the label stripped; the second save changes nothing; the `PASSKEY_REGISTERED` row names the actor, the fingerprint and the label | task brief; common plan (append-only) | yes |
| PSK-SVC-06 | SERVICE | `PasskeyService.revoke` | revoke the only passkey; with two revoke one; an unknown and a closed id | the last passkey is `PASSKEY_LAST_KEY`; the closed one has a revocation row (with the actor), its credential row stays, it is no longer live; `PASSKEY_REVOKED` row; `NOT_FOUND` for an id that is not live | common plan (revocation is an append-only row) | yes |
| PSK-SVC-07 | SERVICE | the owner entity | save two owner entities | one row: the second is left out; found by its handle | one owner | yes |
| PSK-SVC-08 | SERVICE | limits | a label of 61 characters, a credential id of 513, a blank label | `VALIDATION_FAILED` and nothing stored; a blank label is `passkey` | principle 3 (bounds) | yes |
| PSK-SVC-09 | SERVICE | order | save two passkeys 5 seconds apart | listed oldest first | | yes |
| PSK-CLI-01 | CLI | `passkey-code` on the real wiring (OwnerCommandsTest) | run it twice | one code in five groups of four is printed each time (only then); only the hash is stored (64 characters) with `issued_by` `cli:<user>`; two `PASSKEY_CODE_ISSUED` rows with the actor `cli:<user>`; the usage text and both bundles name the command (MessagesTest, UsageTest), the README tables carry its syntax (DOC-0xx) | task brief (a ledger-cli command, as AGS `AdminReset`) | yes |
| PSK-CLI-02 | CLI | `passkey-code --remove-existing` | a live passkey row; a code without the flag; a code with it; an operand; an unknown option | without the flag no revocation row; with it the count is printed (`Closed 1 live passkey`), a revocation row names `cli:<user>`, the credential row stays, `PASSKEY_RESET` is recorded; an operand or an unknown option is a usage error | task brief (removing existing passkeys needs an explicit flag) | yes |
| PSK-CFG-01 | CONFIG | settings with nothing chosen | bind `ledger.admin.*` with the tokens only | `effectiveSignIn` is `PASSKEY` | task brief (default for a new installation) | no |
| PSK-CFG-02 | CONFIG | `ledger.admin.oidc.issuer` and `owner-email` without `ledger.admin.sign-in` | bind | the start fails and the message names `ledger.admin.sign-in` (an upgrade is never decided by guessing) | task brief | no |
| PSK-CFG-03 | CONFIG | `sign-in=oidc` without, then with, the owner e-mail and the issuer | bind | refused naming `ledger.admin.oidc.owner-email`; binds with both | task brief | no |

| PSK-CFG-05 | CONFIG | `sign-in=passkey`, no passkey settings | bind | binds; `rp-id` is null and `origins` is empty (the relying party is derived at start from the allowed hosts and the server port, https except localhost, and several public hosts need `rp-id`) | task brief (rpId/origin from the configured host) | no |
| PSK-OIDC-01 | UNIT | `OwnerOidcUserService` with one e-mail and one issuer | the owner's address in any letter case (also under the Turkish locale); another address; the same address at another issuer; an unverified or absent address; no owner configured | only the verified address of the owner at its issuer signs in (`ROLE_OWNER`); every other case is refused with `OAuth2AuthenticationException` | user 2026-11-15; AGS per-issuer rule kept (ADM-058) | no |
| PSK-ENR-01 | WEB | the sign-in with the one-time code (`oneTimeTokenLogin` over `EnrollmentTokenService`) and the admin token | a good code; the same code again; an unknown code; the admin token posted to `/login`; `/ott/generate` | a good code opens the enrolment session (new session id, `/passkeys`), once; a wrong or used code goes back to `/login?error=PASSKEY_CODE_INVALID`; with the default sign-in the token form is absent (no session; with `sign-in=token` see PSK-TOK-01..04); the web makes no code (nothing stored) | common plan item 5; user decision | no |

## 40. ATT: attachments (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit reviewreport)

Written before the code of each step. Design authority: the common attachment design of the three products, edition 3 (sections 1 to 10; the section numbers below are those of that design), and the principles 4.4 (transactions) and 3.9. The ID prefix is `ATA` because `ATT-nnn` is the author attestation area (sections 5 and 9 of this file). Scope of the ledger: the body of a review report (or any other evidence) is uploaded through the five MCP tools `artifact-begin`, `artifact-chunk`, `artifact-finish`, `artifact-status` and `artifact-read` and stored outside the database, encrypted, never edited; a history row points to it with `attachment_id` (a plain column, no foreign key). "Personal edition" is the trust scope only: no case is dropped for it. The ledger tests PostgreSQL only. Cases ATA-001 to ATA-014 are the 14 cases of design section 9, in order. The CLI client that sends a PC file is an open item (design section 10): uploads are made through the MCP tools.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ATA-001 | SERVICE | a migrated schema, a live key, a live grant; a body of 600 KiB with chunk size 256 KiB | `artifact-begin` (operationKey, files=[{fileName, mediaType, size, sha256}], purpose), three `artifact-chunk`, `artifact-finish`, `artifact-status`, `artifact-read` in pages | begin gives `attachmentId`, per-file `{seq, uploadId}` and `maxChunkBytes`; finish gives `attachmentId` with `files` list of declared size and sha256; status of an upload shows `kind=upload`, `seq`, state, size, sha256 and no path or storage key, and the status of the finished id is answered in the bundle attachment form (`kind=attachment`, `COMPLETED`, no expiry, no sequence, `files` list) and not from the kept upload row; the pages of read joined for `seq` (default 0) are the body and their SHA-256 is the declared one | design 9.1, bundle design | PostgreSQL |
| ATA-002 | SERVICE | an upload with chunks 0, 1 and 2 stored | chunk 5 (out of order); chunk 2 again with the same content (the last applied chunk); chunk 2 with other content; chunk 0 again with the same content (earlier than the last applied one); chunk 3 with a wrong `chunkSha256` | `ARTIFACT_CHUNK_CONFLICT`; the state as it is (idempotent: same sequence, same `received`); `ARTIFACT_CHUNK_CONFLICT`; `ARTIFACT_CHUNK_CONFLICT` (only the last applied chunk and the unapplied tail are idempotent); `CHUNK_HASH_MISMATCH`; nothing was appended in the failures | design 3, 9.2, F6 | PostgreSQL |
| ATA-003 | SERVICE | an upload whose declared sha256 (and in a second case the declared size) is not that of the bytes sent | `artifact-finish` | `ARTIFACT_HASH_MISMATCH` for a wrong sha256 or a size that is too large and `ARTIFACT_INCOMPLETE` for too few bytes (common error table, design 3.2); the staging row and the bytes are still there; the row can still be finished after the bytes are completed (a retry is possible) | design 9.3, F8 | PostgreSQL |
| ATA-004 | UNIT | a stored file of three segments (chunks of different sizes) and the end mark; a segment is a 4-byte big-endian length of what follows, 12-byte IV, ciphertext, 16-byte tag | change one byte (also a byte of a length); swap two segments; cut the tail (with and without the end mark segment); remove the end mark segment | `artifact-read` reports `ARTIFACT_CORRUPTED` in every case and returns no page of unverified bytes | design 4.3, 9.4 | no |
| ATA-005 | UNIT | two stored attachments A and B | put the file of B under the name of A | the AES-GCM tag check fails (the storage key is in the AAD): `ARTIFACT_CORRUPTED` | design 4.3, 9.5 | no |
| ATA-006 | UNIT | a stored file whose body is a known sentence | list the folder; read the raw bytes | the file name is 32 hex characters under a two-character folder with no extension, no original name, no id, no project; the raw bytes do not contain a run of 8 bytes of the plain text | design 4.3, 9.6 | no |
| ATA-007 | SERVICE | an upload with a clock the test moves | a chunk at 09:00 (TTL 10 minutes); the reaper at 09:09 and at 09:11 | the expiry is 09:10 after the chunk (counted from the last chunk, not from begin); at 09:09 the row and the file stay; at 09:11 one conditional DELETE removes the row and then the file | design 3.1 `upload-ttl`, 7, 9.7, F3 | PostgreSQL |
| ATA-008 | SERVICE | the folder with a file that has no row older than `orphan-grace`, one younger, an attachment row without its file, a damaged file, an attachment no history row points to | `attachments-check`; then a row with the storage key of the quarantined file is inserted (a restore) and `attachments-check` runs again; then `attachments-check --purge-orphans` | the old file is moved under `orphans/` and never deleted by the check; the young file stays where it is; the missing and the damaged attachment are reported and never deleted; the unreferenced one is listed and kept; the quarantined file is moved back when its row exists again; `--purge-orphans` empties `orphans/` of the files that no row names (a file whose row exists again is not deleted, R9-D0073) and an `owner_audit` row names the count (no storage key); no line shows a storage key or a path; there is no option `--remove-orphan-files` (`UNKNOWN_OPTION`) | design 7, 9.8 (edition 4) | PostgreSQL |
| ATA-009 | SERVICE | two connections of the test PostgreSQL, the same upload id and sequence, the same content (case a) or another (case b) | both call `artifact-chunk` at once (one barrier) | exactly one is applied; the loser answers idempotent with the current state when the content is the same (a), `ARTIFACT_CHUNK_CONFLICT` when it differs (b), and `ARTIFACT_BUSY` when the file locks overlap; afterwards the rest of the chunks and finish give the declared sha256 | design 4.4, 9.9 | PostgreSQL |
| ATA-010 | SERVICE | `max-file-bytes` 1 KiB, `staging-budget-bytes` 2 KiB, `max-open-per-owner` 2 | begin with 2 KiB; begin 1 KiB, 1 KiB, 1 KiB (third: over the budget or the count) | each refused with `ARTIFACT_LIMIT` and no row; nothing is cut; the first two are accepted | design 3, 9.10 | PostgreSQL |

AND state = 'STAGING' AND expires_at <= ?` against a chunk (which moves `expires_at`) and, in a second run, against `artifact-finish`, at one barrier, many rounds | in no round does a row stay whose file is gone, or an attachment exist whose file is gone, or a completed upload disappear: the winner is the one whose conditional statement affected one row. Run on the test PostgreSQL only (principle 7.3); one node is assumed | design 7, 9.11 (edition 4) | PostgreSQL |

## 41. RUN: the migration run in the common form of the three products (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit schemaalign)

Written before the code of each step. Format: the common migration applier of the three products, edition 2 (the history table of section 2, the concurrency of section 3, the meaning of the errors of section 4, the run time of section 5 for the ledger). The ledger does not use Flyway (a Flyway module is needed for each DBMS) and migrates by the explicit command `ledger-cli migrate`, because the server holds no DDL password (README, section "Database"). There is no old form to read: 2.0 is not released, so the history table is changed in place. The long text type is decided by the dialect of `SqlBuilder` (edition 4, user decision 「SqlBuilder 방언이 정함」; the type info of the driver is not used). Cases that need the test PostgreSQL are run when the main session says so.
