## 42. SFR: the read rule of the secret files in the personal edition (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit secretread)

Rule (the user's decision of 2026-11-15, common to the three products; ledger AGS3-D-0268): secret files and folders (keys, attachment keys, passwords, tokens, session keys, TLS keys, the secrets folder, state folders) are **created** owner-only (POSIX 0600 / 0700, or an owner-only ACL), unchanged. When an existing one is **read or used**, the personal edition does not refuse for broad permissions (an ACL entry for Users or Authenticated Users, group or other bits), for an owner that is not the process account, or for a link (it is followed): the personal edition trusts the whole PC, and on Windows a folder on a data volume or under the root of the system drive inherits Authenticated Users by default. A path that is not a regular file (not a directory where a directory belongs) and an empty file are refused in every case. There is no edition switch in this public repository, no setting to turn the checks on, and the code that only the read-time checks used is removed. The helper `org.pgledger.testing.BroadAccess` (testFixtures of `ledger-attestation`) opens a file to other accounts, says whether a file is owner-only, and makes a link; the cases that need a link are skipped with an assumption where the process may not make links (Windows without the privilege), the cases that need POSIX bits or an ACL choose by the attribute view of the file system and never by the operating system name. The owner of an existing file that differs from the process account cannot be set without a privilege, so no test makes one; the rule is shown by the absence of the check.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SFR-001 | UNIT | an attachment storage on a temporary folder (`StoreFixture`, no database); the key folder, the key file, the storage folder, a sub folder and a stored file are opened to other accounts with `BroadAccess.open` (an ALLOW entry of Authenticated Users with read and write rights, or group and other bits) and the test asserts that they are reachable by others | `AttachmentKeys.byId`, `requirePresent`, `AttachmentStore.requireDiskSpace`, an append and finish of a new file, `PrivateFiles.openRead` of the old file | no refusal: the key is returned, the start check passes, the new file is written, the old one reads; ARTIFACT_STORAGE_UNSAFE is not raised for broad permissions (on the base the ACL or POSIX check refused it). The case of another owner has no test: it cannot be set without a privilege | rule of this section | no |
| SFR-002 | UNIT | the same storage; a key file that is a link to a valid key file, then the key folder and the storage folder moved away and replaced by links (skipped where links cannot be made) | `byId`, `requirePresent`, an append, `requireDiskSpace` | all work: a link is followed (on the base the open with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS` and the attribute check refused a link) | rule of this section | no |
| SFR-003 | UNIT | the same storage; in the key folder a directory, an empty file and a file of 7 bytes; a file where the key folder belongs; a file where the storage folder belongs | `byId` of each, `requirePresent`, `requireDiskSpace` | **edition 11 of the design**: the directory at the place of the key file, the file at the place of the key folder and the file at the place of the storage folder are ARTIFACT_STORAGE_UNSAFE (the storage path cannot be used for this purpose); the empty file, the file of 7 bytes and a name with no file are ARTIFACT_KEY_MISSING (no usable key: missing, empty or not 256 bits; detail = the key id); both `retryable=false`; the file that stands where the storage folder belongs is not replaced. (Edition 10 had the empty and the short key file under UNSAFE.) A key folder that is not there at the start (the server makes neither the folder nor a key: `prepare-host` and `setup` do, design 4.3) is ARTIFACT_KEY_MISSING naming the active key id, and the start check makes nothing; `PrivateFiles.requireDirectory` and `requireFile` of a missing path are ARTIFACT_STORAGE_UNSAFE ("nothing is there"), no longer ARTIFACT_STORAGE_IO_ERROR with a raw `NoSuchFileException` (found by SYS-006 on the integration head 8caa5b6). | rule of this section; common attachment design, edition 11, 3.2 | no |
| SFR-004 | UNIT | a parent folder that is open to every account (`BroadAccess.open`) | `AttachmentKeys.create`, `AttachmentStore.prepare`, an append | the key folder, the new key file, the storage folder, its sub folder and the stored file are owner-only (`BroadAccess.ownerOnly`: no group or other bit, or an ACL whose every entry is the owner's): the creation is unchanged, the repair `PrivateFiles.settle` right after creation still replaces what the parent hands down | rule of this section | no |
| SFR-005 | UNIT | the message bundles | `ledger-errors.properties`, `ledger-errors_ko.properties` | **edition 11**: `error.ARTIFACT_STORAGE_UNSAFE` says in both languages that the storage path cannot be used for this purpose (not a regular file or folder where one belongs, or it cannot be made owner-only: a file system without POSIX permissions or ACLs such as FAT, or an account of the process that is not known), not that the storage is "not safe"; `error.ARTIFACT_KEY_MISSING` names the key id and says that the key file is missing, empty or not 256 bits (`StorageUnsafeMessageTest.sfr005_*`); `ARTIFACT_STORAGE_IO_ERROR` stays the disk failure. README.md and README.ko.md carry both rows; ATA-041 keeps the codes, their names and `retryable=false`. An unknown process account is UNSAFE, not IO_ERROR (`PrivateFiles.processUser`: no account, or an account the file system does not know); no test makes such an account, so the case is stated, not run | common attachment design, edition 11, 3.2 | no |
| SFR-006 | UNIT | a temporary folder; `<tmp>/a/b/attachments` and `<tmp>/c/d/keys` where `a`, `b`, `c` and `d` do not exist (found on the integration head: SYS-006 and W7-SYS-01 failed with `ARTIFACT_STORAGE_IO_ERROR`, `NoSuchFileException` for the `data` folder of a fresh home, `Files.createDirectory` needs its parent) | `PrivateFiles.ensureDirectory(<tmp>/a/b/attachments)` twice; `AttachmentKeys.create()` with the key folder `<tmp>/c/d/keys`; the attachment disk check with a parent that does not exist (ATA-043); with a regular file where a parent belongs | the missing ancestors are made with the default attributes of the file system (they hold no secret) and the last folder owner-only (`BroadAccess.ownerOnly`), the second call changes nothing; the key folder and the key file are owner-only; the disk check of ATA-043 makes the parent and the folder; a regular file where a parent belongs is `ARTIFACT_STORAGE_IO_ERROR` and the file is not replaced | main session, defect on the integration head | no |
| SFR-010 | UNIT | a secrets directory and a secret file made by the store, then opened to other accounts with `BroadAccess.open` (asserted reachable) | `SecretStore.check`, `exists`, `load`, `store` | no refusal (on the base `LOOSE_PERMISSIONS`): the secret is found and read, the replacement is a new owner-only file, the existing directory is not narrowed | rule of section 42 | no |
| SFR-011 | UNIT | a secrets directory that is a link; a secret file that is a link to a valid file (skipped where links cannot be made) | `exists`, `load`, `check` | the link is followed and the value read (on the base `UNEXPECTED_FILE_TYPE`) | rule of section 42 | no |
| SFR-012 | UNIT | a directory at the place of the secret file; a file at the place of the secrets directory; an empty secret file | `check`, `exists`, `load`, `store`; `load` of the empty file | the first two are `UNEXPECTED_FILE_TYPE` naming the path; the empty file is the new `EMPTY_SECRET_FILE` (en and ko message, naming the secret, never a content); the file at the place of the directory is not replaced | rule of section 42, rule 3 | no |

## 43. M1X: the 1.x migration as a separate tool `ledger-migrate-1x` (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit migratetool)

Written before the code of the unit. Source: the user, 「근데 마이그레이션 툴은 제공해야한다는건 기억해?」, 「마이그레이션 툴 제공하던지 가이드 제공하던지 해.」, 「별도 마이그레이션 툴 제공해야지. 애초에 sqlite를 그대로 쓰려고?」, 「애초에 하위호환성 다 버리라고 했을텐데?」 and, for the test server, 「…설치하면서 기존에 있던 장부 mcp 서비스는 중단하고 마이그레이션 하는 방식으로 설치해줘.」.

Decisions. (1) Version 2.0, the body (the modules `ledger-domain`, `ledger-attestation`, `ledger-database`, `ledger-application`, `ledger-mcp-server`, `ledger-admin-web`, `ledger-cli`, `ledger-client-hook`, `ledger-seed` and the installers), knows nothing of 1.x: no 1.x type, no 1.x SQL, no 1.x setting, no 1.x environment file, no 1.x branch. Everything that reads the 1.x tables, settings or environment file, or stops and keeps a 1.x service, is the module `ledger-migrate-1x` (package `org.pgledger.migrate1x`), a Spring Boot application with its own main on the same JVM, built as `ledger-migrate-1x.jar` and shipped next to the other jars; it depends on `ledger-domain`, `ledger-database`, `ledger-application` and `ledger-cli` (for its command line machinery and secret store, so the secret store format cannot drift) and writes through their repositories, and no 2.0 module depends on it. The tool does not copy the command line machinery or the secret store of `ledger-cli`: it uses the plain jar of `ledger-cli` (no transitive dependencies) and its runner, exceptions, secret store, output and message classes; for that the runner and exception classes of `ledger-cli` were generalised (a `CommandName` interface of which `CliCommand` is one implementation, an `ErrorKey` interface of which `ErrorCode` is one implementation), and the codes of the tool that are not codes of `ledger-cli` are the enum `MigrationError`. (2) The commands: `stop-1x` (new: stops and disables the 1.x services `<name>` and `<name>-admin`, keeps each unit definition as `<unit>.1x-backup`, and checks the public host names of the 1.x environment file), `import-secrets-1x` (the former `secret-import-1x`), `import-1x` (the former `import-legacy`) and `retire-1x` (the former `retire-legacy`); the behaviour, the options and the error codes of the three moved commands are those of the rows they keep (the codes are the codes of `ledger-migrate-1x` now). The order of an upgrade: `stop-1x`, the installer of 2.0 (which does not know 1.x), `import-secrets-1x`, `import-1x`, the checks, `retire-1x`. (3) `stop-1x` starts the service manager: Java has no API to stop a service, so it runs `systemctl` with fixed arguments through `ProcessBuilder`, in one class (`SystemServiceControl`), and says so in the code and in the guide; it never touches a service whose name is not `<name>` or `<name>-admin` and whose definition is not that of 1.x (the unit has `EnvironmentFile=` naming `/etc/<name>/env`); 1.x had Linux (systemd) installations only, so on Windows the command refuses (`NO_1X_SERVICE_WINDOWS`, M1X-015). (4) 1.x request receipts are not imported: a 1.x request id is never replayed by 2.0 (every 2.0 write has an `operationKey`), so the receipts would only be rows that refuse; `ConflictCode.REQUEST_ID_LEGACY`, `ReceiptOrigin` and the column `request_receipt.origin` are gone from the body, and the tables of the comparison are the other twelve. (5) What the body keeps for rows that come from another ledger is named neutrally: `ImportedActorId`, the assurances `IMPORTED_LOGIN`, `IMPORTED_SHARED`, `IMPORTED_UNKNOWN`, the history operations `IMPORT`, `IMPORTED_NOTE`, `IMPORTED_CHANGE` and the history column `source_ref`; the tool maps the 1.x values to them (M1X-050). (6) The backup of a file that the installer did not render is now `<file>.backup` (the rows INS-042, REH2-INS-01 .. 04 and REH3-INS-01, REH3-INS-02 keep their IDs and say `.backup`); `stop-1x` keeps the unit of 1.x as `<unit>.1x-backup`.

Rows that moved with their code keep their IDs and run in `ledger-migrate-1x`: section 17 (MIG-001 .. MIG-035, CLI-DIR-02) and section 37 (RET-001 .. RET-018) with the command names `import-1x` and `retire-1x` where they say `import-legacy` and `retire-legacy`; CLI-044 (`import-1x`), SEC-025 (`--owner-account` on `import-1x`), SEC-026 and SEC-027 and W7-SEC-01 (`OneXEnvironment`, `import-secrets-1x`), CLI-PRN-14, REH2-MIG-01 .. 06 and REH3-IMP-01 (`import-1x`), REH4-HOST-01 .. 07 (the host names check of the 1.x environment file: the command is `stop-1x --env=<file>` with `--mcp-host` and `--admin-host`, the codes `HOSTS_DROPPED` and `ENV_FILE_FAILED` are those of the tool; the cases of the installer options `render-deploy --check --from-1x-env` are the same cases through `stop-1x`), and the E2E-008 rehearsal. MIG-004 is replaced by M1X-003 (receipts are not imported); the vocabulary of MIG-003, MIG-006 and MIG-012 reads as M1X-050 says. The rows of the installers that took `--from-1x-env` and `secret-import-1x` (INS-043, REH4-INS-01) are replaced by M1X-030. REQUEST_ID_LEGACY, in SVC-RCP-15, section 1.5 and DOC-027, is gone (M1X-004).

## 44. OPK: the required operationKey of every write tool and no compatibility in the tool contract (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit opkey)

Written before the code of the unit. Sources: the user, 「operationKey 필수」 (decision D14) and 「애초에 하위호환성 다 버리라고 했을텐데?」 (no backward compatibility and no shim in 2.0: no old name is accepted next to the new one), and the attachment design, edition 12 (the user's decision 「판 9 유지(uploadId가 키)」): `artifact-chunk` and `artifact-finish` take no `operationKey`, the one exception; an upload starts with the `operationKey` of `artifact-begin`, a chunk is idempotent by `uploadId` + `sequence` (+ `chunkSha256`) and the finish by `uploadId`.

Decisions. (1) Names: the value type is `OperationKey` (a text of 1 to 128 characters, no UUID format), the wire argument is `operationKey`, the column is `operation_key` (`history.operation_key`, `request_receipt.operation_key`), the claim of the attestation carries `operationKey`, and the output field of the history read is `operation_key`. The error code `REQUEST_CONFLICT` is renamed `OPERATION_CONFLICT` (2.0 is unreleased, so no old name stays; it has the form of `ARTIFACT_OPERATION_CONFLICT`: code enum, messages en and ko, tool descriptions, README, release notes, the CLI refusal switch, the tests), and the table name `request_receipt` is kept (the 1.x table has the same name and the migrate tool needs both). (2) Every one of the nine write tools requires `operationKey`; the server and the hook never make a key up (a missing key is refused, `VALIDATION_FAILED`, path `operationKey`). (3) A batch has one key; its operations carry none and use `derive(index)` of the batch key. (4) `ledger_whoami` and the artifact tools are signed with the empty key; `artifact-begin` has its own required `operationKey` argument, `artifact-chunk` and `artifact-finish` have none. (5) Removed without a replacement: the arguments `author_id`, `request_id` and `batch_request_id` of the write tools and of `ledger_whoami` (refused as unknown, like any unknown argument), the JSON string for an object or array parameter (`fields`, `ops`; refused as `WRONG_TYPE`) and the text `null` as "the argument is missing". The read tool `ledger_history` keeps its real filter `author_id`. The 1.x side (the migrate tool) keeps the 1.x names where it reads 1.x data.

Round 2 (the coordinator's independent review of the unit; findings M1, M3, M4, m3, m6 and the vectors recommendation; the rows OPK-019 .. OPK-024 and the rows marked "changed by opkey round 2"). (M1) The batch has a receipt under its own key: `ReceiptOperation.BATCH` under (author, batch key) with the digest of all operations (each kind and every value as sent, in order); the operations keep their receipts under `derive(i)`. The same key with other operations (added, removed, changed, moved) or under another tool is `OPERATION_CONFLICT` and nothing is written. (M3) Receipts are unique per (author, operation key), like `upload_staging` UNIQUE(owner, operation_key): `request_receipt` has the primary key `pk_request_receipt` on `(author_id, operation_key)` (V2 edited in place, 2.0 is unreleased); another author with the same key writes its own receipt and its own write; the same author with other content or channel is `OPERATION_CONFLICT`; the author comparison of the conflict is gone. (M4) A JSON null in `fields` counts as not given; an empty text clears an optional field (the one way); the digest of the arguments (`ArgumentsDigest`, hook and server) leaves out every object member whose value is null, at every depth, and keeps null array elements. Reason: the server side of the MCP SDK drops a null member of a map before the tool binding, so a null could not be told from a left-out key there. (m3) The order of the checks of a batch is: attestation, number of operations, operation key, content; so an empty key with an invalid attestation is refused by the attestation first. (m6) `BatchService.batch(String, String, String)` and `LedgerJson.readInputList` are removed; the one entry is `batch(String, String, List<BatchOpInput>)`. (Vectors) `contract/write-tool-vectors.json` (hand-kept file) has the machine readable lists `operationKeyClaim` (the nine ledger write tools whose claim carries the key) and `operationKeyArgument` (those nine plus `artifact-begin`: the tools whose input schema requires `operationKey`); OPK-016 and OPK-022 test the two sides against them.

## 45. VRF: the rules of verification, the explicit ids and the links across projects (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit verifyrules; defects D-0022, D-0023, D-0024 and D-0029)

Written before the code of the unit. Sources: the findings D-0022 (a large explicit id stops the automatic ids for good), D-0023 (`record_fix` and `save` pass on a `VERIFIED` defect), D-0024 (a link or a merge into another project checks no grant there) and D-0029 (the verifier rule), and the decision of the user for the role of the ledger: 「VERIFIED: 작성자·보고자·수정자와 다른 세션만」 and 「검증자는 작성자·보고자·수정자와 달라야 한다」 (the verifier is none of the session that registered, reported or fixed).

Decisions. (1) **D-0023.** The rule lives in the domain: `Defect.requireNotVerified()` throws `TransitionException` `VERIFIED_DEFECT` (argument: the defect id) for a defect whose status is `VERIFIED`; `RecordFixOperation` and the defect branch of `SaveOperation` both call it right after the defect row is read for writing (after the version check, before the check of an unchanged save and before anything is written), so the two paths cannot differ. A defect in any other status behaves as before (a fix on a `MERGED` defect stays allowed, SVC-FIX-16). The message says that a reviewer has to reopen the defect first: the way exists, `ledger_review` moves a `VERIFIED` defect to another status with an evidence link (README, tool table). What a `VERIFIED` defect still accepts: the reviewer's `ledger_review` (to `OPEN`, `SOURCE_FIXED`, `NOT_DEFECT`, `DISPUTED`, `MERGED`), `ledger_record_case`, `ledger_link` and its removal, `ledger_correct` and `ledger_review_correct` (they mark a history row, they change no field). `ledger_correct` is not changed by this unit. (2) **D-0029.** A defect is verified only when it has a recorded fix and the verifier is none of: the session that registered the defect (`created_by`), a session that recorded a case of it (`defect_case.recorded_by`), a session that recorded a fix of it (`defect_fix.recorded_by`). `FixHistory` carries the fixers and the reporters (the registering session and the sessions of the cases); the rule is `FixHistory.involves(actor)`; a `created_by` that is NULL adds nobody. The order of the checks stays: same state, fix for SOURCE_FIXED, evidence when leaving VERIFIED, then evidence, fix, session. For a task the verifier is none of: the session that registered it (`created_by`), the session that owns it (`owner_actor_id`), every session that reported it done (every REPORTED_DONE transition that did not come from VERIFIED, not only the latest; sending a verified task back is no report). `TaskReports.latestReporter` and `latestReport` stay (they tell whom a verification request goes to). The reporters of a defect are those of the product's own reading of it (`ledger_defect` shows the cases of the defects merged into it as its cases): the registrant and the case sessions of the defect and of every defect merged into it, directly or through others (`MergeChainRepository.mergedInto`); the fixes are not read the same way (the detail shows only the fixes of the defect itself), so only the fixes of the defect count. A release condition follows the same principle (review of verifyrules): `MET` and `NOT_MET` are set only by a session other than the one that registered the condition (`Condition.createdBy`), `SAME_SESSION`; it has no owner and no report, and leaving them for `UNKNOWN` needs no check of the session (as before, it needs no evidence either). (3) **D-0022.** The column `project.next_defect_no` (and `next_task_no`) holds the last number that was issued (the first number is 1 and the column starts at 0). An explicit number is taken by `IdAllocator.takeDefectNo` and `takeTaskNo`: one statement `UPDATE project SET <counter> = :n WHERE code = :code AND <counter> = :n - 1` (an UPDATE that writes only when the number is exactly the next one, never a lock only); when it updates no row the counter is read, and a number at or below it is a past number (a free gap) that is accepted and leaves the counter alone, a number above it is refused with `ValidationCode` `ID_NUMBER_AHEAD` (arguments: the id as given, the next id; issue at the path `id`, reason `NUMBER_ABOVE_NEXT`) and nothing is written. The counter only grows, so the read after an empty update is safe against another transaction. The raise methods are gone. Review of verifyrules: the update does not wait when the row it sees does not satisfy the condition, so a transaction that has taken the next number and is not committed makes a later explicit number "ahead" until it commits; the answer names the next id from the committed counter, which is true when it is read. When the counter read after an empty update equals the number minus one, the counter changed between the statement and the read, and the answer is not "ahead" (its message would name the id itself as the next one): it is the conflict code `ID_COUNTER_CHANGED` (argument: the project code; the message in English and Korean says that another request took the number meanwhile and that the request is to be sent again; no value of the message is an untranslated text), a new code of the category CONFLICT (88 codes); the server retries nothing. **D-0049.** The explicit id of a save is held to the canonical form: a defect id, and a task id of the issued form that has a number, must have a number of at least 1 and be the string that `DefectId.of(code, number)` (`TaskId.issue`) makes; the issue is `VALIDATION_FAILED` at `id` with the reason `ID_NOT_CANONICAL` (argument: the canonical id) or `MUST_BE_POSITIVE` (number 0). The pattern of `DefectId` is not changed, only the save is stricter (reads, the lookup and the import of 1.x use the pattern, and `P-D-0002` and `P-D-00002` are one number but two strings, so both would have been stored); a free text task id has no number and is unchanged. (m2, D-0023) `record_fix` always saves the defect row, with its version condition and a real change (`updated_at` and the version), also when the owner and the status stay, so a concurrent transition of the defect is decided by that statement and no fix row lands on a `VERIFIED` defect; it writes no history row of the defect for that (the fix row is the history of the call). **An exception to the rule of `before_data`** (an operation that changes an existing row stores the row as it was, SVC-BEF): a `record_fix` that changes only `updated_at` and the version of the defect (the owner and the status stay) stores no `before_data` of the defect, because it writes no history row of the defect; the change is of the bookkeeping columns and not of the content, and a history row for it would pile up an empty edit row for every fix. `record_fix` runs no check of the codes of the defect: it changes no code field. The same principle for every write (3rd review): the check of the common codes of a defect (`CodeRules.checkDefect`) runs only in a write that stores the code columns, that is the create and the edit of `ledger_save`; a state change (`ledger_transition`, `ledger_review`, with its merge and its verification) changes no code column, so it runs none, and a defect that holds the retired severity `UNRATED` can be moved, verified and merged; the rule that such a defect must be rated is kept where the rating is written, in the edit of `ledger_save` (`CODE_INVALID`). The number of an issued form is read by the key (any case) in the save: `<code>-t-0005` is the issued form of number 5, held to the canonical form like `<CODE>-T-0005`. (4) **D-0024.** The write gate asks, for the other end of a link or of a merge, the same scope that the first end needs (as the relation of SVC-GATE-09 and the move of SVC-GATE-08 do): `defect_task` the `defect` scope on the project of the defect and of the task, `condition_task` and `condition_defect` the `task` scope on the project of the condition and of the task or of the defect, a merge the `defect` scope on the project of the merge target (a move out of `VERIFIED` into `MERGED` asks the `verify` scope for the defect itself, as before, and the `defect` scope for the target). The targets of the gate are a set, so two ends in one project are one check. The unlink is the same operation (`remove=true`) and passes the same lookup. A merge target that does not exist adds no target here and fails as before with `NOT_FOUND` in the transaction. (5) **Where the cases run.** The gate cases are in `GateTest` as SVC-GATE-43 (VRF-030, VRF-031), SVC-GATE-44 (VRF-032), SVC-GATE-45 (VRF-033), SVC-GATE-46 (VRF-034) and SVC-GATE-47 (VRF-035); VRF-002 .. VRF-006, VRF-009, VRF-012, VRF-015, VRF-016, VRF-018, VRF-019 and VRF-028 are in `VerifyRulesTest`; VRF-022, VRF-023 .. VRF-027, VRF-037, VRF-038, VRF-046 and VRF-047 are in `IdsTest` (VRF-023 is SVC-IDS-03); VRF-020, VRF-021 and VRF-036 are in `CounterTest`; VRF-001, VRF-010, VRF-011, VRF-014 and VRF-017 are in `TransitionPolicyTest`; VRF-008 is in `BatchAndErrorsTest`; VRF-007 is in `WriteCallTest`; VRF-013 is the test of MCR-D04 in `ReadDetailTest`. Also changed: the 18 digit task id of SVC-PRN-03 (`PrinciplesTest`).

## 46. PCS: the scope of the personal edition: no proxy, no certificate, one host name, loopback only, no seal-host (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit pcscope)

Written before the code of the unit. Decisions this section applies (user, verbatim, 2026-11-16, passed on by the AGS lead):

- Installation scope 「PC·서버 둘 다, 공개는 운영자」: the two services (the MCP server and the admin web) bind to loopback only. A reverse proxy and TLS are the operator's part. The installer writes no nginx site, orders no certificate (ACME) and installs no renewal timer. The list of host names (up to 10 per service) becomes one name. The README keeps "run on your own computer or server".
- seal-host 「없앰」.
- No backward compatibility: 「애초에 하위호환성 다 버리라고 했을텐데?」. A refusal of a setting or a token version that the tags v1.0.0 and v1.1.0 never had is not kept.
- The personal edition is one person who trusts the whole installation target. The basic features and tests are not reduced. Vendor independent: no product of a reverse proxy is required or recommended.

Design. (1) The product has one network stance: both services listen on `127.0.0.1` and nothing can change that (no property, no command line option, no environment variable of the service); what makes them reachable from outside is a reverse proxy and a certificate that the operator chooses and runs. `--mcp-host` and `--admin-host` are the one public host name of each service (optional): the name that the operator's proxy passes in the `Host` header, which the Host allow list of the service must accept; `127.0.0.1` and `localhost` are always allowed. A second port for the actuator (`management.server.port` different from `server.port`) would be a listener that the fix does not cover, so a service refuses to start with it (PCS-014); `/healthz` stays on the one port. `stop-1x` of `ledger-migrate-1x` takes the same one name per service with the same form rule as the installer, and does not refuse the names of the 1.x file that it is not given: it lists them as names that 2.0 no longer accepts (PCS-024). (2) `ledger-cli` has no command `seal-host`, `tls-issue` or `tls-renew`, no acme4j, no nginx site, no renewal unit and timer, and the installers do not know them. (3) Windows: the service account stays LocalService (no password). A secret folder or file is **created** with the ACL: the installing user (the owner) with everything, the service account LocalService with read only, and nothing for anybody else. No inherited entry is put in when the path is made (the entries that the parent hands down are replaced right after creation; the DACL is not marked protected). Why LocalService reads: the services run as LocalService and must read the secrets, and without `seal-host` there is no hand-over (no `SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege`, no `SeRestorePrivilege`, no owner changed to the service account); the installing user stays the owner, a re-run by the same user finds everything as it left it. The personal edition trusts the whole installation target, so access of another administrator is inside the trust and is no case of this section. The rule "creation is owner-only, a read refuses only a path that is not a regular or is an empty file" (SFR) is unchanged on Linux and for the key store of the hook; on Windows, for the files of the services' secrets (`SecretStore`, the key and attachment folders of `PrivateFiles`), "owner-only" is defined as the owner plus the service account with read. (4) `pgla1` (the token version before the first release) and `ledger.admin.oidc.owners` (the list before the first release) are no more refused by name: the token is "malformed" like any text that is not of the format, the setting is an unknown setting like any other (ignored by the binder). The check of the two tags is a case of this section (PCS-052).

## 47. PSEC: no lock by client address, and the secrets of each service apart (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, unit pcsec; defects D-0013 and D-0031)

Written before the code of the unit. Scope (user, verbatim, passed on by the AGS lead): the personal edition is one person who trusts the whole installation target; both services listen on loopback only and the public name is the operator's reverse proxy on the same host; the same problem is solved the same way, a difference has a reason; no backward compatibility (2.0 is not released; 1.x has the separate tool `ledger-migrate-1x`); the basic features and tests are not reduced (a test whose rule changes asserts the new rule).

### 47.1 D-0013: a lock per client locks the owner

Fact. `LoginAttempts` locked a client (an IPv4 address or an IPv6 /64) for `lock-period` after `max-failures` wrong checks and refused a right credential of a locked client. Since pcscope the admin web listens on `127.0.0.1` and is reached through the operator's proxy on the same host, so the public reaches it through the operator's proxy. The address that proxy forwards (`X-Forwarded-For`, believed from the loopback address only, `application.yml`) is a record of the request and not an identity: depending on the proxy, every user arrives as one address or as whatever the proxy passes on, and a lock keyed by it locks the owner together with whoever sends a few wrong credentials (the token, the one-time code and the passkey sign-in). The personal edition is one person and has nobody to tell apart, so no key is needed; the forwarded address stays for the records. A token made by `token-generate` (256 bits from `SecureRandom`) and an enrolment code (100 bits) cannot be guessed, so the lock protects nothing that the delay does not; a token that the owner chose or imported from 1.x may be weaker, so the admin token of 1.x is not imported and the one the installer made is used (LGN-014, LGN-015). The delay is not serialised either: a queue of checks beyond the budget would let a caller that keeps it full keep the owner's check waiting, which is the lock again (stated in `LoginAttempts`).

Design. The per-client lock is removed with its settings `ledger.admin.lockout.max-failures` and `ledger.admin.lockout.lock-period`. What stays is the global budget, a delay and never a refusal: `global-max-failures` checks of all clients within `window`, every check beyond it waits `global-delay` and is still made, so a right credential always passes (after the delay). `LoginAttempts.admit` returns the delay (no `Admission`, no `allowed`); there is no client key, so no `X-Forwarded-For` setting is added: a client is not a notion of the personal edition (one proxy on the host, loopback only), which `LoginAttempts` states. The error code `TOO_MANY_AUTH_FAILURES` stays for the one case that is left, a delay that is interrupted while the server stops (the attempt is refused without a comparison); no lock produces it any more.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LGN-001 | ADMIN | the default settings (`@AdminTest`); one client address | 20 wrong bearer tokens, then the right token; then 20 wrong tokens and the right token again; with `sign-in=token`, five wrong tokens at the login form, then the right one | every wrong token is 401 `UNAUTHORIZED` (never 429 `TOO_MANY_AUTH_FAILURES`); the right token is 200 each time, however many wrong ones came before from the same address; the right token at the form signs in; a success needs no reset. Covered by: `NoClientLockTest` (replaces `LockoutTest`; changes ADM-028), `TokenSignInTest.tok03_noLock` (changes PSK-TOK-03) | D-0013 | no |
| LGN-013 | ADMIN | a context with the budget 2 and the delay 300 ms and `RecordingLoginDelay` (the delay of each check is recorded, nothing sleeps); every test starts a new window by moving the test ticker 61 s | (a) two wrong one-time codes at `/login/ott`, then the right token as a bearer; (b) two wrong passkey assertions at `/login/webauthn`, then the same; (c) three right passkey sign-ins in a row, then a bearer; (d) three right one-time codes (made by `PasskeyService.issueCode`) in a row, then a bearer; (e) ADM-049 through a real server | (a) and (b) the delays are 0, 0, 300 ms and the bearer is 200: the one-time code path (`EnrollmentTokenService`) and the passkey path (`PasskeyLoginGuard`) count on the one budget; (c) and (d) every delay is 0: a success gives its count back through the real paths. A change that removes `admit` from either path turns (a) or (b) red, and one that removes `succeeded` turns (c) or (d) red. Covered by: `BudgetPathsTest`; (e) `ProxyAndHostTest` | D-0013 | no |

### 47.2 D-0031: the MCP server reads the secrets of the admin web

Fact. Both services and the command line tool imported one configuration tree, `optional:configtree:<etc>/secrets/`. The two services run as the same operating system user, so the Spring `Environment` of the MCP server held the admin token, the OIDC client secret and the other secrets of the admin web. Spring Boot maps "the names of the folders and files under the config tree" to the property name (https://docs.spring.io/spring-boot/reference/features/external-config.html, section "Configuration Trees"), so a file of the folder `secrets/attachment-keys` also became the property `attachment-keys.<file>` of both services (the files of the attachment keys were in the Environment; fact from that rule, shown by SPL-006).

Where each secret is used (read from the code: `AgentTokenProvider`, `AdminTokenProvider`, `LedgerAdminProperties`, `LedgerJdbcProperties`, `McpCheckProperties`, `ImportSecrets1xCommand`, `LegacySourceProperties`, `OwnerAccountPropertySource`, `deploy/install.*`):

| Secret (the file name is the property) | Read by | Folder below `secrets/` |
|---|---|---|
| `ledger.jdbc.password` | the MCP server, the admin web, `ledger-cli` (`migrate`, `setup`, the owner commands), `ledger-migrate-1x` | `common` |
| `ledger.admin.token`, `ledger.admin.agent-token` | the admin web | `admin` |
| `spring.security.oauth2.client.registration.<id>.client-secret` (OIDC) | the admin web | `admin` |
| `ledger.mcp.token` | the MCP server, `ledger-cli mcp-check` | `mcp` |
| `ledger.import.source.password` | `ledger-migrate-1x` (`import-1x`, `retire-1x`) | `tool` |

Design. (1) One class, `SecretFolder`, maps a secret name to its folder (the table above; a name that is in none of them is refused with `UNKNOWN_SECRET_NAME` before anything is asked or written: a secret that no process reads is a mistake). `SecretVault` (the store the commands use) puts a secret in its folder with the existing `SecretStore` of that folder; `secret-set`, `token-generate` (also `--copy-to`, which may name another folder) and `import-secrets-1x` need no folder option. (2) `DeployRenderer.configImport` gives each service the folder `common` and its own (`mcp` or `admin`) as separate configuration trees, never `secrets/` itself (a tree imported at `secrets/` would name every property `common.ledger.jdbc.password`); `ledger-cli` imports `common` and `mcp` (its commands need the database password and, for `mcp-check`, the token of the agents; no command needs an admin secret); `ledger-migrate-1x` imports `common` and `tool`. (3) The key files of the attachments are in `<etc>/attachment-keys`, a sibling of `secrets`, below no configuration tree of any process; the services and `ledger-cli` read it by the path `ledger.attachments.key-directory`. (4) The folders are made as the secret folder always was: Linux, the service user, 0700 (`prepare-host` makes `secrets`, the four folders and `attachment-keys`); Windows, the installing user with everything and LocalService with read, no hand-over; an existing folder is never changed. (5) The README (both languages) says what the change is and what it is not: the process of a service no longer has the secrets of the other in its `Environment`; the two services run as one operating system user, so the file permissions do not keep one service from reading the other's folder (the personal edition trusts the whole installation target).

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SPL-001 | UNIT | the names of the table above, a second registration `spring.security.oauth2.client.registration.second.client-secret`, and `cloudflare-api-token`, `ledger.mcp`, `ledger.administrator`, `ledger.admin.oidc-secret`, `ledger.admin.tokens`, `ledger.admin.agent-token.x`, `ledger.jdbc.passwords`, `ledger.mcp.token.x` | `SecretFolder.of` | the names of the table map as in the table (exactly `ledger.admin.token`, `ledger.admin.agent-token` and the OIDC registration name to `admin`: no other `ledger.admin.` name); the others are `UNKNOWN_SECRET_NAME` (a `FailureException`, in `ErrorCode` and in both message bundles; the name is not echoed). `SecretFolder` is the only place that holds the correspondence. Covered by: `SecretFolderTest` (and `MessagesTest` of both modules: the code has its message in both languages) | D-0031 | no |
| SPL-002 | CLI | a temporary secret directory; POSIX file system (`RequiresPosixPermissions`) and the recorded ACL path | `secret-set ledger.jdbc.password`, `token-generate ledger.mcp.token --copy-to=ledger.admin.agent-token`, `token-generate ledger.admin.token`, `secret-set cloudflare-api-token` | the files are `secrets/common/ledger.jdbc.password`, `secrets/mcp/ledger.mcp.token`, `secrets/admin/ledger.admin.agent-token` (the same value as the first), `secrets/admin/ledger.admin.token`; the folders are 0700 and the files 0600 (the ACL of PCS-030 on Windows); nothing is written at `secrets/` itself; the unknown name is `UNKNOWN_SECRET_NAME` (exit 1) and nothing is asked and no folder made; `--if-absent` and "an existing secret is kept" work per folder. Covered by: `SecretSetCommandTest`, `SecretSetStdinTest`, `TokenGenerateCommandTest` (changed: the names are routed names), `SecretVaultTest` | D-0031 | no |

| SPL-008 | SYSTEM | the packaged product (`PackagedProductSystemTest`, the jars of the build) installed into a temporary home (on Linux the files are rendered below a root and name the paths of a real installation, so the expected paths come from `InstalledInstance.renderedEtc()`, the mapping of `serverArguments()`) | render the deployment; `token-generate ledger.mcp.token`; start the MCP server jar with the rendered arguments | the rendered `--spring.config.import` of the MCP server has `<etc>/secrets/common/` and `<etc>/secrets/mcp/` as trees and neither the secret directory itself, nor `secrets/admin`, nor `secrets/tool`; the token is the file `secrets/mcp/ledger.mcp.token` and the server answers with it (SYS-002 onwards); `application.yaml` has the key directory `<etc>/attachment-keys` and not `secrets/attachment-keys`. **Not tested here:** the admin web jar is not started by this system test (no step starts it), so what the admin web imports is covered by `ConfigImportTest` (SPL-003, SPL-006: a real Spring `Environment` over the rendered trees) and not by a process; a start of the admin web on the Windows and Linux test servers (INS-031) is the check that remains | D-0031 | no |

A revoked grant still refuses the replay with `SESSION_NOT_GRANTED` for the scope of the answered call (`task`), as SVC-GATE-12 decides (a revoked session cannot read stored responses): the replay is not authorized by state, and the grant is still needed. A call that looks like a verification through the author channel is still refused first for a caller that cannot prove its author and for an author without an answer to the key (SVC-GATE-04, `AttestationGateTest`). Left as it was: the first chunk of an upload and the finish of an upload with no chunk still make their file, so when the row of such an upload is removed in the same instant a file without a row can still be left, which the check moves to `orphans/` after `orphan-grace`. The test checks the cause by a loaded class, not by a time. Covered by: `ColdStartCostTest` | D-0096 | no |
